- **The project's kind** is set and cleared on the project's page, the one place it is asked: a pull-down of §8.1's four words and *None*, which removes the key; a word this app does not know is an entry of its own, as written, with the sentence that says so, until another is chosen, as every pull-down of a token says it (above); **a legacy spelling shows as the kind it means**, on *Edit* as on *View*, and stays in the file until the kind is really changed — choosing the entry on show again writes nothing, and what is then written is the kind's own name, never the legacy token (`franchise` typed by an agent is written `series`: legacy tokens are read, never written); a `kind` that is no word — a number, an object — is drawn as it is, locked (`ShapeLocked`), and nothing writes over it; an empty `kind` reads as *None*, with no entry of its own, and choosing *None* removes the key. An agent types the word into `field:<stem>:kind`, or nothing; a word that is no kind writes nothing. It is a word and chooses nothing: `children` is untouched.
- **One token out of a list** (`filmopen_forms/form_fields.dart`, `tokenChoiceField`) — a scene's **Location** and **Epoch**, a location's **Located within** and a project's **kind**, **Aspect ratio** and **Resolution** are one pull-down, drawn the same way wherever it is asked, each page keeping only its own rule (its words, what an agent may type, how it reads a legacy spelling); its key may be a dotted path (`format.aspect`), where *None* takes out an object it leaves empty, and an entry may carry an icon (an aspect ratio's shape), which the box shows while that entry is chosen. Where a page keeps the shared setter (`tokenSetter`; not the project's kind, whose rule is its own), an agent's word the list does not have writes nothing and logs `form.token.refused {key, reason: not_listed|shape}`. The way: a key that holds nothing — absent, empty or blank — reads as ***None***, with no entry of its own; a token the list does not have is **an entry of its own, as written**, and the sentence that says so stands under the box **as an error** while the field can be written and as the locked value's **helper** when it cannot; the field's own helper is said in **both modes**, which differ in what may be typed and in nothing else; helper and error **wrap** (`kHelperMaxLines`); **choosing the entry already on show writes nothing** — except where the key is there holding nothing, where *None* takes it out; and a value of **another shape** — a number, an object — is drawn as it is, locked (`ShapeLocked`), which no door writes over, an agent's included. The **Name** box every form starts with is one field too (`nameField`): the type's icon, *Title* on a project, and an empty box an error rather than a removal (§7).
- **A scene's form** (`filmopen_forms/scene_edit_tab.dart`) — a scene is a series of events at a time and a place, so its form names **one** location and **one** epoch and lists its characters, through the one type door and in two modes like every form: **Name**; **Synopsis**, multi-line; **Location** (`sceneLocationField`): the project's locations in a menu, each by its name in use with its short name, and ***New location…*** last (`field:<stem>:location.new`), which opens the tree's *New location* through the page (`EntityCreation`, `EntityCreationScope`: the forms may not import the tree, and Compare offers none), the scene's epoch offered first, and puts the new location in the scene — its epoch as `location_epoch` where it is not the scene's and the project still declares it, none where it is the scene's or the project declares it no more (`attachedEpoch`); with no location in the project the control opens *New location* at once; unset it says *Choose where this scene happens*; ***Clear location*** (`field:<stem>:location.clear`) removes the `location` key alone — an unresolved one too, in a project with no location as in any — never a location's file; choosing another location writes its short name and clears `location_epoch` — it resolves at the scene's own epoch — while the one there already writes nothing; a box that can neither choose nor make one — Compare's column, in a project with no location — says why in its tooltip; a press on the box takes the focus, so that the keyboard's keys go where the person last pressed, and the box keeps it while a location is being made — a press meanwhile does nothing — so that the dialog gives it back when it closes; what a scene's boxes said, and their focus, stay with that scene's file and never show on another's; a long name in the menu wraps and is cut on its second line; Enter or Space on the box opens its menu with the entry chosen, else the first, focused, so that the arrows move in it and Enter chooses, the box having the focus back as the menu closes — the Epoch's alike —; where a press makes a location rather than opening a menu, the box shows a `+`, not the menu's arrow; both boxes are buttons to assistive technology; what a box said after a creation stays only while it holds — the field's next change ends it, and the sentence for a draft that could not be written first ends once the draft is the file's again — and is said as a message where the field is gone by then; an agent's `set_field` on Location writes as the menu does, another location clearing `location_epoch`; while empty the box's words are its semantics' value too; **Epoch** (`sceneEpochField`): *Default* while the scene names none — the project's default, which is no entry of the menu — and the declared epochs by their labels, each once, a label two of them share followed by its token; choosing one writes its token; **Delete** or **Backspace** on the focused control, or ***Clear*** (`field:<stem>:epoch.clear`), removes the key and returns it to *Default*, never touching an epoch of the project; **Characters**, one row per `cast` entry — the character's name in use and short name, a remove button — and **Add character**, a menu of the project's characters the scene does not list yet, each item about 56 px tall with the character's 40 px picture (`pictureInUse`: at the scene's epoch, else where its name is read; its icon where it has none), its name and, muted under it, its short name, and ***New character…*** last (`cast:<stem>:add.new`) — the menu's one entry where every character is listed, and the button's own press where the project has none — which opens the tree's *New character* as *New location…* does and puts the character in the cast once, its epoch on the entry where it is not the scene's and the project still declares it (Compare's columns, which offer no creation, keep the menu of characters, disabled with the reason where there is none to add); **Notes**, multi-line. **A creation from the scene keeps the scene** (`createForScene`): the scene's draft is written first, and one that cannot be stops it with nothing made (*Nothing was made: …*); the page stays on the scene; the answer goes only where it was asked for — the same project, the same username, the scene's form still open for writing — else the new entity stays, made, and the scene says so (*"…" was made, but not put in this scene: …*); the attachment works on the draft as it is when the dialog answers, so what was typed meanwhile and every other entry's `epoch`, `outfit` and `notes` stay; once attached the draft saves as every change does, and a refused save is the form's to say, with *Retry*, which writes the attachment again and never makes the entity again; *Cancel* writes nothing. Every way out is logged once, `scene.create.decided {scene, type, key, outcome: noSaver|unsaved|noProject|unmounted|cancelled|attached|notAttached, reason, stem, ms}`, the entity made named where there is one and the reason saying which change kept it from the scene. **Where the project declares more than one epoch, a character's row has an epoch pull-down of its own**, labelled *Character's epoch* so that the form has one box called *Epoch* — the scene's, *As the scene* by default, which writes the entry's `epoch` (the sample's Kira is `30yo` in a scene set in the `1990s`); a project with one epoch shows none, neither as a pull-down nor as a locked value, and an entry that names one keeps it (kept, and shown only where a project declares more than one epoch). **Where the character has two or more outfits at the entry's epoch, else the scene's (the project's default where it names none), each counted, offered and named as its version in use there for the viewer — one deleted there left out, one nothing resolves for offered by its short name — the row has an *Outfit* pull-down** beside the epoch's (`cast:<stem>:<index>.outfit`): *Default outfit* first — the character's `default_outfit`, which writes no key and removes one — then each outfit by its name and picture; a choice writes the entry's `outfit` (`castWithOutfitAt`); the three parts share the row and stack below 560 px of pane. **What an entry holds beside that — `notes`, anything else — is never touched** (§5.13). Each use of *Add character*'s menu, the speaker's and the outfit's is logged — its opening, however it opens (a press, the keyboard, a screen reader's expand), then one outcome with its time, a collapse a cancellation; a press that makes a character at once, or finds no anchor for the menu, one line of its own (`cast.picker.*`, `script.speaker.*`, `cast.outfit.*`). A short name the project has nothing for — a location no file has, an epoch the project does not declare, a character nobody wrote — **is shown as written and said**, under the pull-down as an error on the viewer's own version and as the locked value's helper on another's, as a location's `within` is; a `cast` that is not a list of entries that name a character is drawn as it is, locked (`ShapeLocked`), and never written. Then the plug-ins' cards for `sc` (`pluginSectionsFor`: the *Template* plug-in's *note* is one), then the **Dialog and actions** card (the key `blocks`), which on the page **is where a scene's script is written** (`sceneBlocksSections`, `filmopen_forms/script_editor.dart`'s `ScriptEditor`): one field, `blocks`, on the form's own draft and its one saver, editable exactly where the form is (`FormScope.enabled` — the viewer's own version, or anybody's in development mode, with the page's guard line), drawn wherever `blocks` is a script — absent or empty too — and as the key's one locked value where it holds the ids a cue writes or is no list. **A scene with no block shows one, local**, where the form may write: the placeholder, drawn as a first block open on its fields, with no ⋮ and no handle, and named as one (`script:<stem>:1`), which the draft does not hold — a visit, a focus, a speaker chosen, a duration and blank typing write nothing, neither an entry nor `next_block`, and a speaker or a duration chosen there goes with the page; its first nonblank dialogue, direction or action makes it the scene's first block in one change — the next number of the scene's own count (Project Specification §10.3), with the speaker, the duration and every box's text — the focus and the caret staying where they are; *Add block* makes it the block as it stands; a microphone on it takes no number, its accepted words being its first text; the registry names it nothing until it is a block, so that an agent types into it through its box. A placeholder that comes as the scene's last block goes — deleted, moved to another scene, gone with a re-read — starts empty, never with that block's words; a block another change brings to its place opens as any block does, only the placeholder's own first words keeping their card open; while a speaker or a duration waits on it, a line under it says it is not kept yet; it keeps the room a block's ⋮ and handle take, so that its first words change no box's width under the caret; with no character in the film, its speaker says that *Add character*, above, makes one. Where the form may not write, the card says *This scene has no dialog or actions yet.* and that its author writes them, or a fork, which the Script tab of such a version says too, naming no Edit tab it does not have. **A card per block**, condensed to one line — the dialogue, moved right of its speaker's mark (the first live reference image of the character the speaker resolves to, its thumbnail else its file, about 18 px, or the character's icon where none resolves or there is none, over the speaker's name in the smallest style, one line cut short, the whole name its tooltip; laid in the line's own height, so the row is as tall as an action's; a picture that cannot be read drawn as the icon and logged once per character and reference, `script.speaker.picture {stem, reference, error}`); or the action; or the direction in parentheses, after the speaker's name in capitals and bold; or that name alone, a speaker chosen and nothing said yet; or *(empty block)* — until a field of it holds the focus, a menu of it is open, or it was just added or clicked: then the speaker's pull-down with the *Dialogue* box beside it (under it in one column), *Direction for character* and *Action* (multi-line) — each saying what goes in it while empty, *e.g. look into the camera* and *e.g. Cars are passing by* under the boxes' labels, wrapping rather than cut where the box is narrow, its label kept above it — the block's box alone says *Direction for character*: any other `direction`, a shot's key light, keeps its own word — and *Duration* with the hint *auto*; the block's `output_duration_sec` is not shown here, but kept as it is. A click on the line opens the block with the focus in the field the line shows; Tab reaches the line and opens it on its first box; Shift+Tab reaches its ⋮ first and opens it there, so that it walks back through every box as Tab walks forward (a locked block, which has no ⋮, opens on its own *Duration*, Shift+Tab then passing over its beats' and subtitles' boxes). **Tab then walks the open block's controls in the order they are built** — the speaker, *Dialogue*, *Direction for character*, *Action*, *Duration*, each beat's boxes and its ⋮, *Add beat*, each subtitle's boxes and its ⋮, *Add subtitle*, then the block's ⋮, no microphone being a Tab stop (§9.6) — and the block stays open until the focus leaves the card (Tab past the ⋮, or another field) or a mouse's click lands outside it, which also takes the focus from a button of the card; a click in the card keeps the focus, and nothing condenses it while a press is under way in the card or one of its menus is open. **Where the focus went decides**: a focus **parked** on a scope — the window losing its focus to another application, the app suspended, a box letting the focus go on a press outside — is not leaving, and the block stays (`script.block.kept {stem, block, index, reason: focus_parked, primary}`), its box given the focus back when the window has it again (`kept {reason: focus_returned}`), or kept where the focus lands on what does not count as leaving — a route, a menu (`kept {reason: focus_excepted}`); the next node to take the focus decides, so a finger's tap on another block — which on the desktop parks the focus a frame before moving it — condenses the first block then (`focusLeft`). **The timeline in charge** — the editor's side of the state's script focus (§7), through which a timeline marks a press as its own (`dockPressed`), takes the charge (`following`) and asks for a block: a press so marked condenses nothing (`kept {reason: timeline_press}`); while the timeline is in charge, the block it asks for opens — found by its id where the list is now, so a block moved before the frame opens where it went and one gone opens nothing (`script.focus.skipped {serial, reason: gone}`) — without the keyboard's focus, scrolled into view, and the one open before condenses (reason `timeline`), each request answered `script.focus.answered {stem, asked, block, index, serial, outcome, ms}` — `opened`, naming the block opened and its place, `missing` (no such block here, ignored), `not_following`, `other_scene`, `unmounted` — and one overtaken by the next in its frame logged `script.focus.skipped {reason: overtaken}`; a press in the editor, the person's focus arriving in a card (not the window's focus coming back), or a press elsewhere on the page, which condenses the open block as before, take the charge back (`script.follow`), and the person's focus arriving in a card — Tab, a finger's tap — condenses any other open block (`focusLeft`), a block the timeline opened holding no focus to lose. The block open is published as that script focus after the frame that opened or condensed it; an opening whose card went or changed before then is not, logged `script.focus.skipped {reason: unmounted | changed}`, as is any publication whose scope is gone (`scope_gone`). Opening is always an act — a click, Tab or Shift+Tab into the block, a block just added, a speaker chosen: the focus coming to the ⋮ after a move leaves the moved block condensed, and a block that comes to an open block's place — after a move to another scene, a delete, a re-read — is condensed there. **A block stays open behind a popup above the page** — its microphone's, a dialog — and a click in the popup is not outside it, so dictated text lands in its box and the focus comes back to the box that had it. **Each box that can be typed into has its microphone** (§9.6) — *Dialogue* once a speaker opens it, *Direction for character*, *Action* and *Duration* — dictated text going in as typing does. **Dictation is bound to the block, never to its place**: a block with no id takes its number as dictation starts, as at its first change, and the words go in only where the block at that place is still that block — where another block came to its place (a re-read after another hand's insert), or the block moved or went — told by the id at its place —, they are dropped, the popup closes saying *Dictation discarded: the block changed*, nothing is written, and no box takes the focus. Where the page itself went, the box went as any box goes: the popup stays, says so, and keeps the words to copy. The usage record names the block's field as `blocks.<id>.<field>` where its id is `bl_<n>`, else as `blocks.#<place>.<field>`: a block's id is the file's free text, and nothing of a file's free text goes to a log. **The speaker's pull-down** lists *No speaker* (which removes `character`), then the characters the scene names — its cast's and its blocks' speakers — in the text colour, a line, then every other character of the project, muted, each group by the name shown (the Script tab's naming rule, `speakerName`), each about 56 px tall by its picture as in *Add character*; a short name nobody wrote is shown as written and said under the box; choosing writes `character` and nothing else, never the cast, and puts the focus in the dialogue. **The dialogue box is open exactly when a speaker is chosen**; without one its text stays legible, locked, with *Choose a character to write dialogue*. *Duration* takes a number of seconds above zero and a day (86 400) at most — finite, so that hundreds of digits, which parse to infinity, are refused with the rest — with a dot for decimals (`4.`, on the way to `4.5`, is written as 4, the box keeping its text); anything else is said inline and not written, the refusal kept with the block it was typed for, so that a re-read putting another block at its place shows that block's own value; an emptied box removes the key (*auto*). **A block's beats** (Project Specification §10.3) are listed under its duration and above its media, indented: each a row of *Position* — *Before the line*, *During the line*, *After the line*, shown only where the block has dialogue, a position outside the three shown as written —, *Beat* (prose, the block's @-mentions), *Duration* (parsed and refused as the block's own, hint *auto*), a ⋮ (*Move up*, *Move down*, *Delete beat*) and a handle that reorders the block's beats only, the beat's words travelling under the pointer; *Add beat* under them adds one at the end, *During the line* where the block has dialogue. Each edit is one change of the draft. A block's boxes are made when its card first opens, or an agent types into it, and seeded then from the draft (`script.box.made {stem, box, seed, live}`), so that a block whose card never opened holds none and a change seeds only the boxes made; they are kept by block and beat place, so a beat added, removed or moved, and the block's own move, seed them again from the draft and drop their refused durations. Beside the duration (under it in one column) *Beats: 7 s*, their seconds' sum, where the block has beats; in the caution colour with *longer than the block's duration* where the block's duration is set and the beats' seconds add up to more; under the list, where the block has dialogue, *A beat before the line follows one during it.* Neither warning refuses anything. None on the placeholder, which is no block yet; locked, the beats are read-only, with no ⋮, handle or *Add beat*. Every beat edit logs `script.beat.<op>.start` (`op` added, changed, removed or moved, `source` person or set_field, `block` by its log name, `k`, `to`, `field`, the `value` entered, the `beat` as it was) and one outcome — `.ok` (`outcome` applied or unchanged, the `beat` as written), `.refused` (`reason`: the helper's code, `duration` for seconds not given, or an agent's `beatOutOfRange`, `unknownField`, `unknownPosition`, with the key, the value and the beat it would have replaced) or `.failed` (`stage`, `cause`, `error`, the error's message), rethrown — each with `ms`; the beat's menu `script.beat.menu.opened`, then `chosen` (`action`) or `cancelled`, and its position's pull-down `script.beat.position.opened`, then `chosen` or `unchanged` (`position` as chosen) or `cancelled`, each with `ms`; a beat's description is logged as written; a warning `script.beats.warned` (`reason` order or total, `total`, `duration`) once as it comes and `script.beats.cleared` (`ms`) as it goes; each of these lines names its block as `block` (its log name) and `block_id` (its own id), the clearing line naming the id the block had when it was warned. **A block's subtitles** (Project Specification §10.3) are laid out exactly as its beats, under *Add beat*: each a row of *Position* (as a beat's), *Subtitle* — plain text, a newline a line break, no @-mention, since the words are burnt over the picture as typed —, *Duration*, a ⋮ (*Move up*, *Move down*, *Delete subtitle*) and a handle that reorders the block's subtitles only; *Add subtitle* under them, *During the line* where the block has dialogue. *Subtitles: 5 s* stands under *Beats: 7 s* beside the duration where the block has both, in the caution colour with *longer than the block's duration* as the beats' does; under the list *A subtitle before the line follows one during it.* Their boxes are kept by block and subtitle place, as the beats' are. Beats and subtitles are one editor: every subtitle edit, its menu and its pull-down log as a beat's, under `script.subtitle.*` with the `subtitle` and `subtitleOutOfRange`; a warning `script.subtitles.warned` and `script.subtitles.cleared`. A subtitle's words are logged as written. Each card's menu: *Move up*, *Move down* (disabled at an end, saying so; the focus follows the moved block), *Insert block after*, **Move to scene…**, *Delete block* (no confirmation, and the block's number is never given out again in this file, Project Specification §10.3); a drag handle reorders, the block's line travelling under the pointer. The ⋮ and the handle stand at the card's right, and under an open block's fields where the form is one column (below 560 px of pane, §9.1), so that the fields take the card's whole width — *Direction for character* whole at a 700 px window; the placeholder keeps their room in the same place. A later block of an id the file uses twice, and an entry that is not a block, are drawn locked as JSON with what to do, and only *Delete block*; the first block of a doubled id stays editable by hand, while no agent's setter and no move can address it. A speaker chosen stays on the condensed line while nothing is said yet (beside the direction, or alone), never beside an action; a film with no character yet says under the speaker's box where one comes from. **No block id is ever shown.** **An open block's two frame places are always shown, each under its Prompt card**, *First-frame prompt* above *First frames* and *Last-frame prompt* above *Last frames* (`_FramePrompt` in `script_editor.dart`, the place's header; the writers of `first-frame` and `last-frame`, given the block's id), **whether or not a frame place holds anything**: a prompt is written before its image. **A *Clip* place, where shown, takes the block's clip prompt as its header the same way** (the writers of `clip`, the block's `prompts.clip`), yet is not always shown: *Add a place…* offers it, and it is shown where it holds a take or where the block's `prompts.clip` is stored, a prompt being written before its clip; its `@` offers what the frames' cards offer (the film's characters', locations' and props' pictures), and its writer is handed the block alone and no tokens, since the page knows no model to take them. Its boxes, `field:<stem>:blocks.<id>.prompts.<kind>.positive` and `.negative`, are prose boxes in the block's tap group whose `@` offers the film's pictures (§9.8), and write the block's `prompts.<kind>` through `withBlockPrompt` as a keystroke does, a changed word taking the prompt's `plugin` away (logged `prompt.pluginCleared`); the holder whose `updated` a frame's prompt is read against is the block; its Generate writes through the scene's draft (`ScriptEditor.binding`, the form's saver). **Its media, under its fields** (Project Specification §10.3; `_BlockMedia` in `script_editor.dart`): the two frame places, and a media place for each of the block's other four groups that holds entries, in the table's order — *Clip*, *First frames*, *Last frames*, *Speech*, *Music*, *Sound effects* — each a `MediaPlace` on a `BlockPlace` of the block's id and the group, editing through the scene's draft as any keystroke does, telling its observers no key; then, where the block may be written, *Add a place…* (`media:<stem>:<block id>.place`) naming those of the four not shown, which shows an empty place to add to and writes nothing, logging `block.place.opened {block, offered}`, then `block.place.chosen {block, group, ms}` or `block.place.cancelled {block, ms}`. The condensed line shows no tile. A block without an id, or of an id the scene gives twice, has no media and no *Add a place…*, since no place could name it. Each block opened or condensed is logged `script.block.opened` / `script.block.closed {stem, block, index, reason, ms}`, `ms` the time it spent in the state it leaves, the reason the act — `tap`, `tab`, `shiftTab`, `added`, `typed`, `speaker`, `timeline`; `focusLeft`, `tapOutside`, `timeline`, `blockChanged` (another block came to its place, named as the block that closed), `listChanged`, `disposed` (its card went while it was open) — the block by its id as it is, its index beside it. The places are drawn only while the block is open, so what a place says of itself — a failure, the archived fold, a group just shown and still empty — goes when the block condenses; the queue keeps every job and its failure, and a job comes back with its block. A block's take wears no *new* mark. **Move to scene…** (`filmopen_forms/script_move.dart`, `showMoveBlockDialog`; its trail `script.blockMove.start {film, from, block, to, toName, viewer, forks}`, `script.blockMove.stage {stage: settle | read | recheck | target | remove, outcome, ms}` and `script.blockMove {film, from, block, to, toName, viewer, outcome: refused | copied | moved, reason, stage, ms}` with the values that decided it — a coverage's `position`, `first`, `last`, the `covering` entry with its group and owning block, and the block's `content`, the target's refusal, the version `written`) lists every other scene of the film, one line a scene, in script order (`scriptOutline`: the script, then what is not in it), its folders as headings, a scene listed twice drawn once, an entry nothing resolves for left out, a scene whose version in use is in the recycle bin left out (Project Specification §7) — and a destination deleted after the list was drawn refused before any write (*That scene is now in the recycle bin, so nothing was moved.*) —, each named as the tree names it for the viewer; **the target is the version in use for the viewer** (`pickAt`) — written where it is theirs, forked where it is not, the line saying *will fork {author}'s v{n}* and the button *Fork and move*, else *Move*. On confirm, in this order: every open draft written (one that will not stops the move); the block read again **from this scene's draft as it now is**, by its id; **a coverage across it refused there, before anything is written** — the block's own entry whose `spans` lists another block, or another block's entry listing it, read through the format's readers, a deleted entry covering nothing — with *Media of block {n} covers blocks {n} to {m}, so this block cannot move to another scene.*, the blocks named by position, while an extent (`until`) or an entry of its own block alone moves freely, the block's `media` travelling with it as content and the film's records staying where they are; the target read afresh and its content built from that read, the block copied **under the target's next number** with every other key (`output_duration_sec` among them); **the target written first**, once — `WriteActions.trySaveOver` on the viewer's own version, `tryForkWith` holding the new content otherwise — a refusal (changed on disk since that read, the film gone) said in the dialog with *Nothing was moved*; the block compared again with what was copied, a change meanwhile stopping with the copy kept and nothing removed; then the block removed through the form's draft as a change of the list, **the draft settled**, and the dialog closes with *Moved to {scene}* (`script.blockMoved`: the two stems and whether it forked) **only where the removal's save was not refused and the scene's file, read back through the source after it, no longer holds the block** — never the project's index, which overlapping re-reads can leave older than the block's own addition. A refused save — the saver left failed — is said as the copy kept, with the refusal, whatever any index says; the saver's error alone decides nothing, since it keeps an older failure after a later success, and a write that landed after another's failure holds the removal; a save that landed while more was typed is a move, since the file has the removal. **The page's draft must be the one open on the scene's file** (`OpenDrafts.of`): asked once the drafts are written and before the target is, where a page that went to another file, or away, refuses with *Not moved: the page changed before the move.* and nothing is written anywhere; and asked again before the removal, where the block stays in this scene and the dialog says so, naming it, since the page under the dialog may show another by then — *The block was copied to {scene}, and {source} still holds it: delete it in {source}.* — as it does wherever the file still holds the block after the settle. A save refused after the copy says so in the dialog — *the block is already in {scene}; this scene's file still holds it until this save goes through* — and the card's save status shows the failure. The dialog cannot be closed while a move is under way (neither Escape nor a click beside it), and an outcome reached after it has gone is said on the page; the list is drawn from the film as it is now, so after a move that stopped half-way a fork made for it is the version in use. *Move* is disabled, saying *Choose a scene first*, until a scene is chosen. A crash between the two writes leaves the block in both scenes, never in neither. Disabled, saying why, on a block the file gives no id yet (*Type in this block once before moving it*), on the first block of a doubled id, and on another author's version open in development mode (*Blocks are not moved out of another author's version, even in development mode: fork the scene to move its blocks.*): a move removes the block from this version, which must be the viewer's own. The editor owns no draft and no writer: a keystroke reaches the saver as a change that tells the draft's observers no key — the whole script never travels with a letter — and a change of the list tells them `blocks`. Its boxes are its own, by position and field, and re-seeded only where the draft changed under them, so a caret elsewhere stays. `blocks` is never under *Other attributes* (`hiddenKeys`, said outright and not asked of the file), and never copied as one value; nor is the scene's `next_block`, the format's own count, which the card's field owns so that it is shown nowhere, and which copy-left never moves on a scene (`uncopyableKeys`), whatever either side holds — a version with no `blocks` key included: a copy could lower it and let a deleted number come back — then, folded in *Other*, *Other attributes* with everything else the file holds (`story_day`, `time`, `props`, `location_epoch` and the rest). In Compare the rows pair — *Location*, *Epoch* and *Characters* are one row each, and copy-left moves `cast` as it moves any list — and the blocks are a row per block, paired by id as before and labelled by their place in that version, `#n`, never by their id. An agent types into every field (`FieldRegistry`), a block's included — `blocks.<id>.character` (a short name, or nothing), `.dialogue`, `.direction`, `.action`, `.duration_sec` (a number, or nothing), each through the editor's own setter beside the form's, false (the agent's `not_found`) for an id the scene does not have or gives twice, and for a dialogue no speaker has opened: a short name into `location` and `epoch`, or nothing to remove the key, a name the project does not have writing nothing (the one the file names already may be typed back, which changes nothing); and **`cast` one short name per line**, a line that names neither a character of the project nor one the scene lists already refusing the whole text. Identifiers: `field:<stem>:name|synopsis|location|epoch|cast|notes`, a row `cast:<stem>:<index>` with its `.epoch`, `.outfit` and `.remove`, `cast:<stem>:add` and the menu's `cast:<stem>:add.<short name>`; a block `script:<stem>:<n>` (the n-th as listed, 1 first, its label the condensed line) with, once opened, `.character`, `.dialogue`, `.direction`, `.action`, `.duration`, and on every block `.menu` and `.handle`; `script:<stem>:add`; *Move to scene…*'s `script.move:<short name>`, `script.move.confirm` and `script.move.cancel`. **Locked** — on another author's version without development mode, the library, a read-only project — the blocks are the same cards, condensed, with no ⋮, no handle and no *Add block*; an opened one is read-only and legible, its speaker the box a pull-down leaves behind and no validation shown.
- **Category view** — a list of every entity of one type with tag, epoch and version counts and an "official set" chip. The character, location and prop pages carry **Add character** / **Add location** / **Add prop** (`category.add`, its words the page's type) beside the title and the count, and under the empty state when there are none; disabled with *This project is read-only here* on a project that cannot be written. It opens the **new character / new location / new prop dialog** (`filmopen_tree/new_entity_dialog.dart` — the tree's row menu opens the same dialog for a folder and a scene, §9.2, and the tree may import neither the forms nor the pages, while the pages import the tree), laid out as the New project dialog is: a sentence saying the filename is permanent — *The filename is permanent, so pick something generic like "main-hero" if the name isn't decided yet.* for a character, *The filename is permanent.* for a location and a prop; the **Filename** in a `ShortNameField` — a segment of every file name of the entity, which its tooltip says, with *main-hero* as a character's example while the box is empty and focused, never a value — refused inline when the type already has that tag here or in the library; the name (optional — an empty box writes the short name as the name, since §7 requires one); a character's **Role** (the key `kind`: a pull-down of A.1's vocabulary, nothing written when none is chosen) — a location is made without a kind, its form setting one later; a prop's **Kind**, a pull-down of A.3's ten in A.3's order with *None* first and chosen, nothing written when none is chosen, since A.3 requires none (a prop is an ordinary library entity at an epoch: its file and its pick, no folder); and the **epoch**, only where the project declares several, the default first, or the scene's where a scene asks (below). **No username box**: the file is written as the username in effect (§9.4), `default` where none is chosen, read once a change of it in flight has landed; a change made while the dialog was open is said inside it (*Your username changed from … to …*), what was typed kept, and the next *Create* writes as the new one. *Create* is disabled until the filename is valid and free, **and says why in its tooltip, the reason naming its box** as New project's does (*Filename: …*; on the button's own semantics node); the short names a list already names (`takenChildTags`, which walks every list of every version) are read once for an index and not at every keystroke; a refused write is shown inside the dialog — a file of that name written since the index was read as the same *already taken* sentence, the refusal reading that one file into the index (§7.5), so the name then shows as taken while it is typed. Once written the page moves to the new version, whose first tab is Edit — from the category page; a scene that asked stays on screen.
- **Epoch overview** (`detail/epoch_overview.dart`) — what a library entity chosen as a whole shows (no epoch, no version: the group is not a file, §8.2): for a character or a location, one card per epoch **the project declares**, in its order (a hand-written token that is no filename segment excepted), and after them any epoch the entity has that the project does not declare; any other type, which the writer does not copy between epochs, one card per epoch it has. A card with a version has the picture (no type or tag chip under the heading) (the version in use's `preview`, else its `picks.sheet`, else the first image take at that epoch), the epoch named as everywhere — its label, else its year, else, for an unlabelled declaration, its token (`epochNaming` in `filmopen_state/tree_builder.dart`) — with the year beside a label and no token chip, the age for a character (`filmopen_format/character_age.dart`'s `ageAt`: the file's `appearance.age`, a whole number or N of `"N (YYYY)"`, none for a phrase), the version in use and the version count; it opens the epoch, and the epoch row has an *All epochs* chip back. **A card with no version yet** names the epoch and says so, and offers a copy: *Based on* (`overview.<epoch>.basedOn`), a pull-down of the epochs at which the entity resolves for the viewer (`FilmProject.pickAt`), the first in the project's order chosen, and *Create* (`overview.<epoch>.create`), which makes the version in use there the viewer's v1 at this epoch (`ProjectWriter.createAtEpoch` through `WriteActions.createAtEpoch` and `ProjectController.createAtEpoch`): a complete independent copy naming its source in `forked_from`, picked where the pick can be written and otherwise in use by the ordinary rule, the card filling after the re-read and a message naming the epoch's label. The source is bound at the click, by stem and by text, and checked again in the entity's commentary queue: a pick completed meanwhile, the same version re-read with other bytes, the epoch no longer declared or a version made there meanwhile, another handle or another film refuses with nothing written — a changed binding says *What this was based on changed meanwhile: check it and try again*, changed bytes the stale-file sentence, and a source in use nowhere any more that nothing resolves; where another film is open by the time the copy is written, nothing is said over it, a refusal included, and a refusal's *Re-read files* reads through the app, never the card, which may be gone by then. Where versions exist and none is in use for the viewer at any epoch, the card says *No version of this character is in use at any epoch: pick one first* and offers no copy. *Create* is disabled, saying why, where `PageAccess.canCreateVersions` does not hold or the version to copy was not read as text. A blank first version is not made here: that is *Add character* / *Add location*'s, since the overview exists only for an entity that has a version. **A character's outfits** (`outfit_list.dart`): every card of a character, with a version or without, lists beneath what it holds the outfits **at that epoch and no other** — each by its picture (as a card's), its name as its version in use there says it — its id where nothing is in use there — and that version, a tap (`overview.<epoch>.outfit.<tag>`) opening the outfit at that epoch — or says that none is yet, and ends with **New outfit** (`overview.<epoch>.newOutfit`), which asks a **name only** in a dialog naming the character and the epoch (`outfit.new-name`, *Create* — a target of standard density, 44 px at least — disabled until one is typed, a refusal cleared by retyping), bound to the film it was opened in: another film by the time *Create* is pressed refuses in the dialog before anything is allocated, *Create* then disabled with that refusal as its tooltip and *Cancel* the way out: the id is allocated by the writer (`ProjectWriter.createOutfit`, `<character>.<owner>-<n>`), never typed. It runs through `WriteActions.createOutfit` and `ProjectController.createOutfit`, in a queue of the character and the viewer's owner (§7), bound at the click to the film and the viewer: the new outfit's page opens at that epoch with a message, and where the id was taken meanwhile — creation writes no file the person has open, so a changed file there means another outfit took the place — the folder is re-read and the creation tried once more with a writer on the new index, a second collision being said in the dialog as that (*Another outfit took that place meanwhile*), never as a changed file; a character no longer there, a refusal of its own; an epoch no longer declared by then, the undeclared sentence below; any other failure the dialog's own sentence, never an error's text; a film closed or replaced by the time it answers hears nothing of it, and the dialog closes. The heading's help says, for a character, that each card lists its outfits, and that *New outfit* adds one where one can be made. A declared epoch already has its card; one the project does not declare where only the character's outfits are has a card of its own after the declared ones, its outfits listed; **where the project declares epochs and not this one**, the card is for reading — its line says so in place of *No version at this epoch yet*, and *Based on*, *Create* and *New outfit* are disabled, each with the same reason as its tooltip (*The project does not declare this epoch: what is here opens, and nothing new is made at it*), since the writer and the controller refuse an undeclared epoch and the project's form declares only epochs it allocates; the heading's help then promises no copy there. A project declaring none makes no version at a missing epoch either, since the controller copies one only to a declared epoch: *Based on* and *Create* are disabled with *The project declares no epochs: a version is made at an epoch once the project declares it* as the card's line and their tooltip, and *New outfit* stays, as the writer makes an outfit at any epoch of such a project. *New outfit* is disabled, saying why, where `PageAccess.canCreateVersions` does not hold. Below **720 px of pane** (`Breakpoints.overviewTwoColumns`) the cards are one to a row. On a character whose every version is discarded *New outfit* is disabled at every epoch, the overview passing the reason in as it passes its others: *Every version of this character is discarded: no outfit can be added*. **An epoch whose every version is discarded** keeps a card with a version — the card and *Create* read every version, the discarded included, so no *Create* the controller would refuse is offered there — whose line names the versions (*Every version here is discarded: ann · v1, bob · v1*), and counts them all, with no action: nothing brings a discarded version back. A missing card of an entity whose every version is discarded says so — *there is nothing to copy to this epoch* — rather than asking for a pick that cannot be made.
- **Entity detail** — header (icon and the name, nothing else: took the type, stem, library, forked-from, updated and kind chips away); an **Epoch** row when the entity has several, in the project's order, each chip the epoch's label with its year beside it, never its token; and the version row (`detail/version_row.dart`), with no *Version* label beside it or inside its pull-down: the **author** of this version as text (other authors' versions are one click away in the tree), a pull-down of that author's versions (`v2`, or `v2 · goth` when labelled; star and/or check as trailing icons), the **label** button (right of the pull-down, left of the star, the author only, a small dialog writes `version_label`, §7, removing an old `label`), the **official star** (a toggle: grey, yellow when set; enabled for directors of a writable project; setting writes the pointer, clearing deletes it), the **check** — the version in use for the viewer (§6.1 step 2): on the version in use that nothing explicit chose (official with the star alone, your latest, the only author's) a press records it as your own pick, the version itself and never the tracking value, so that it holds whatever another author or the assistant writes later and a draft of the assistant's that you pick is no longer a draft, its next change making a new one (§7.3); on the version your pick, or one you follow official by, already names it only says why it is in use (your pick, you follow official), writing nothing; grey and clickable on every other version, where a click moves the pick here (on the official version a click removes your pick, since no pick means official — unless your own file still holds a pick naming no version here, a missing file, which the check on the official version then removes); enabled for any viewer of a writable project, library versions included, since the pick is the project's own commentary; the decision is made in your commentary file's queue, against the index the previous write left, and logged as `version.pick.start {kind: explicit-in-use|ordinary|already-picked, reason, ignoredPick?}` (the stem of the viewer's own pick where it named a discarded version and was ignored) then `version.pick {outcome: picked|already|gone|stale|failed|refused, kind, reason, stage: decide|write|refresh, refresh, ms}`, a failure with its error's type, a stem only as a digest, the press itself as `write.pickFromCheck {stem, inUse}` — and **Fork**. Nothing follows Fork (*No version in use*, *In use:* and *Official is* chips are gone, the tree still flags an epoch where nothing is in use). Tooltips say why a control is disabled (no username, read-only, directors only). **On a plug-in the star and the check are unlit and disabled** (`PageAccess.choosesNothing`), whoever the viewer and in development mode too, the pull-down's star and check are not drawn, and both say *A plug-in runs the version installed; remove it and install another to change it.* — one installed version of each plug-in runs whatever the film (§6.11), so a pick or a pointer there would choose nothing; the check on the version in use says nothing either. **On a discarded version** (Project Specification §7) the star and the check are disabled, each saying *This version is discarded*, since it resolves for nobody (`PageAccess`). **Where your own pick names a discarded version** it is ignored: the version in use — the fallback, or a pick your workspace inherits — is unchecked, its check saying *The pick names a discarded version, "…", and is ignored — Use this version* (on official, *— Use the official version: …*), and a press writes your pick of it in place of the ignored one. **Two boxes point at the check** (`check_popover.dart`'s `CheckPopover`, in the overlay under the version row, tapering at its top into an arrow at the check, kept inside the page's pane — never over the docked assistant — and its text wrapped, so that nothing overflows at 375 px): after an assistant's verified `update-entity` or `create-entity` (the command's observation, `ok`, names the stem written) the change is said with *Open* (`assistant.write.open`), whatever entity it changed, and the page moves by itself only to a chat's record (§6.11a); *Open* opens that version as a click would, on *View/Edit* whatever tab was open, and the box says *This version is a draft with ai changes. Pick it, go back to the prior version, or compare to pick which changes to keep.* — on a version the assistant created, which has no prior version and so no *Go back*, *This version is a draft with ai changes. Pick it, or compare to pick which changes to keep.* —, **bordered in the theme's error colour**, with *Compare* — the prior version's page on Compare with the draft in the left column (`openCompare`), nothing written, logged `assistant.page {action: compare, ok}` — and *Go back* — the person's version in use opened, nothing written — where there is a prior version. **The box shows whenever the page shows the person's draft** (§7.3's `assistantDraftOf`), on every opening, not only after a write, until the person picks it. A verified change of any entity — the page's own, a character the assistant made from the project page, another it changed — moves nothing by itself: a message names it, *The assistant changed <name>.*, with *Open* (`assistant.write.open`), which opens that version with its box, so that the page and the chat on it stay where they are; each is logged `assistant.page.decision {command, decision: messaged, reason: no automatic move, stem, page}`, the stem and the page's tree key as they are. Wherever the version on screen is not the one in use and the viewer may pick, a smaller box says *Use this version*, never over the first. A box without a link — *Use this version*, and the first after a created entity — takes no taps, so a tap on it reaches what lies under it (the tabs at a narrow width), and dismisses *Use this version*. Either goes on the check's activation (a click, a key or a screen reader's tap), a move to another version, or its link, and *Use this version* also on a tap outside it or a wheel or trackpad scroll; **the draft's box waits for the person's decision through a tap outside and a scroll**. Gone, a box stays gone until the next write, or until such a version is shown again — the draft's box included, which every opening of the draft shows again; nothing of it is kept across navigations (`assistantWriteProvider` holds the last write while its version is on screen). It is placed from where the check was laid out, measured after the frame while it is shown and again whenever a scrollable around the check scrolls, so that it follows the check as the header scrolls; where the check is scrolled out of the pane's sight, the box keeps to the pane's nearest edge, top or bottom, with no arrow, and the overlay holds it only while it shows, so that a page without it stands under no empty layer and the app is idle between frames. Logged as `assistant.page {action: opened|shown|dismissed|back, kind: write|use, …}`: `shown` with the version and its reason (`written`, `draft`, `not-in-use`), `dismissed` with its `cause` (`check`, `outside`, `scroll`, `action`, `secondary`, `move`, `write`) and how long it was shown, `back` with the version opened and why it is in use; an observation whose label is no version's stem is `refused {cause: not-a-stem}`; a stem only as a digest. A failed or refused change shows nothing new: the chat says why. Then tabs:
 - **The media card** (`filmopen_forms/media_section.dart`, `MediaCard`) is a card **within View/Edit** on each entity type that has been given it — the second on a character, a location, an outfit and a prop, right below *General*; on the project file after *General*, *Synopsis* and *Epochs*, above the treatment's prompt and *Other*, which holds the visual treatment its pictures are described into, and on a folder after *Details*, just above its visual treatment — part of the view, not above it. A type gains it with **one line** in its tab (`characterFormSections`, `locationFormSections`, `outfitFormSections`, `propFormSections`, `projectFormSections`, `folderFormSections`), and a type that has not been given it simply does not have it; what an upload *is* for that type is the format's to say (`uploadPrefixFor`: a picture of a place is a concept sheet like a picture of a person, `cs`, and a sound of a place is ambience, `am`, where a character's is a voice sample, `vo` — the prefix names the purpose, not the origin, §5.5), so a tab knows nothing of media beyond the line. The card owns `media`, `preview` and the voice in use (`voice.active_reference`), which therefore leave *Other attributes*, and shows them as **places**, one media place per group (below): the type's places of the format's table (`entityMediaPlaces`), always and in its order, each taking its kinds through the file dialog, a drop or an address — a character's *Reference images*, *Character sheets*, *Voice samples* with **the voice panel** under it (below), *Inspirations* and *Motion clips*; an outfit's *Reference images*, *Outfit sheets* (the place its composite sheet will be rendered into) and *Inspirations*; a location's *Reference images*, *Location sheets* and *Inspirations*; a prop's *Reference images*, *Prop sheets* and *Inspirations*; the project's and a folder's *Reference images* alone, on the project *Cover image*; neither the outfit nor the prop offers *Analyze*; a group the file holds that its type does not list stays in the file and is not shown, logged once per display as `media.places {stem, type, shown, hidden}`, the stem as its digest and the two counts the places shown and the groups hidden; a `preview` no entry names, as a tile of its own under *Preview* (`media:<stem>:preview:1`), opened as any tile is, its border its record's stage and the status the draft gives it, with nothing to review; on its title row, always, ***the stage*** its renders are asked at (`StageControl`, `media:<stem>.stage`, over the shared `StageSelector` the render dialog uses too, which writes and logs nothing of its own): *Prototype*, *Draft*, *Final*, left to right, each with its tooltip, written as the version's `render_stage` through the form and read *Prototype* where it says none or one of another shape, held with its reason where the form may not write, the segments' initials where the card is narrow, and the card's field owning the key so that *Other attributes* and a comparison do not show it — the card's title row wraps, the stage and the save status moving to a line of their own where the title leaves them no room; logged `media.stage.start {stem, from, to}`, `media.stage.done {stem, outcome: changed|same, ms}`, `media.stage.refused {stem, reason: readOnly}`, the stem as its digest, and on a move up `media.stage.outdated` for each render it puts below the stage (§6.9). The card has no *Add a place…*: a type's places are fixed. Every edit of the entries goes through the form's draft (`FormScope.change`), so the autosave writes it as it writes a typed field. **The card owns the display's last look** (§13.4): made when the page opens or the version on screen changes, it reads the viewer's `visited` at the epoch once, hands that instant to its places, which mark what arrived after it, and **writes the look when the person opens one of its media** — an entry's tile or its menu's *Open* (`MediaPlace.onOpened`), a sample's tile in the voice panel (`VoicePanel.onOpened`), or the loose preview's tile — through `WriteActions.visit` and the state's one door, where any medium the card shows (its places, the page's chosen groups, the loose preview) is newer than the last look, or where there was none; nothing is written on the card's first frame, on a landing or by scrolling; never without a handle, never on a project that cannot be written, and never shown; each open is logged, written or not, `media.visit {stem, cause: open, opened, place, viewer, newest, since, decision: write|skip, reason: firstLook|newMedia|nothingNew|readOnly|noViewer, ms}`; the marks stay until the look is written, then go. **It owns the delete, too**: a tile's *Delete* writes the flag through the draft, then `WriteActions.deleteTake` writes every open draft (`OpenDrafts.settleOpen`), stops with *The file was not deleted: this page's changes, the entry's deletion among them, could not be saved yet.* where one did not land, reads the entity again and stops with *the file changed meanwhile* where the flag is not in it, and only then asks the state to delete the take's file, saying what went — *Deleted, and its file with it.*, *Deleted from this version.*, or why the file stays (`writeProblemText`). The entry is gone from the page once its flag is in the draft, so where the file stays for a reason that may pass — the changes unsaved, the file held by another program — the message offers *Retry*, which runs the delete again; another author's take and a take still named elsewhere stay by the rule, and are said without it. Where the page is gone once the drafts are written, the file stays, since no page would say what went; the flag is in the file and `write.deleteTake` ends refused as `page-gone` with its time. A tile opens the viewer (`filmopen_media_ui/media_viewer.dart`), **a dialog as large as the window**, 24 px from each edge, the picture taking the room the lines around it leave and the file's name naming it for a screen reader; Escape closes it: the picture at full resolution in an `InteractiveViewer`, the file's name, then the tile's own status box (the same `MediaState` its caller built for the tile, the outdated sentence under an unreviewed box; none where the caller gives none), then its kind and size, the provenance strip's words and the entry's note under the picture (`player.note`), *Show in folder* where the file is on this machine (`player.reveal`), and *Close* (`player.close`). A clip or a sound plays **through the engine the scenes play through** (`player/media_player*.dart`): the app's `main` sets `mediaEngineFactory` to the factory it installs for the dock (§19.16), and the player makes an engine of its own from it, probes the file, prepares its one-file timeline (`fileTimeline`, §19.2: a clip as one video item with its own sound, a sound as black and one speech lane at 0 dB) and shows the engine's texture at the file's aspect, a sound with its waveform icon over the black picture — **and plays at once**: every place that opens the viewer does so on a person's gesture — a tile's tap or the tile menu's *Open* — so `showMediaViewer` asks its player to play unless it is told `autoplay: false`. The engine's calls run one after another, and one the engine refuses is said under the controls by its code (`player.failure`) and never retried. The popup is modal, so a person cannot reach what is under it while it is open, and **closing it releases the engine**, after the calls already asked for — `player.released {path, kind, why, ms}` once it is; an engine still opening when the popup closes is released by the opening itself, once. The log says how far it got: `player.open.start {path, kind, engine, autoplay}`, the engine's own `engine.*` lines (§19.9), `player.ready {path, kind, engine, duration_ms, fps, width, height, texture, playing, ms}`, `player.<op>.start` and `.ok` for each play, pause and seek, `player.playing` where the engine's clock changes it, and `player.failed {path, kind, stage, code, cause, ms}`, the stage `probe`, `prepare`, `play`, `pause`, `seek` or `release` and the cause as the engine gave it. (The decoder is the engine's; the **controls row is a widget of its own** (`player/player_controls.dart`, over a `PlayerNow` of position, length and whether it is playing), so what a person presses is pumped at 700 px rather than stood in for. `player.play` / `player.pause` are one control named for what pressing it does next — a file that has run out reads *Play*, and plays from the start — and `player.clock` the time it shows; `player.seek` follows the finger and asks the engine for **one** seek when it lifts, holding the thumb where it was let go until the player arrives there or two seconds pass, and is disabled with its reason for a file that does not say how long it is — whose clock still counts up. The player is made when a tile is opened and never at start, so nothing constructs one in a widget test.) **Where the engine plays no media** — the fake, which refuses every probe `unavailable`: the web and a desktop without the native layer — the player says *Coming soon* (`player.no_engine`), as it does for an engine that gives no texture (`player.no_texture`), and as the viewer does itself for bytes a session holds only in memory, which no engine opens since the application writes no temporary copy, and in a build that sets no engine; *Show in folder* stays where the file is on disk. A file the engine cannot read is this file's, not the engine's absence: it says *This file cannot be played here…* with the engine's code (`player.failed` at stage `probe`) — the walk proves it with bytes that are not a clip under an `.mp4` name; the sample's own placeholders are a clip with no tracks and a silent sound with no frames, and what an engine makes of each is its own to say. **An address a file names is never fetched**: the viewer shows it and says why, since a reference may be a URL (§6.3) and a file is somebody else's work. The upload sheet's primary button **stays enabled** and says what is missing under the box (*There is no address here yet.* for an empty box, *An address starting with http:// or https://* for anything that is not `http` or `https`), rather than being disabled until the box parses: a control whose enabled state follows the last keystroke cannot be pressed in the same breath as the typing, which is how both an agent and a person pasting with the keyboard work. A place's add tile is drawn only where the form accepts input (`PageAccess`): a read-only source hides it, as do somebody else's version or a missing handle when those rules make the page read-only, and the page's guard explains the refusal; the tiles open in every mode. Compare pairs the card by its id and a `<` **adds, group by group, the live entries the left version lacks and removes none** (`CopyMedia` in `filmopen_format/copy_left.dart`), giving an entry both hold in the same group the right's set flags and note where the left's is not deleted, copying `preview` as any other word — but never one naming an entry the left holds deleted, nor one the right holds in an entry the copy cannot bring — and no bytes at all. **The takes tab** draws every take of the entity with the same tile (`filmopen_browser/takes_tab.dart`): a `MediaTile` as wide as its card, wearing its own batch's provenance strip (a take is found by its name, so its record is its own batch's), identifier `take:<take stem>`, opening the viewer; beneath it the caption as before — the deliverable and the take's number, the *picked* chip, the version it was made from, the batch chip (`take:<take stem>.batch`) that opens the batch, the renderer and the file's name.
 - **Media still in the cloud** (§6.14). A media tile whose file the sync left on its target (`MediaFetch.isRemote`) paints the kind's stock picture with a small cloud mark below it, and its tooltip and label add *In the cloud; opening it downloads it.*; while the file downloads the mark is a ring with the download's progress. A tile never downloads. The viewer opened on such a file says *Downloading…* with a progress bar, then shows the file; a failed download says *The file could not be downloaded ({code}).* It logs `media.viewer.open {ref, film, path, kind, status and reason (the status box's code and its first change's kind, where the caller gives one), stage: fetch | open, outcome: start | ok | failed | closed, code or type, ms, totalMs}` — each stage's start before its wait, and `closed` where the dialog was closed before the file opened — digests only, the same `film` and `path` as the download's `media.ensure.*` lines, `ms` the stage's own time. A tile follows its file: once the file is here it is drawn as any other. The epoch card, the outfit row and a mention's picture show the stock picture for a file still in the cloud.
 - **View / Edit** (first, for every entity type; `tabs/entity_form.dart`) — **one presentation of the file's data in two modes**: *Edit* on a version the viewer may write, *View* on any other, the same fields in the same order either way. The form renders **sections → fields**, a field being the JSON keys it owns, a preferred width and a builder taking a `FormScope` (the file, the draft, whether input is accepted, whether the column is narrow, a text controller by key, a redraw, and `lock`, which hands a locked box `lockedDecoration` — the one locked look, shared by the script editor's boxes, a character's relationships and an outfit's garments); nothing in the layout names an entity type, so a type is added by supplying a sections function. A section's title row carries its badge, the AI icon where the assistant's catalog offers an action on the form section (§6.11a, the dock), and the title; the first card's row carries the file menu and the save status at its right, and a card with an action that too; a row with an action, the status or the AI icon wraps, what stands at its right going, each at its own width, to a line of its own where the title leaves it no room. The project, the character, the location, the outfit, the prop, the folder and the scene have forms (`tabs/project_edit_tab.dart`, `tabs/character_edit_tab.dart`, `tabs/location_edit_tab.dart`, `outfit_edit_tab.dart`, `prop_edit_tab.dart`, `folder_edit_tab.dart`, `scene_edit_tab.dart`); every other type shows the **generic section** of `tabs/generic_view_tab.dart` — one row per dotted leaf path of the file (`filmopen_format/flat_json.dart`, the rows Compare uses; a scene's or an episode's story `epoch` among them, since the filename carries none — `nonValueKeysFor(type)`), label above value with the raw key on hover, values rendered as the attribute view renders them, read-only until a type has a form of its own. (The rows of a scene's blocks, one per block id and labelled by place, are `blockRowsSections`, which a comparison draws; on a scene's page the *Dialog and actions* card is the editor.) Whatever no field names is shown read-only under *Other attributes*: a trailing card of its own, absent when nothing is left — or, on a project, a scene, a character, a location, an outfit and a prop, a heading in *Other* (below). No sentence stands above the cards in either mode (and): the tab's title says *View* or *Edit*. Then the cards about the file rather than in it: *Notes* when the file has one and *File* (path, `filmopen`, the header dates, and `by` / `source` / `license` / `x` when present); a red card listing header/filename disagreements stands above the cards. **On a project, a scene, a character, a location, an outfit and a prop what no field names and the file's facts fold into one last card, *Other*** (`EntityDataSpec.foldsOther`, `filmopen_forms/other_section.dart`, drawn by `filmopen_ui`'s `DisclosureCard`): its title row opens and closes it — one button to the semantics with its expanded state (`form.other`), taking Enter and Space — and it holds the sections the form folds, each under its title as a subsection — on the project *Release* and the treatment's *Camera*, *Look*, *Light and composition* and *Grading* — then *Other attributes*, where anything is left, and *File*, as headings. **It is closed on every entry to a version** — any navigation closes it (`otherOpenProvider`: the version's stem and the entry it was opened on, compared by identity where the card is drawn, so that a navigation made while the page is offstage closes it too) — and left as the person left it by an edit and its save, a re-read, a change of theme or of the window's size. *Notes*, words a person wrote, stays a card of its own, and nothing that warns is ever in *Other*: a refused save, a field's error, what the page may not do and the red card stand where they stood. A folder and a type with no form keep both cards, and Compare folds nothing. A section may carry **an action on its own card's title row** (`FormSectionSpec.action`), at its right at its own width, on a line of its own below the title on a narrow card; the form draws it and Compare's columns do not. A **locked** field is read-only rather than disabled, so its text stays at full strength: it wears `AppColors.locked`, floats its label, shows an em dash where it is empty, and drops the author's validation errors, which only the author can answer; a locked choice is drawn as the box a pull-down leaves behind, and a chip inside a locked box carries an outline. On the first card's title row, at its right — no row of its own, and a 28 × 20 px button that leaves the title row as tall as its title — an overflow menu (`detail/entity_file_menu.dart`) holds *Edit JSON…* on the viewer's own file (`tabs/json_editor_dialog.dart`: it opens on a tree of the file, `voo_json_tree` with `JsonTreeConfig.full`, where a long value keeps to its row, cut short with an ellipsis, and a tapped value is asked for, whole, and written into the document — a number, `true`, `false` or `null` typed where one stood stays one, anything else is text, and a path two keys would spell alike changes nothing — and a *Tree / Text* switch shows the whole file as text for adding or removing keys, back to the tree only when the text parses; both edit the one text an explicit *Save* writes, a duplicate key refused before anything is written, the identity fields refused by the writer, a changed-on-disk file refused, a confirm before discarding changes — the form's pending draft is written first, so the editor never opens on text the form has not saved), *Show in folder* (disabled with an explanation when the file is not on disk; absent on the web) and *Copy JSON*, which copies exactly what the writer would write. Fork is in the version row above the tabs, not in the tab. Every form autosaves through `tabs/draft_saver.dart`: a working copy of the file's JSON — a deep copy, as is every snapshot written, so nothing done to a nested object reaches the index — written 700 ms after the last change, one write at a time; the file the writer hands back is the base of the next write, taken as it comes with the times it stamped, so the next write is checked against the text this draft wrote; a saver is for one file: when the form moves to another file it gets a saver of its own and the old one is disposed, and a change typed while a write is in flight is written once, when that write returns, against the text it handed back — however many files the form passed through meanwhile, each keeps its own saver and its own typing (a write that fails after the form has gone is logged with what the draft held, having no form to show it or offer *Retry*, and is not tried again; §14); `settle` waits for a write in flight and writes what is pending, and **every fork settles every open draft first** (`DraftSaver.settleOpen`, from `WriteActions.fork`, which also waits for the drafts forms left behind when they moved on or went away), so a fork carries what was typed a moment ago, and a draft that cannot be written stops it with a message saying so; flushed when the page is left, when the form moves to another file and when the app loses focus; a keystroke during a write keeps the draft dirty (a change counter); a re-read of the same file — a new index, never the page drawn again over the same one, which may not yet hold the draft's own landed write — is ignored while the draft is dirty or writing, so an outside change is refused as stale rather than overwritten, and, while it is clean, gives the draft its times where only the times differ and re-seeds the boxes otherwise; a draft whose write landed while its publication failed takes the re-read that shows that write, the times it stamped included (§7.5); a status on the first card's title row that shows **only a failed write** — the error, its sentence wrapping rather than cut, with *Retry*, or *Discard my changes and re-read* when the file changed on disk, beside it where both fit whole, else under it, the sentence keeping its width: below `Breakpoints.singleColumn` (560 px) of the status's own room — and nothing while the draft is saved, unsaved or saving. **Closing the window writes every open draft first** (`lib/app.dart`'s `DraftsBeforeExit`: an `AppLifecycleListener` whose `onExitRequested` waits for `OpenDrafts.settleOpen`, at most 10 seconds, then lets the app exit, logging `app.exitRequested`). A change that leaves the draft as it was — an empty box cleared, the same kind chosen again — is not written: the draft is compared with the file where it would be written — after the 700 ms, on leaving the page, on moving to another file — never per keystroke. A change does not draw the form again: a box shows its own text, so a letter typed — in a field or in a scene's script — draws the form again only where the draft's state changes (its first letter) or in a comparison, whose copy controls answer the draft; a control drawn from the draft — a chip, a pull-down, a row, a change of a script's list of blocks — draws the form again on its own change; the epochs' editor draws only itself again, a letter of a label or a year included; a folder's form is drawn again when a letter sets a leaf of its treatment or empties one, since the boxes' inherited help follows which leaves it sets, and *Clear local overrides* draws only itself again when an edit — a leaf or the treatment's prompt — gives the folder a treatment or takes it away; and a box whose error follows its text (a number, the name, the age, a checked box) draws only itself again. Each write logs, at its start, the fields changed since the last write with their values and their count — a change that names no key, such as a script's keystroke or a copy, by the top-level keys where the draft and the file differ, the keyed ones among them — then, once, how it ended — written, with the values the writer handed back for those keys — the times it stamped included, later typing never — and whether it handed the file back (`handedBack`, with the `updated` it stamped; without a hand-back, the values the draft gave the writer), or unchanged, no writer called — and its time; the autosave logs nothing per keystroke. The writer's own checks apply to every form: a name is required (an empty box is an error, not a removal), a version label is at most 32 characters, a project keeps at least one epoch. The **character form** (`tabs/character_edit_tab.dart`) owns every key of A.1 that is typed rather than generated, in eight sections and three Prompt cards in its media card, each with a small icon badge on its title row (`FormSectionSpec.icon`, drawn by `SectionCard.icon`): **General** — name, kind (A.1), *Birth date* (`birth_date`) accepts `YYYY` or `YYYY-MM-DD`; empty removes it, while malformed text stays in its box with an inline error and changes no draft value. A valid nonempty date fills numeric `appearance.age` from its birth year and the era's year when possible, otherwise keeps age with the helper's reason. *Age* is one free box: 1–3 digits write a whole number, exactly 4 move the year to Birth date and fill numeric age, other text stays a phrase as typed (`"31 (1999) "` stays a phrase), and empty removes age. On a fill, both boxes show their written values and Age is read-only in `AppColors.locked`, with the tooltip *From the birth date and the era's year*. This lock remembers only a fill in this form session and for this stem; opening an existing matching date and age does not lock it. A kept fill or an emptied birth date clears the lock. It is the person's lock: an agent's field setter can still set age. A four-digit year with no era year or after it stays in the Age box with its inline error; Birth date is still written and seeded, and the draft's age is restored from the value held when Age gained focus (the file's age when it never gained focus), even if partial digits were autosaved. The hint names the era as the tree does. `form.birth_date` logs start, set or refusal, text, stem, epoch/year, decision/reason, before/after ages and elapsed time; an unexpected failure records its stage, error and stack and is rethrown. A refused Age year also records the restoration's values —, summary, aliases (comma-separated), `default_outfit` (a file's `arc`, which A.1 no longer names, is shown under *Other attributes* as any unknown key); **Features** (a face) — `appearance.face`, the four keys of `hair`, the three of `eyes`, `skin`, `facial_hair`, `teeth`, `glasses`; **Body** — `gender`, `species`, `ethnicity`, `height_cm` and `weight_kg` (numbers), `build`, `posture`, `gait`, `hands`, `jewellery`, `marks` (one per line), `appearance.notes`; **Personality** — `summary`, `traits` and `mannerisms` (one per line), `wants`, `needs`, `fears`, `speech`; **Voice** — `description`, `pitch` and `pace` as chips of A.1's vocabulary, `timbre`, `accent`, `lang` — what its previews say, `audition_text`, stands on the voice's Prompt card above *Voice samples*, under what the voice is designed from, so that the card holds a voice's prompt as a picture's card holds its prompt, in View/Edit and in Workflow alike; **Relationships** — one row per `{character, relation}` with a remove button and *Add relationship* (one empty row at a time; a row keeps any other key its object holds; a row emptied while it is retyped stays on screen, with its focus, until its × removes it, and the file leaves it out); then **Creator notes**, `creator_notes`, a section of its own on all four forms (section `creator_notes`).
