A box of a prompt's words takes away the prompt's writer — `plugin` and `writer` — where the words change (`pathBoxSetter`, through `promptFields`; the rule is `withPromptWordChanged`, `filmopen_prompt_ui`'s, which a block's frame prompt and the render dialog's card use too): a person's words are no writer's, and the writer stamps the prompt's `created`. **Each prompt stands above the place it renders**, in the media card, under the place's title and above its tiles (`MediaPlace.header`): on a character, a location, an outfit and a prop the **Reference prompt** card — `prompts.reference.positive`, `.negative` (section `prompt`) — above *Reference images*, the **Sheet prompt** card — `prompts.sheet` (section `prompt.sheet`) — above its sheets, and on a character the **Voice prompt** card — `prompts.voice` (section `prompt.voice`) — above *Voice samples*, its voice panel under them; inspirations, motion clips and a cover have none (`placePromptKind`, the places table's prompt column). The form hands the card its prompt sections by kind (`referenceMediaSection`'s `prompts`); each is `compareOnly` — drawn as no card of its own, its fields still in the form's field index and covered keys — and a comparison draws no card in the media card (`FormScope.comparing`) and pairs each prompt's boxes as rows where its section stands, with their `<`. **A prompt's section is its Prompt card** (`promptSection`, `filmopen_forms/form_fields.dart`; the card, `PromptCard`, is `filmopen_prompt_ui`'s): a `FormSectionSpec` whose `body` draws the card in place of the section's own card, handed the section's built boxes with their identifiers — prose boxes whose `@` offers the media a prompt of the card's kind may name, not the film's entities (§9.8) —, so the card owns the section's whole frame — its one header, its border, its collapse — while a comparison reads the section's fields as any other's. The card reads the prompt from the form's draft (in *View*, the file as read) and paints its state against its holder (Project Specification §9.0, `promptStateOf`): **empty** — expanded, the plain outline; **current** — collapsed to one line: the icon, the card's title (*Reference prompt*, *Sheet prompt*, *Voice prompt*, *Treatment prompt*, *First-frame prompt*, *Last-frame prompt*), *written by* the writer's name where `plugin` is set, found by its pair, `plugin` and `writer` (the plug-in's tag where no writer offered has the pair) else the version's author, and how long ago in whole minutes, hours or days (*just now* under a minute), then the arrow that opens it; its border is a current image's of the stage (the document's `render_stage`, a scene's for a block's prompt; `stageBorder`, `filmopen_media_ui`: final green, draft green and dashed, prototype blue, from `render_stage`, absent being prototype); **outdated** — expanded, red. Turning outdated or empty opens a card; open, its header shows who wrote it and when whole, wrapping as it needs. A `created` that is not a string, or not an instant, is no time: the prompt is outdated. Where the form takes input, the card's **top row** is the **Writer** pull-down — every enabled plug-in's writers of the card's target (`promptWriters`; `fo-prompts`'s first), starting on the writer *Render* would ask (`startingPromptWriter`: the first stock `fo-prompts` writer of kind `llm` with a text model reachable, else Basic) where the card offers it, else on the first offered, until the person chooses another, which stays chosen across a change of model (`prompt.writer.default`: the place, the writer, its model, rate and quote, `why`: `model`, `noLlm` or `notOffered`; the card's own *Generate* is a paid model call where it started on that model's writer; a card on screen keeps the writer it is on while that is still offered, chosen or not, so that a key or a model coming or going meanwhile changes its default only when the card is next built; *Render* itself does not read the card's choice), each by its name, *<plug-in> / <label>* where two plug-ins offer writers, chosen by its pair, *No writer for this prompt* where none — with, **under it for an LLM writer, one line**: its text model and rate (*GPT-5.6 Sol · rate $4.00/1M in, $20.00/1M out*), the model with *rate unknown* where its platform states no plain rate, or *Text model unknown* where none is reachable; nothing for a template writer — and **Generate**, with the AI icon; held, with its reason as its tooltip, where there is no writer or while one runs. **The card listens to the host's changes** (`PluginInvocation.changes`: a key, a credit answer, a change of *Use the LLM:*, a plug-in turned on or off) and reads the writers again, their models and rates, keeping the writer it is on where that is still offered, else the one it would start on. Where the card's caller gives a catalogue model (`model`), the writers offered and Generate are that model's: a change of model reads the writers again, keeping the writer it is on where that is still offered; where its caller gives the blocks a clip covers (`covered`), Generate hands them to the writer. A page's cards give neither. The render dialog's card gives the model chosen — its catalogue entry, never its way, so a change of way within the entry asks the writer nothing again — and, at a clip over several blocks, the blocks after the owning one, in order: one prompt, on the clip's own block, written over the blocks it covers, its state the ordinary state of that block. **Generate** asks the writer from the draft as it stands, typing included (an `Entity` of the file's identity and the draft's snapshot), never the file the page opened on, handing it the media tokens the prompt may name ( the card's `tokens`, asked of its owner once as Generate starts): the section computes them from the draft for the open film and the viewer and the frame card from its scene's film (`promptWriterTokens`, over `promptTokenCandidates`), each as written with the name its chip shows — *inspiration 1*, *reference 1*, *Kira Voss · sheet 1* — and the voice's and the treatment's hand none; then settles the draft, and where its save was refused drops the answer (*Not generated: the page could not be saved.*); then, in one change through the draft, sets `positive`, `negative` (removed where the writer gave none) and the writer's pair, `plugin` and `writer`, and removes `created`, which the writer stamps with the holder's time, so the prompt is current and the card collapses. **A page left while the writer answers or the draft settles** — the card gone, or its draft no longer the one open on its file — drops the answer: nothing is applied and nothing is reported written (`prompt.generate.refused {code: pageLeft, stage}`); *written* is logged only once the change went through the draft without a refusal. A writer's `no-prompt` changes nothing and says so (*Nothing generated: the writer had no prompt.*); a failure says its code (*Not generated (…).*), and a writer refused for want of a key, credit or a server (`credentials`, or `noProvider` for `credentials`, `noPlugin` or no reason; a chosen model the catalogue no longer has keeps its code) says so (*No text model is available — add a key, credit or a local server in Settings.*). One Generate at a time: the button is held while one runs. In *View*, or where the form takes no input, the card shows the prompt read-only with *Read-only: edit your own version to write this prompt.* and no top row. The card's doors are `prompt:<stem>:<path>:toggle`, `:writer`, `:llm` (the line under the pull-down) and `:generate` (a block's path being `<id>.prompts.<kind>`); a collapsed card does not draw its boxes, which an agent's `set_field` still types into through the form's fields. It logs, each line with the kind, the stem, the block's id and the model as they are — the lines of a Generate the model its request was made for, whatever is chosen meanwhile — and each writer by its pair (`writer` the plug-in's tag, `writerId` its id): `prompt.state {kind, state, was, reason}` when the state it shows changes (held while Generate applies, so no outdated is shown between the words and their stamp), `prompt.toggled`, `prompt.writer.chosen {writer, writerId, writerName, was, wasId, reason: person}`, `prompt.model.changed {writers, writer, writerId, was, wasId, kept}`, `prompt.card.relisted {cause, writers, writer, writerId, was, wasId, kept, reason}` (each writer with its model and rate; `stillOffered`, `notOffered` or `noneChosen`), `prompt.card.llm {writer, writerId, kind, llm, llmName, rate, shown, why}` (what the line under the pull-down says, and whether it was read, chosen, relisted or read for another model), `prompt.generate.start {writer, writerId, writerName}`, `.tokens {count, tokens}` (each token handed and its name, once the owner is asked), `.settled {writer, writerId, writerName, positive, negative, clean, refused}`, `.done` or `.refused {writer, writerId, writerName, outcome, code, reason, line, stage, positive, negative, ms}` (the stage where the page was left; the writer's words wherever it answered some) and `.failed {writer, writerId, stage, cause, message, ms}`; a box's hand edit `prompt.pluginCleared {stem, path, plugin, writer, text}`. What is generated or chosen from media — `media`, `picks`, `preview`, `voice.provider_bindings` — stays in *Other attributes*. The **outfit form** (`outfit_edit_tab.dart`, Project Specification A.2) opens on *General* — Name, Description (`description`, the outfit as a whole, multiline), Era, Footwear, Condition, A.2's seven tokens as chips (a tap on the chosen one removes it), and the chosen sheet —, then the media card, *Garments* as one group of boxes per garment (item, colour, material, fit, pattern, condition — prose here, not the outfit's enum — and notes) with *Add garment* (disabled, saying why, while an empty garment is on screen) and a remove button each, a garment keeping the keys the form does not draw, a value of another shape in it shown locked and left alone, and one with nothing typed not written — an agent fills a garment box by box (`garments:<stem>:<n>.<key>`), `garments` taking no `set_field`, *Accessories and palette* (one per line; a palette takes hex or names), its *Reference prompt* and *Sheet prompt* cards in the media card, and *Creator notes*; an outfit has no voice. The **prop form** (`prop_edit_tab.dart`, A.3) opens on *General* — Name, Kind as A.3's ten chips in A.3's order (optional), *Hero prop* as a switch writing `true` or `false`, Description, Scale, Material, Condition, which is prose, and the chosen sheet —, then the media card, with its *Reference prompt* and *Sheet prompt* cards (a prop's sheets render from its `prompts.sheet`), *Handling and sound* and *Creator notes*. Their prose boxes are plain text boxes with the microphone; the **chosen sheet** (*Chosen sheet*, `picks.sheet`, `sheetPickField`) is chips over the version's own sheets (*Outfit sheets*, *Prop sheets*), each named as its tile is — one chosen writes a reference, several a list in the place's order, none no key; a chosen reference the place no longer holds stays chosen whichever door sets the field — a chip, or an agent typing references, which one rule serves; a typed reference the place does not hold is not chosen, so a sheet is uploaded before it is chosen —, in the locked look, named by its file, its tooltip giving it whole and saying the JSON editor removes it, never dropped; with no sheet yet the field says inside its box *Add a picture to … to choose the sheet*; a value of another shape gets no field and shows in *Other attributes*, read-only; neither form has a plug-in card. The **location form** (`tabs/location_edit_tab.dart`) does the same for A.3 in eight sections: **General** — name, kind (a pull-down of interior / exterior / both), **Located within** (`within`, Project Specification 1.9 §9.3: a pull-down of the project's other locations by name, *None* removing the key; a tag no other location has — its own included — is kept, offered as an entry and said under the box, as every pull-down of a token says it (above); the names are the viewer's version in use, as in the tree; an agent types a tag, and one that is not another location's writes nothing; purely informational — the tree, the category page and resolution ignore it), aliases, `description`, `era`, `architecture`, `areas` (one per line); **Geography** — `city`, `region`, `country`, `coordinates`, `access`; **Time and weather** — `time_of_day` as chips of A.5's vocabulary, `weather`; **Dressing and palette** — `dressing`, `palette` (one per line); **Lighting** — `default`, `practicals` (one per line), `windows`; **Sound** — `room_tone`, `ambience`; **Creator notes** — and its **Reference prompt** and **Sheet prompt** cards in the media card, as the character's. A box inside an open object is labelled without the object its section names (*Hair · Colour*, through `flatPathLabel`). The boxes on dotted paths are `tabs/form_fields.dart`'s: a type's form names each path once with what it holds (`PathBoxes`: text, number, lines; a character's, a location's, an outfit's and a prop's maps are the format's `entity_fields.dart`, which the assistant's context reads too, §6.11a), which gives the seed and the box its kind — a field the sections draw after the seed, a plug-in's card arriving while the form is open, is seeded from the file when it is first drawn, every other box kept as typed —, and each box's `onChanged` and its `FormFieldSpec.set` are one function, so an agent's `setField` on `appearance.hair.color` or on a chip's key (typing the token) writes as a person does. The rules are pure Dart in `filmopen_format/field_paths.dart`: a box starts with a string as it is, a number as its digits, a list one item per line; typing writes the trimmed text, a number or nothing (text that is not a number writes nothing), lines without the blank ones, creating the objects on the way and copying every container it descends; **an emptied box removes its key and every object the removal leaves empty** (and clearing what was not there changes nothing); and **a box never writes over a value of another shape** (`pathFits`: an object on the way that is not one, an object or a list where a word goes, a list holding an object) — such a field is drawn locked in *Edit* too (`FormScope.lock(locked:)`), with *The file holds this value in another shape; change it with Edit JSON* — the value itself, where the path reaches one (an object where a word goes), rendered in the field's place (`ShapeLocked`), and an object on the way that is not one (`appearance` written as a sentence) shown in *Other attributes*. The age box follows the same rule: it never puts an age into an `appearance` that is not an object. The boxes carry no prefix icon (an icon moves the label off the column the others share and makes the box taller), and their widths are shares of a four-column grid (`FormFieldSpec.width` at 1 and below: `0.25` a column, `0.5` two, `0.75` three; two columns where one would be under 150 px), so a section's boxes line up and end at the row's right edge. A chip row shows a token the vocabulary does not know as a chosen chip of its own; tapping the chosen chip removes the key; locked, it is the chosen word in a `LockedValue`. Compare pairs the new sections by id (`general`, `features`, `body`, `personality`, `voice`, `prompt.voice`, `relationships`, `prompt`, `prompt.sheet`, `creator_notes`; `general`, `geography`, `time`, `dressing`, `lighting`, `sound`, `prompt`, `prompt.sheet`, `creator_notes`), draws them without the icon badges, and copies each box's path as it copies any row. The **project form** (`tabs/project_edit_tab.dart`), in its cards' order: **General** — the title on its own row; then **Type of project** (the key `kind`, so labelled on this form alone: a pull-down of the four words and *None*, narrowed to a quarter of the row where the row holds four boxes, and without a helper; a word for people that chooses nothing, so `children` is untouched — *The project's kind*, above), **Aspect ratio** (`format.aspect`: *16:9 wide screen*, *9:16 cell phone*, *1:1 square*, *4:5 social media*, *3:4 analog TV*, each after an outlined rectangle drawn in its ratio, writing the ratio as a string) and **Resolution** (`format.resolution`: `480p`, `720p`, `1080p`, `1440p`, `4k`, written as shown), both keeping the rest of `format` and showing a value the list does not have as itself; **Subtitle font** (`format.subtitles.font`: *Noto Sans*, *Noto Sans Bold*, *Noto Serif*, *Noto Serif Bold*, the faces bundled under `assets/fonts/`, *Default (Noto Sans)* removing the key) and **Subtitle size** (`format.subtitles.size`, pixels at the output's height: a whole number above zero written as a number, an empty box removing the key — the default, 48 —, anything else refused under the box with the old value kept), each one setter for a person's choice or typing and an agent's `set_field`. The font, a gesture, logs `project.subtitles.font.start` `{value as entered, old}`, then `project.subtitles.font` `{old, new, outcome, ms}` or `.refused {value, reason: shape | not_listed, old, ms}`. The size logs nothing per letter: a size taken is named by the write that carries it (`draft.save.start`'s `fields`), and a text refused is logged once, `project.subtitles.size.refused {value, reason: shape | not_whole | not_positive | too_large, old, ms}` — when the person leaves the box, or at once for an agent's `set_field`. A token pull-down's choice goes through the field's own setter where it has one (Kind's, the subtitle font's), so a person's choice and an agent's typing are one operation; then **the *Style*** (below, under the visual treatment) under the type of project, and genre (`filmopen_ui/genre_picker.dart`: a pull-down of check boxes over the recommended list plus whatever the file names, shown as chips) beside it. Where the pane holds two boxes to a row and not four they pair in that order, and below 560 px of pane they stand one to a row; **Synopsis** — logline and synopsis; **Epochs**, the line *Time period(s) in the film, such as present day, 17th century, 2030, future* under its title (`FormSectionSpec.subtitle`, drawn by `SectionCard.subtitle`, which Compare's columns do not draw), over the epochs (`tabs/epochs_editor.dart`: a reorderable list with a drag handle, label and year boxes — in a card narrower than a form's single column, the label beside the handle and the remove button and the year under it — no explanation above them and no token chip or *default* badge, item 12 — a remove button disabled for an epoch that files use — by filename, by a scene's story epoch or `location_epoch`, or by a cast or props entry (`FilmProject.epochUsage`) — or the last one; a label box cleared to blank is refused in place, the last label kept and *An epoch needs a label* under it, and the year is optional. Reordering and editing labels and years are changes of the draft; **adding is a write of its own**: *Add epoch* (`epochs.add`) opens a dialog asking for a label, required, and a year, optional — no token, which the writer allocates (`ProjectWriter.declareEpoch`) — and declares on **the project version in use**, through `WriteActions.declareEpoch` and `ProjectController.declareEpoch`. On the viewer's own version in use it is saved in that file, which the clean form adopts; on another author's version in use the dialog says *Adding an epoch makes your own version of the project*, and the writer forks it and picks the fork, to which the page moves, saying so; a fork whose pick failed still declares the epoch, and the page moves to it and says it is not yet the version in use, whose check picks it. A project pick or another handle meanwhile refuses with the changed-binding sentence. The dialog logs `epoch.add.start {label, yearText, year}` and `epoch.add {label, year, outcome: added | refused | failed, refusal or error, pageOpen, ms}` (a missing label `refused`, `no-label`). Each row's label box takes every re-read of the file, its own echoes apart, and a reorder writes `order` alone. *Add epoch* is disabled, saying why, on a version that is not the one in use and where `PageAccess.canCreateVersions` does not hold. The dialog stays, busy, through the declaration and its re-read, and shows a refusal inside it: the film, the viewer and the version in use are taken at the click and checked again in the project's commentary queue (`ProjectController.declareEpoch`), so a project pick, another handle or another film meanwhile refuses rather than declaring on a version the person did not see); the media card; the treatment's Prompt card; and, folded into *Other*, **Release** — year, rating — and the rest of the visual treatment in subsections (below). A change is saved 700 ms after the last keystroke, unless the draft then holds what the file holds; a failed write shows its error on the first card's title row; a pending change is written when the page is left. An official version shows a banner offering Fork. On somebody else's file, or a read-only project, the tab says so and offers Fork.
 - **Script** (a scene, a folder, and the Script page's second tab) — read-only (`filmopen_browser/script_tab.dart`). **A scene** is its blocks as a screenplay page (`filmopen_ui/screenplay_view.dart`, each block through the format's `ScriptBlock`), set against a US Letter page's six inches of text: the action across the full width, the speaker's cue in capitals at its indent — with Fountain's `(O.S.)`, `(V.O.)` or `(dual)` from the head of the direction on the cue's line — the rest of the direction in parentheses under it, bracketed once (a direction that carries its own brackets keeps them), the dialogue in its narrower column, left aligned, and a transition (`screenplay_element: transition`) at the right margin. **A mention** in the words is read through the reference codec's tokens (§5): a character the project has shows its name, a sentence's full stop after it staying the sentence's; a media reference such as `@ch_kira:character_sheet-1` is one token and left whole, as is a mention of anyone or anything else. An empty block draws nothing, and an object the format does not read as a block shows as its JSON, muted (an entry that is no object at all is not drawn: the page reads the scene's objects). **No block id is drawn**: an id is the file's address for a block and means nothing to a person. **The reading page is a screenplay**: it shows dialogue, action and directions, and **not a block's subtitles or beats**, which stay in the editor and the timeline; reading writes nothing, so they are kept as they are. **A folder and the whole film** read every scene beneath them in the script's order, depth first, through `folderOutline` (§5.12) — the version on screen's own children, not the version in use's — and the Script page's walk, as a **Page** (each scene's slug line in capitals with its number at both margins, a rule between scenes; a lazy list, so only the headings, rules and blocks on screen are built) or a **Scene grid** (`filmopen_ui/scene_grid.dart`: one card per scene, `SceneCard`'s facts read from its file — number, slug line, its first words with their mentions read as on the page, `INT`/`EXT` from the slug line's head, the file's `time`, the source's page eighths, up to four of the cast and the count of elements — as many columns as `Breakpoints.sceneCard` allows, at most six, a card opening its scene, `script.card.<stem>`; a lazy list of the sections' headings and rows of cards, so only those on screen are built, an agent reaching a card off screen by scrolling) or a **Timeline** (`filmopen_ui/script_timeline.dart`: the reading view's strip, not a player — each scene a block in order, as wide as its imported page length in eighths, `TimelineLayout` working the widths out once for the strip and every lane; the scale fills the strip's room (the pane less its padding and the lanes' names) or keeps the lightest scene 3 px wide, whichever is larger, and a strip wider than its room scrolls sideways; where some lengths are unknown each of those takes the median known length, marked beneath and said *length unknown* in its tooltip; where none is known every scene takes an equal width, and a line above the strip says which rule holds — *Widths by page length, eighths*, the same with how many scenes of unknown length take the median width, or *Equal widths: no page lengths*; nothing is inferred from dialogue or seconds; under the strip a lane for each of the eight characters in most scenes shown, keyed by tag and labelled by name, the tag in its tooltip, so two characters of one name are two lanes; the blocks coloured by the grid's stripe, a missing fact neutral and said *unknown* in the tooltip, or by a hue for each location's tag; under either, the tooltip names each block's location — resolved as the scene's own reference, at its `location_epoch` or its epoch and official for an official scene, its tag where that resolves to nothing, never another epoch's name — or says *location unknown*, so the colour is never the only sign; a block opening its scene, `script.timeline.<stem>`, the colourings `script.timeline.colouring.board` and `.location`), chosen with `script.mode` — below 560 px of pane the switch shows its icons, each word a tooltip and the icon's semantic label, so a segment is chosen by its name (`tap {"label": "Timeline"}`) at any width. The choice, and the timeline's colouring, are the reader's (`scriptViewProvider`, §7): kept from one folder or tab to another and across a re-read, back to *Page* and the grid's stripe when another film opens. **Filters** (`filmopen_browser/script_filters.dart`), under the switch, narrow a folder's and the whole film's scenes, never a scene opened by itself, which is always shown whole and carries no filters — its folder keeps them for the reader's return: the chips `INT`, `EXT`, `DAY` and `NIGHT` (`script.filter.int` … `.night`; of a pair, neither asks nothing and both ask for either; a scene whose file gives no night time counts as day), a character of the scene's cast, a location, and a prop or outfit of the film's (the pull-down's), matched against what the scene names in its `props`, `outfits` and `resources` — as a tag or as a map naming its tag, `{"prop": "chart", "epoch": "default"}`, a list of another shape naming nothing (`script.filter.character`, `.location`, `.resource`; a tag the film no longer has stays the filter, shown by its tag) — and **the search** (`script.filter.search`), which looks in the words the page draws for a scene: its slug line and each block's speaker, direction, dialogue and action, mentions read as the page reads them, so a character's name finds the scenes that mention them or where they speak. Every filter set must hold. While anything narrows, the bar says how many scenes are shown, with *Clear* (`script.filter.clear`), which empties every filter and the box; where nothing is left, the page says so. The filters are kept while the reader moves about the film and are never written; another film starts with none. A scene the filters hide is left out before the sections are reckoned, so a section starts against the scene shown before it. **Sections**: where the folders a scene is in differ from the scene's before it — told apart by their stems, so two sibling folders of one name are two sections — a section starts, headed by the folders' kinds and names from the outermost (`Act: The voyage  >  Sequence: The storm`); a scene back at the top after a folder starts an untitled section, ruled, never the folder's. A cycle, an unresolved entry and a second listing are not read again; **the recycle bin is not read** (§7): a deleted scene is skipped, and what a deleted folder holds stands in its place, its own sections only. **The stripe** of a card is one rule (`AppColors.sceneStripe`): interior or exterior, by day or by night, `INT/EXT` striped as an exterior — a presentation default — and a card whose slug line says neither, or whose file gives no `time`, neutral: a missing fact is shown unknown, never guessed; the card's words say what the colour says. **The log is one line per act, its per-scene values as lists in it**: each list an act gathers from its scenes is a key ending in `_list`, and a count keeps its name; the walk's, a narrowing's and the search's lines each carry the speakers and locations resolved while they ran (`speaker_list`, `location_list`), empty where none was. Each open logs, once and never on a redraw, `script.projection` (the scope, the stem, the rows walked, the scenes read and what was skipped and why), carrying each row's decision (`row_list`), every distinct mention with what it was read as and why (`mention_list`) and the speakers it resolved for them (`speaker_list`); every speaker of each scene is logged once with the version and name it resolved to, in the `speaker_list` of the search, the walk or the narrowing that resolved it, and one resolved while the page is drawn (on the Page, once its block is first drawn) as a line of its own, `script.speaker`, as a mention decided outside the walk is `script.mention`; and `script.page.open`, `script.grid.open` or `script.timeline.open`; each change of mode logs `script.mode`, and each card or block chosen `script.card.open` (`via` the grid or the timeline). The timeline logs its geometry so that the strip can be rebuilt from the log — `script.timeline.layout.start` (the scope, the stem, the scenes, the pane's and the strip's pixels), then `script.timeline.layout` (the rule, the median, the scale, the width, whether it scrolls, the time) with each scene in `scene_list` (its length as imported, its eighths, its width, and whether the width is its own, the median or an equal share) — for each reading, a filter's included (a keystroke's search excepted: its geometry and lanes are logged once it settles, where the timeline is drawn) and one that leaves no scene (`scenes: 0`, the rule `empty`, no width), and laid out once per reading and room; a resize logs one `script.timeline.resize` (the pane, the room, the rule, the scale, the width, whether it scrolls) once no new room has been laid out for 300 ms; its lanes `script.timeline.lanes` (each lane's tag, name and scenes, the characters left out, the time); each scene's location once an open, `script.timeline.location`, in a line of its own or an act's `location_list` as a speaker is (the scene, the tag, the epoch and whether the scene gave it, the version it resolved to, the name shown, `resolved` or `unresolved`, the time); an unexpected failure `script.timeline.failed` with its stage, thrown on. The rows are walked once per open and only narrowed when a filter changes. A narrowing for anything but a keystroke — *Clear* included — logs `script.filter.start` (the scope, the stem, the filters) and `script.filter.applied` (shown, hidden, the sections, the time) with each scene in `scene_list` (shown or not, and the first filter that failed with what it compared: `{filter: resource, wanted: chart, had: [...]}`), every section's start in `section_list` (from, to, `enter`, `leave` or `root`) and the speakers it resolved; each pull-down's entities are named once per film and viewer (`script.filter.list`), an unexpected failure `script.filter.failed`; each change of a chip, a pull-down, the mode, the colouring or *Clear* logs `script.view` (what changed, to what, the mode, the colouring and the filters), and another film `script.view.reset`. A keystroke's narrowing logs nothing; the settled query logs one line, `script.search` (the query, where it was typed, the scenes it left and their stems, each scene hidden with the first filter that hid it and that filter's value, the time and why now), with the speakers — the grid's cast included — and the timeline's locations its keystrokes resolved (`speaker_list`, `location_list`) and — the first time an open or a re-read is searched — each scene searched in `scene_list` (shown or not, and the very words it was searched in): once typing stops for the autosave's 700 ms, and at once before *Clear*, a navigation or another film — never per keystroke; a search kept into a new reading, a re-read's or another folder's, is logged once that reading is drawn (`reason: reading`).
 - **Workflow** (a scene, a folder, and the Script page's third tab, after *Script*; `filmopen_browser/workflow_tab.dart`) — the chosen scriptwriting plug-in's steps from the version on screen down, read from the plug-in's workflow file (§6.11), each run from its row. A folder's and a scene's tab first names the plug-in (*Script Aid — change it on the project's Workflow tab*); the project's has the *Create Script* card (above). **What is shown where**: the focus's own sections first, with no heading of their own (the page names the focus) — on the project those of scope `project`, on a folder those of scope `folder` with no `kind` or the folder's `kind` as written, on a scene those of scope `scene` — then each folder and scene beneath (an entry of another type has no steps), in the script's order, headed as the Script tab heads a section (*Episode: The return*) or by the scene's name, with theirs; a folder with no section of its own still heads what is beneath it. The rows are read as the Script tab reads them, through the project's script or the folder's own `folderOutline` (§5.12): a cycle, a second listing and an unresolved row are skipped, a deleted row is not shown and a deleted folder's children stand in its place. A section's heading is sized by its `level` (1–3) with its help under it; the headings indent 12 px a depth, up to three; the plug-in's words — a heading, a help, a label — are its strings' keys, shown through the plug-in's strings where it has them. **A step's row** (`workflow.step:<stem>:<step>`): at the left a mark — lit where the step's *done* holds, unlit where not, none where the step has no *done* — read from the project (`workflowDoneProvider`, §7), never ticked, with its tooltip (*Done — read from the project*, *Not done yet*; `…:<step>.done`); the label with its help under it; at the right a chip with the model decided for the step and its rate (`…:<step>.model`: *Claude Opus 5 · rate $15/1M in, $75/1M out*, *… · rate unknown*, *… · set in Settings* where *Use the LLM:* chose it, *Text model unknown* with the reason in its tooltip), decided once per step id, not per row, before anything runs; a step that declares no model (`llms: []`) calls none and has no chip. Below 560 px of pane the chip and *Run* go under the text. **Run** (`…:<step>.run`) opens a dialog titled with the step (`workflow_run_dialog.dart`): *On <the focus>*, then a row per field of its action (`workflow.run.field:<name>`) — `text` a line, `note` a paragraph box (3–8 lines), `number` a box read as a number (an `int` where whole; a comma decimal is not read), `choice` a pull-down of its `choices` as written, `flag` a check, off until ticked — empty every time it opens; then the quote (`workflow.run.quote`): the step's estimate at the decided model's rate (*About 3 calls on Claude Opus 5 · about $2.25*, `DecidedModel.quote`), *… · rate unknown* without a rate, the model and its rate alone without an estimate, *No text model can be reached for this step.* without a model, and *No model call* for a step that declares `llms: []`; then the ceiling (`workflow.run.ceiling`: *Stops at $5.00.*, or *No ceiling — set one on the project's Workflow tab.*), left out where the step calls no model; *Cancel* and *Run* (`workflow.run.cancel`, `workflow.run.go`, 44 px). A required field left empty (*Fill this in.*) or a number that is not one (*Enter a number, like 5.*) is said under it and nothing runs; from then on each field is checked again as it changes. Logged: `workflow.dialog.opened` (the fields, the model, the estimate, the quote, the ceiling), `.refused` (what was typed, the missing and unread fields), `.submitted` (what was typed, and what is sent with the reason for each field: as typed, blank and left out, a whole number, a choice, a flag) and `.cancelled` (what was typed), each with its time. **The run** goes to the entity the row stands under — the page's own for the focus's sections, the folder or scene whose heading it sits beneath otherwise — as `{type, tag}`, with the action's `args` beneath the fields, and the ceiling (§6.11, `runOffer`); it is held while the app is open (`workflowRunsProvider`), not by the row, so it survives leaving the page, and a row's next run replaces its last; nothing is saved. While it runs the row shows a progress bar (`….progress`), the time so far (`m:ss`) and **Stop** (`….stop`) in place of *Run*; every other step of that plug-in has *Run* disabled with *Another step of Script Aid is running: Write the scene.*, since a plug-in runs one call at a time. At its end the row shows what it spent and on which model (`….spent`: *$0.58 on Claude Opus 5*, *An amount not known on …*; not for a step that calls no model), the plug-in's text as written, selectable (`….text`; Markdown reads as plain text), a failure in the plug-in's words in the error colour (`….failure`; an answer the plug-in refused or failed among them, beside its spend; *Stopped* when stopped, *Stopped: this step reached its $5.00 ceiling.* when the ceiling refused a call after some spend, with the spend unknown, or where the call alone was within the ceiling; before any spend, for a call whose worst case alone passes the ceiling, *This step could cost up to $1.42 — more than its $1.00 ceiling. Raise the ceiling to run it.*; a model with no known price, *This model has no known price, so it cannot run under a ceiling. Clear the ceiling or choose another model.*; a call with no output limit, *The plug-in did not say how long its answer may be, so this step cannot run under a ceiling.*), kept until the row runs again, and after a done run **Show the script** (`….script`), which opens this page's own Script tab (`EntityTabs.show`), where an applied proposal is drawn. A proposal the call holds opens in the import review by itself, as every plug-in's does (below); the run reads no proposal. Logged: `workflow.step.start` (the arguments whole, the model, the estimate, the quote, the ceiling), `.stop`, `.done` (the spend, whether known, the model, the text), `.failed` (the code, reason and message; a throw from the runtime with its stage, type and error, at warning, shown as a failure) with the time, and `workflow.step.script` (the tab and whether it opened). A long script costs what is on screen: the lines are one lazy list. Each open, and each change of the chosen plug-in or of the host's plug-ins, logs `workflow.shown` once — the plug-in, the cause, the focus, the state shown (`shown`, `noWriters`, `noneChosen`, `missing`, `refused` or `empty` — *{plugin} has no steps here …*), every row whole (where, the step, done, the model, why, its rate and the reason where none was decided), the done rows and the time —; a draw logs nothing. **The media sections** (`filmopen_browser/workflow_media.dart`), below the steps — shown whether or not a plug-in is chosen and whether or not its read failed — gather the focus's scope (`workflowScopeProvider`, §7) as more lines of the same lazy list: **Characters**, **Locations**, **Props** and **Scenes**, each with its heading. A character, a location or a prop is a line per tag and occurrence epoch, headed *{name} at {epoch}* — the name the version in use itself carries, the epoch by its label —, and under it the version's `MediaFormCard` (§9.3) where the line is the first to resolve to that version; one that falls back says so (*Uses the 1990 version*); a later line resolving to the same version is its heading alone, *Mia at 2010 — uses Mia at 1990*, with no card, so that one version has one form on the screen. A character's outfits at that epoch follow it, indented, by the same rule. **Every character and location of the project is listed**, not only those the focus's scenes use: after the walk's lines, each other at every epoch with a version, the recycle bin's left out, the library's unless a scene uses them, and an epoch that resolves to nothing for the viewer (several owners, none official, none the viewer's) skipped and logged. One no scene of the project uses says *Not in any scene* under its title: any scene version counts, a recycled or superseded one too, and a scene naming an epoch with no version counts for no version; one used only outside the focus says nothing. They start unchecked like every place, and *Select all* takes those the viewer may write. An empty Characters or Locations section says the project has none yet, a recycled one counting as none. A line that would show only its heading, a version an earlier line's fallback already shows with no outfit at that epoch, is left out. A line that does not own its version but has outfits at the version's own epoch reads its plain title; one the walk reached with none reads *Kai at 1990 — uses Kai at 1990*, naming itself. Props stay those of the focus's scenes. **Scenes** heads each folder at its depth and draws each scene as its card. A section with nothing says what would fill it (*No props appear in the scenes below. …*). Each card is writable as its own page's `PageAccess` says. **The headings, frozen**: an entity's heading — a folder's and a scene's above their steps, a character's, a location's, a prop's or an outfit's above its card, a folder's among the Scenes — is ruled and titled as Compare titles an item, a rule, the type's icon and the name; a step's row is spaced as Compare's rows (8 px between its controls). Over the list's top, opaque, one row (`workflow.heading`) names the entity the topmost line stands under — a row's and its card's, a scene's card, a folder's — with its icon, and the next entity's name replaces it as that entity's first line reaches the top; it does not push the last one up. The focus's own steps, a section's heading and its empty state stand under none, and nothing is drawn there; a step's own heading scrolls. The row is found among the lines the list has laid out, never by building or measuring another, so the list stays as lazy as it was: on a film of 100 scenes of three blocks each, no card is mounted at the tab's top and two halfway down, before and after. Every media line is keyed by what it shows — a card its stem, an entity's heading its section, type, tag and occurrence epoch, a folder its stem, a section itself — and the list finds a line again by its key (`findChildIndexCallback`), so a dirty or failed card keeps its draft and its way out when the steps above it change. **The selection**: the tab holds one set of checked places under a `PlaceSelection` (§9.3); the places offered are computed from the scope, not from what is built — every named place of a card the viewer may write, and every named place of each block an editable scene's card draws (an id the scene gives twice names none) —, so that a place's checkbox appears where it is offered. A section with a place to check carries **Select all** and **Select none** on its heading (`workflow.<section>.all`, `.none`), which check or uncheck that section's places alone. The selection lives as long as the tab: a page of another focus starts with nothing checked. Logged: `workflow.places.shown` (every line with owner, fallback and access and, but for an outfit's, `inScene`, once per scope and access rules), `workflow.offered.start` and `workflow.offered` (each row's or block's decision with its reason — offered, not owner, not editable, id given twice, no id), `workflow.select.start` and `workflow.select` (the section, all or none, the keys and the selection), `workflow.selection.reset` (what was dropped and why); a throw in any is logged `….failed` with its stage and time and thrown on, the tab saying *The places could not be shown: …* in the sections' place. **Render** (`workflow_render.dart`): a button on a row of its own at the tab's bottom right, below the list, which does not scroll with it and covers none of it, *Render {n}* with the number of checked places (`workflow.render`), disabled with its reason while nothing is checked (*Check the places to render first.*) and while a press is under way (*Rendering…*) — one press at a time, claimed before its first wait, until it is cancelled, refused, failed, the pass answers, or the tab has gone. A press settles the open drafts first (one that cannot be written stops it with its own error, nothing prepared); then each checked picture or clip place whose **Generate** box on its Prompt card reads checked has its writer decided, the boxes read once before the first write and in the sections' order: the first stock `fo-prompts` writer of kind `llm` with a text model reachable (the app's own as bundled, never a development copy), else, only where no such writer is offered at this decision, the free Basic writer (`fo-prompts` `basic`, a template), logged `workflow.generate.choosing` (the place, before the writers are read) and `workflow.generate.writer` (place, writer, model, rate, quote, `why`: `model` or `noLlm`). **Then each such place is asked whether its render can run**, before anything is agreed or paid (`QueueRunner.prepareBeforeWriting`, `prepareRenderBeforeWriting`): a place with a picture its prompt may name is let through with nothing prepared, since its words decide the kind, the route and the count; with none, a clip always among them since its writer is handed none and its frames choose its kind, it is prepared on one stand-in word with nothing written. A refusal no words can settle — anything but `no-prompt`, `prompt-too-long`, `tokens-missing`, `references-count` and `no-placeholders` — takes the place out of the writing: it is not in the prompts summary, not written and not charged, its box stays as it was, and it reaches the render summary with that reason; a ready answer is only leave to write, and a place that passes is prepared again from its real words. A place whose readiness changes after this check, or whose words then fail to use its pictures, is said by that later preparation. Logged `render.prepare.beforeWriting` (the target, the tokens, the stand-in word, `wordsDecide` or the preparation's answer), `workflow.render.ready` (per place: readiness, reason, whether it is written and why) and `workflow.prompts.dropped` (the places left out with their reasons). **If any writer left is a model's, the prompts summary comes first**, before any writer's call and even where the price is unknown, since an unknown price never means free: a line per model, *{n} prompts to write with {model}, about {amount}* from the writers' quotes, or *price unknown* where none has one; *Price unknown, not counted above:*; *Written free by Basic (no text model reachable):*; *Cancel* makes no call and leaves the boxes as they were, *Write* (`workflow.prompts.confirm`) answers once (`workflow.prompts.summary`, then `.confirmed` or `.cancelled`; `.none` where no writer is a model's). With Basic alone there is no first summary. Then each prompt is written by its writer, the button reading *Writing prompts {n} of {m}…* beside a spinner meanwhile, a model's with the decision agreed passed to `buildPrompt`, which the host refuses once its model, rate or quote has changed; a writer not offered as decided, stock and of the kind decided, is not asked and the place is skipped (`no-basic-writer` for Basic, the reason logged `absent`, `notTemplate` or `notStock`; `decisionChanged` for a model's, said as *the model's price or choice changed after you agreed: press Render again*) and no other writer is asked — Basic never stands in after a paid failure; with a form open on the file, through its draft, taken before the ask and checked after the ask and after its settle (`draftGone`, `saveRefused`, `promptChanged` where the person's words changed since), else through a save of a copy of the file (`sourceChanged`); a refused place is skipped, not prepared from its older words; if the tab goes during the pass, the call in flight ends as it ends, its words saved where no form of the tab held them, no further writer is asked and no render is sent, and a message says what was written, what was refused and why, and what was not reached, the places the check left out among the refused (`workflow.render.stopped`) (`workflow.generate.decided`, `.start`, `.settled`, `.done`, `.refused`, `.failed`, `.none`, `.stopped`; `workflow.render.summary` lists `generated` and `free`); then, in the sections' order, each checked place the tab still offers, but those the check left out, is prepared from its saved words (`QueueRunner.prepare`, §6.15; no prompt is written), with the target its card builds, so a place checked off screen is prepared too; a checked place no longer offered — its row left the scope — stays checked and is listed *no longer here*, never prepared. A summary follows: *{n} renders, about {amount}* (the known costs summed), the ready ones of unknown price listed apart and never counted as zero, each skipped place by its name at its epoch and its place with its reason in words (*no prompt — write one on its card*, at a voice sample keeping no voice *no voice prompt — write it on the voice's Prompt card*, at one keeping a voice *no audition text — write it on the voice's Prompt card*, *the audition text is not the length the voice's model takes*, at a block's voice line *no line to speak — write it in the scene*, a route's own reason, …), the places whose prompt Basic wrote named free, and *Clips use the first frame each block holds now* — frames and then their clips are two presses; *Cancel* adds nothing, the prompts already agreed and written staying written, *Render {n}* (disabled at 0) answers once. On it the ready items alone are added to the viewer's pending batch, and the pass takes exactly their numbers (`submit(only:)`), an item already waiting there left for its own Render; the message says the pass's answer — *{n} renders sent to the batch*, or the queue's own reason followed by *The batch's page shows each render and whether it was sent.* — with *Open* to that batch record — opened only while the film the renders were sent in is still the open one, else refused with *That batch is in the film that was open when you rendered: open that film to see it.* —, and each result lands in its place through the place's own jobs. A pass that answered (*{n} renders sent to the batch*) has handed its places to the batch, so each place it took leaves the checked set — one the pass itself refused or left unresolved included, which the batch's page says — and *Render {n}* counts what is left: the places skipped in the summary, and any checked meanwhile; a cancelled summary, a refused pass or a failure leaves the set as it was. Logged: `workflow.render.start` (focus, checked), `.busy` (a press while one runs), `.settle` (the refusal), `.prepared` (per place: stem, group, readiness, reason, route, model, platform, cost), `.stale` (a place no longer offered), `.summary` (ready, skipped with reasons, sum, unknown), `.cancelled`, `.queued` (the record and each number's place), `.submitted` (the numbers and the answer), `.cleared` (the places cleared and those left checked, or that the tab has gone), `.open.start` and `.open` (the film, the record, opened or refused and why; a throw `.open.failed`, said as *The batch could not be opened: …*), `.failed` (the stage and the error, said as *Rendering failed: …*) and `.idle` (the outcome), each but `.stale` and `.open.start` with its time. **The Generate box.** A Prompt card of a picture or clip place the tab offers carries a *Generate* box, beside its title, or beneath it on a card under `Breakpoints.treeHeaderFull`: it reads the person's choice, else checked while the prompt is empty, or outdated with words a writer wrote (it carries the writer's `plugin` and `writer`), and unchecked over any other outdated prompt, the person's own among them, whose words Render leaves as they are. A choice back at the default is dropped, so that the box follows the state again, and a tick is spent once written; a voice sample, speech, a read-only card and a card outside the tab have none. A card whose prompt *Render* has written collapses on that write unless its text field had the focus as the write began (`prompt.card.written`; a press of *Render* with a pointer takes the focus first, so this holds for a field focused during the pass, or on touch); `workflow.generate.select` and `workflow.selection.reset` log the box's changes.
 - **Compare** (`tabs/compare_tab.dart`, `tabs/compare_form.dart`) — **exactly two versions**: the viewer's own latest version at this epoch on the left (`EntityVersions.highestBy` under their exact handle — a workspace does not inherit its owner's versions for this), and the version they selected on the right, whatever it is — **unless the page was asked for an exact pair** (`BrowserState.compare`, `openCompare`, §7): after a write the app made, the right version's page opens on Compare with the left column the version written, whatever else the viewer holds at that epoch, until the next navigation (`EntityTabs` opens a tab it is asked for once for each request, `requested` and `request`; `CompareTab.requestedLeft`). The others are reached by selecting them. Both columns are the View/Edit layout of `tabs/entity_form.dart` in single-column mode, so a field looks the same here as on its own page; the left is editable when the project can be written and the file is not the library's; the right is editable only where it is **the viewer's own version** too — never another author's, official or not, and never the library's (`CompareTab.rightAccess`, logged as `compare.columns {left, right, viewer, left_editable, right_editable, right_reason}`, the reason one of `own`, `no_viewer`, `not_yours`, `library`, `not_writable`, as the tab opens and whenever that answer changes, never for a redraw alone); each column that writes carries its own save status under its heading, which shows only a failed write and its way out, the right's controls named `right:draft.retry` and `right:draft.discard`. The right column's controls that name no version — the epochs' rows and *Add an epoch* on a project — carry the same `right:` prefix (`FormScope.idPrefix`), so each column's is reached by its own identifier; a field's identifier names its version already. It is **one scroll view of paired rows**, not two forms side by side: sections pair by id, fields pair by id within them (`filmopen_format/flat_json.dart`'s `mergeOrder` keeps a key only one version has beside its siblings rather than below everything), and the taller cell sets the row's height so the next pair starts level on both sides. A key the other version does not have draws nothing, its row keeping its place. **Every label stands at the top of its box and an empty box is empty**, no hint in it, no dash and no placeholder words — *Select Style*, a token pull-down's *None* (no entry chosen; *None* stays the menu's way to clear it, its entry drawn by its own widget so that choosing it leaves the box empty), a scene's empty blocks list's *(empty)*, the empty sheet's sentence, a generic or residue row's dash, an empty relationship, garment or cast list's dash or line about what fills it (`FormScope.lock` in a comparison, and `ComparingFields`, a plain inherited widget, for the boxes a field decorates itself; not a theme, which a dialog would take with it, so that a dialog opened from a column, such as *Add epoch*, keeps its own hints) —, so that a value that was empty reads as empty; elsewhere a label sits in an empty box and rises as it fills. No column is bordered for being picked: the pick is the version row's. The path, the format and the dates are no field and are not compared: there is no *File* card in a comparison; *Other attributes* keeps the film's keys no field names (a scene's `weather` and `props`, a folder's `children`) and never a header key. **A scene's script is a git-like block diff** (`filmopen_format/script_diff.dart`'s `scriptDiff`), wherever the two columns draw block rows: the longest common run of the two versions' block ids pairs its blocks, same or changed; a block only one version has is a row blank on the other side; and a block both have outside the run is **moved** — two rows, the block *out* at its place on the right with the left blank, and *in* at its place on the left with the right blank, each blank side empty, since the block is in both versions. Each cell is drawn from its own side; every other section keeps `mergeOrder`. The diff is logged as `compare.diff.start {stem_left, stem_right, left_ids, right_ids}` and `compare.diff {stem_left, stem_right, rows: [{display, kind, left_id, right_id, left, right}], counts, ms}`, each row with its two blocks as compared. The diff is derived when the comparison opens and again only when either column's file or blocks change — another version, a copy, the times an autosave hands back to a written block —, so typing outside the script, its save and a redraw (a theme, a resize) neither derive nor log it again; what *Show changes only* hides is derived again when the project, either column's file or either draft changes, or when the rows change while the drafts do not — a plug-in turned on or off in Settings moves its data between its card and *Other attributes* —, never for a redraw alone. **Show changes only**, a checkbox under the column headings (`compare.changes-only`), kept in the settings (`compareChangesOnly`, on until chosen otherwise, a saved choice standing; its write logged as `settings.compareChangesOnly.start {from, to}`, then `settings.compareChangesOnly {value, written, ms}`, a warning with the failure's `code` and `cause` where the store refused it, the choice kept for the session either way), hides every row whose two versions are equal — a block row where the diff calls it the same, any other row where its keys hold equal values (`canonicalJson`); a section ends with *n unchanged* for the rows it hid, the invisible `next_block` row never counted, and a section with nothing changed is hidden whole; the Notes card stays. While it is on, `compare.filter {stem_left, stem_right, changesOnly, hidden: [{section, row, left, right}], hidden_sections, ms}` says what it hid, each row with its two values as compared. Down the middle runs the gutter of **copy-left**: a 28 px box for `<`, and a second for `>` where the right column may be written — 56 px then, 28 px everywhere else. The column headings open that version, each on one line: whole where it fits, else **its handle cut and the version's number kept** (*maximiliana-… v7*), the left heading taking the larger share beside the save status; the right one carries the check and the star, the left — the viewer's own — no pencil and no check, only the star when it is official. The intro line, the file menu and the official-edit banner are the page's, not a column's, and are not repeated here.
 - **The media place** (`filmopen_insertion_ui/media_place.dart`) is the one element a page shows and adds media with. One `MediaPlace` declares a place — its `InsertionTarget`, the version and a `SlotPlace` naming a group of its `media` (§9.0), or a `BlockPlace` naming a group of a block's (§10.3); the document the page is about to write; `onDocument`, the page's door for every edit of the entries, its draft and autosave; whether the version may be written; a title; `newSince`, the instant a take is new after (the viewer's `visited`, §13.4); and `onDelete`, the page's delete door — and the element draws one wrap of tiles. **The add tile comes first**, on an editable place only: **+** as its heading above one button per action of `InsertionActions` that makes a kind the place takes (`acceptedKindsFor`) — *Upload*, built in with the id `upload`, then what the application registers — *Render* (id `render`, the render dialog below) — in that order. A button that may not run keeps its place, disabled, with its reason in its tooltip: no handle, no media folder, *Adding…* for Upload while a job of this place is queued, working or waiting, and for every action while an upload or submission is under way; Render and Voice can queue beside producer jobs — busy is per place, and another place on the page submits independently — or the action's own reason (`availableFor`). The tile is one tile wide and grows with its buttons, and a button whose word does not fit keeps its icon, its tooltip and its name and drops the word, measured in the person's language; a disabled one keeps its name too, its reason what its tooltip adds. **The whole media area of an editable place takes a drop** where the platform can drop files (`file_drop.dart`, on `desktop_drop`; never on the web) — the add tile, the job tiles, the entries and the space between and after them, the area as wide as the place: the add tile's **+** says so in its tooltip; files dragged over the area colour its edge and say, in a label drawn over the tiles, what letting go would meet — *Drop files here to add them*, or why not yet (*wait until this place has finished adding*, no handle, no media folder) — painted over the tiles, so that nothing on the page moves and no pointer is taken; and the files let go on it are judged as an action's are, through the same submission as *Upload*'s file dialog — those the place takes submitted as one selection in the order dropped, the rest named under the tiles — or, where nothing may be added now, refused whole with the reason under the tiles (*wait until this place has finished adding* while it is busy). A link dragged from a browser arrives as its address, which no file is: it is named under the tiles, by its last part and never its query, and pointed to *Upload*'s address box, and never read as a file. A folder dropped is judged by its name as a file is: the wrong kind where its name has no media extension, unreadable where the import tries to read it; a link to a file is followed as the file dialog follows it. A drop outside the area reaches nobody — nor does one under a dialog, a menu or a drawer, or on a page hidden behind another, since the seam asks a hit test at the drop's point over the area's whole box — nor one on a version that may not be written, which takes no drop; and the keyboard reaches every button, so a drop is never the only way. **Where media goes is fixed when it is dropped or chosen**: what the file dialog answers after the page has moved to another place is discarded, never inserted there; a submission keeps the place busy until it ends, and only that place; a failure is said on its own place: a failure that comes back while the page shows another place is kept for its own place and said when the page shows that place again; it is never said on another place; it is lost when the page's element is disposed before the place returns. The insertion runtime does not yet log its refusals before submission. A refusal line and an action's failure are cleared when the page shows another place. A refusal of a drop is said for as long as its reason lasts. **Then this place's jobs, newest first** — **one tile per import**: one file's import or render at its attachment — the stage at which the document comes to name its output, which the place draws first, where the job's tile stood — is drawn on that entry's own tile, the tile saying the job's words with its own and wearing a small spinner at its centre, the picture in full view, until it ends, never as a second tile beside it, its *Stop* not offered for that moment; a job at any other stage keeps its own tile and its actions — one that waits, needs attention, failed or is being stopped, one run again, and one whose output the place does not draw: deleted, archived behind the fold, another group's — and one of several files keeps its tile until it is done, a job naming its first output alone — each a `JobTile` two tiles wide — the phase's mark, its sentence through `jobStateText` (a render's — a producer's job, `producerJobKind` — in a render's words where its code is the render's: where it is by the render's phase, *Waiting to render…*, *Rendering…* — from the moment it is sent, a render done within one call as much as one its service holds — *Fetching what it made…* (`renderPhaseText`), and why it ended where the insertion kept the producer's own code (`producerFailureOf`, `renderEndedText`): the plug-in's own words where it gave them (§6.15), else the host's refusals in the sentences its pages use, those that would name what a job does not keep in general words, *FilmOpen closed while this was rendering; it is never sent again by itself.*, the contract's `unknown` — a render its service took whose outcome could not be read — as *The render was sent, and whether it finished is not known: it may still be running where it was sent. Nothing is sent again by itself; look there before rendering again.*, a render queued a week that nothing took in given up without being sent, and **after any of these, where a paid service took a request of the render** — any of its calls, its submission kept with the job through a collect that then fails, or a call on a person's key in flight when the application closed — (its code marked `charged.`, the mark kept where the code is too long to keep with it), *It may have been charged: Activity → Usage shows the cost.* before its own sentence, so that a tile too narrow for the whole says it, a failing tile showing up to eight lines and whole on hover and to a reader, a plug-in's own code as it gave it, *The render did not finish (missing-models); nothing is sent again by itself.*, and the same without a code where it failed with none the job could keep; a code of the insertion's own after the render, a take it could not write, keeps the job's words), a bar where progress is a number, one small button per action it offers (`job:<id>:<action>`) — keyed by the job's id, as an entry's tile is by its reference, so that a rebuild moves nothing while a job runs. A job that reaches *done* is said once (*Added …*) and dismissed, its outputs arriving as entries with the re-read its attachment made; every action the place sends for a job — that dismissal, or a tile's button — is logged as it is sent (`media.jobAction`: the job's id, kind, phase and state, the action, the place and the reason, `doneShown` or `person`) and as it ends (`media.jobActionDone`: the outcome, the acknowledgement's code and the time, a refusal or a failure a warning, a failure with its error's type and text); one that ends `recordNotWritten` says so, with *Dismiss*. **Then the entries**: the live ones, then, behind *Archived (n)* (`media:<stem>:<group>.archived`), the archived ones, dimmed and wearing the archive mark; a deleted entry is never drawn. Each is a `MediaTile` (`filmopen_media_ui`) wearing what the film knows about its take — the **provenance strip**, from the batch item the entry names and from nowhere else (`provenanceText`: the model on its platform and the cost, as every amount is shown (§9.5), and left out where the item says none; a model the catalogue knows named as the catalogue names it, as the render dialog and Usage name it, else as the item wrote it, and the platform as the item wrote it, since one platform runs on this computer and on a server; *Imported* for an import batch; nothing where the entry names no batch, even where the take's own record exists, or where its item names no model) — a star where the version marks it a favourite, and *New* where the batch the entry names was created after `newSince` (`arrivedAfter`, §13.4), which an entry that names no batch, a null `newSince`, or an entry with `kept_at` never is, so *Keep* clears it at once; its note, provenance and kind are in its tooltip, a note also wears a mark, and a screen reader hears what is drawn — the name, the kind, the marks, that there is a note, and the strip; an archived tile as archived, not new. **Its border** says what a person needs to know about a render, from three plain values the place hands it — the entry's `stage` and `unreviewed`, and the status of the one `MediaProvenance.of` the place computes per entry per build (§6.9) — in one order: a status that is unknown, its record gone or saying no `request.asked_at`, the plain 1 px outline, whatever stage the entry retains; unreviewed orange and dashed (`AppColors.caution`, `DashedBorderPainter` of `filmopen_ui`: 6 px dashes, 4 px gaps); outdated red (`colorScheme.error`); then the stage — final green, draft green and dashed (`AppColors.positive`), prototype blue (`AppColors.prototype`); else, an upload, the outline; every coloured border 2 px, painted over the picture where it is dashed. So a new render already outdated is orange, and *Keep* turns it red. The tooltip's top line is **the status in a box** with the border's colour, width and dash (`MediaStateBox`): the word of the border that holds — *Unknown*, *Unreviewed*, *Outdated*, *Final*, *Draft*, *Prototype*, or *No stage* for a current placement with no recorded stage, an import or a render whose entry names none — and one sentence saying what it means, built by `mediaStateOf` in the border's own precedence, so the box and the border cannot disagree: outdated names the object its first change was made to, never a field — *The character changed after the image was generated*, *Block 3 changed …*, *The blocks it covers changed …*, *The character Aaron changed …* (a source the project cannot name: *A source it was made from changed …*), *The character is now at the final stage; this image was generated at draft* — in the media's own word (image, clip, sound); unreviewed *Nobody has kept or rejected this image yet*, with the outdated sentence as the next line where both hold; a placement's current stage *Generated at the final stage, and nothing has changed since*; a stage with no placement status (a take placed twice or nowhere, a frame in the render dialog) *Generated at the draft stage*, claiming nothing about changes; unknown *FilmOpen cannot tell what this image was generated from*; no stage *Nothing has changed since it was added*, claiming no origin; nothing said, no box. The label leads with the same word and sentence, so the colour is never the only sign, and ends with what only the tooltip shows — the note's words and why the tile cannot open — since the tooltip itself is kept out of the semantics; then the tooltip's lines — `provenanceLines`, then `factLines`: its length and its size, the record's where it has them, else the entry's, so an entry no record names still says its own. The tile reads nothing of the project: it computes no status of its own, only the box from what it is given. A row narrower than one tile, a Compare column's, gets tiles as wide as it, so that they stay square. A tap opens the viewer in every mode, with the strip's words and the entry's note under the picture. **Each tile has a menu** (`media:<stem>:<group>:<n>.menu`) on an editable place, and on any version plug-ins may run on (`PageAccess.canRunPlugins`: a project the viewer writes, a handle, never a library file) — there, on another author's version, *Open* and the injected actions alone, nothing that edits the version. On an editable place: *Open*; on an unreviewed render *Keep* (`media:<stem>:<group>:<n>:keep`), which removes `unreviewed` and writes `kept_at` — a UTC instant to the second — through `onDocument` and says *Kept …*, and *Reject* (`media:<stem>:<group>:<n>:reject`), which is *Delete* without its question: the entry flagged deleted through `onDocument`, then the page's delete door, the record kept; neither asks, and neither moves the document's `updated`; on a take whose record is a render's where the registered *Render* applies at the place, *Render from this take* (`media:<stem>:<group>:<n>:render-from`, dimmed with *Render*'s own reason where it may not run), which runs *Render* through the same checks as its button — a block's extent asked first — continuing the take (`InsertionAction.continueFrom`): the render dialog opens on the record's model on the route of the plug-in tag the record's version stem names, whatever its version, and its platform, at the place its record names — the server it was sent to, as its request's `destination` holds it, else this computer, as a take made here and every record written before the destination was kept say — and only where no candidate is there, the first of that route (`render.continued {recordedAt, at, reason: recordedPlace|routeOnly|modelNotOffered|routeNotOffered}`), with the prompt and the negative prompt as they were sent, whole, and no treatment added (the record's already holds it), the take's own seed — else the one sent where it was applied —, the way, the values, the stage and the first frame where the model takes it, never the take itself; where that model or route is offered no more the dialog opens as a new one does, keeping the prompt, the seed and the stage; the request carries the take as `from` (`MediaProvenance.of`, Project Specification §12.2); on a take whose record asked at Draft, *Render at final resolution* (`:render-final`, dimmed with the same reason), the same continuation at Final (`continueFrom(…, stage:)`), which the dialog refuses in its body at an outfit's composed sheets and at a place that names no kind, opens only on the picker's accepted Final default and gives only the take's recorded first frame and references (§6.15); *Favourite* or *Unfavourite*, *Archive* or *Unarchive*, *Set as preview* (a picture only), *Move earlier* and *Move later* (among the live or the archived entries, the rest of the array kept in its order), *Note…*, *Delete* — an item that may not run disabled, with why under its name (*Already the preview*, *Already at the start*, *Already at the end*) — every one but *Open* and *Delete* one edit of the document through `onDocument`, which writes back `media`, `preview` and `picks` and nothing else, and only where the edit changed something. *Delete* asks once and says what goes: the file too where the take is the viewer's own — made by them, or under a workspace of theirs as a plug-in's render is (`isViewersTake`) — and no other file of the film keeps it in a live entry — the rule the writer applies again when it deletes (`takeNamed`, `documentsNamingTake`, §6.1a) — and otherwise the entry alone, and why: a file no upload or render of theirs made, a take whose file is not in this folder, another author's take, or a take other files keep. Confirmed, it writes `deleted: true` through `onDocument`, which clears `preview` and every `picks` value that names the reference in the same change (§9.0), and then calls `onDelete`. **After the built-in items come the injected actions** (`MediaEntryActions` in `filmopen_state`, registered by the application): each action whose `appliesTo` holds for the entry and the version, read as the menu opens, behind a divider, identifier `media:<stem>:<group>.entry.<id>`, enabled or disabled with why under its name — a local reason or a provider's refusal, each in a sentence of its own (`mediaActionReasonText`, `providerReasonText` in `labels.dart`, from the reason's code) — and told the entry and the version's access: its project and stem, whether the page may write it (the caller's word and `PageAccess.editable` together) and whether plug-ins may run there. The film it stands on is the one the state holds only while that film is open, not loading while another source is opened, and is the film the place names (`projectKeyOf`); a film closed, loading or replaced by another source is no identity, and every injected item then says *The version or the media changed since the menu opened: open the menu again.*, disabled, and the choice is refused with it. Chosen, everything is asked again — the action still registered, the place and the version the same, the entry still in the document, the action still applying and available, and writing or plug-ins still allowed — and a refusal is said in a message and runs nothing; an action that fails is said under the tiles, by its name, and reported where the app's log hears Flutter's errors (`flutter.error`, with the package, the error's own words and its stack, and the action, the entry and the place as its `context`) (*Analyze did not work.*), until the next choice, drop or submission, or another place. An action that cannot answer whether it applies is not drawn, and one that cannot answer whether it may run is disabled, saying so in general words; where the version allows neither writing nor plug-ins — a version the film no longer holds among them — every injected item is disabled with *Plug-ins cannot run on this version.* whatever the action answers. *Open* says why it may not run where the media folder cannot be reached, as the tile's own tap does. The element imports no form: an action is the application's to register. On another author's version where plug-ins may not run — a read-only project, a library file — there is no menu; on any version that may not be written there is no add tile and the tiles open, and a place with nothing in it says that what the version's author adds will be shown there, in a line of the strip's size rather than a pane's empty state, whose words a column as narrow as Compare's does not break. **What an action brings is judged by the rule the service holds it to** (`acceptsFile`, §5.10): a picture, a clip or a sound of a kind the place takes, never a file of no media kind, though a group may hold one placed by hand. *Upload* opens the upload sheet (`filmopen_insertion_ui/media_upload_sheet.dart`) for the place's kinds — the file dialog filtered to their extensions (`chooseMediaFiles(context, kinds:)`), and an address judged by the name the import would give it, its last part, before anything is fetched: one that names another kind is refused, the sentence saying what the place takes (*pictures*, *clips*, *sounds*, or all three on a place of an author's own), and one that names no file at all is asked to end in the file's name — and every file an action brings that the place does not take is named in a line under the tiles with what the place takes, which stays said until the next submission, whatever that brings, while the rest go to the service as one selection, in their order, and arrive at the front of the place's group, before what was there — the newest beside the add tile and the place's jobs, as the jobs are newest first; the service's refusal stays inline too. The element holds no writer: it imports no journal or importer, of the store only the log (`AppLog` and `LogLevel`), and of the jobs and the insertion it names only their contract with a page — the service, the target and its places, the sources, a producer's job's kind and its failure's code, the job's view, phase and action, an action's acknowledgement and the error's description its log line carries (`JobActionAck`, `describeJobError`), and the two refusals `insert` throws, `MediaImportException` and `JobUnavailable` (`test/boundary_test.dart`, which reads every package import of the element). Its identifiers carry the version's stem and the group — `media:<stem>:<group>.action.<id>`, `media:<stem>:<group>:<n>` (the n-th tile shown), `media:<stem>:<group>:job:<n>` (the n-th job, newest first) and the two above — and its dialogs' are `media.note.text`, `media.note.save`, `media.note.cancel`, `media.delete.confirm` and `media.delete.cancel`. Render and Voice remain available while this place has only queued or active producer jobs, so several outputs can be requested for one character. Submission in progress and an active upload still hold every add action; system drops retain their busy refusal.
