A producer job needing attention with `bindingChanged` uses the render recovery sentence only when it offers Retry; a view without Retry, including Dismiss alone or Stop alone, uses the action-neutral insertion sentence about the changed film or settings. **A block's place** is drawn and edited as an entity's is, through the same readers given the block's id (`entriesIn`, `withEntryAttributes`, `withEntriesReordered` with `block:`; *Keep* one edit through `withEntryAttributes`), every edit writing back `media`, `blocks`, `preview` and `picks` as the result holds them; its identifiers carry the block's id (`media:<stem>:<block id>:<group>`, since two blocks' places of one group share a page), a tile's status is read for its covered blocks (`MediaProvenance.of` with the group and the block), and a frame offers no *Set as preview*, `preview` being the entity's picture. **An add to a block's `clips`, `music` or `sound_effects` asks first what it covers** (`spans_chooser.dart`, *What does it cover?*), once, before any road — an action's, the render dialog's included, which copies the target it is given, and a drop's — and offers only what the group allows (§10.3): a clip *This block only*, the default, which hands no `spans`, or *Through block n* over the blocks after its own, by position with each block's line, so that a list of blocks always names two or more, consecutive from the owning block; music and sound effects *Until the end of the scene*, the default, *Until block n*, or *For n seconds*, *Continue* waiting for a number above zero and saying so; a clip on the scene's last block, and the frames and voice lines, ask nothing. *Cancel* adds nothing. The answer travels in the target (`BlockPlace.spans`) and is read-only afterwards: an entry's extent is one line of its tile's tooltip, in the chooser's words, and none where it has no extent its group allows or the block it names is no longer in the scene.
- **The render dialog** (`filmopen_render_ui/render_dialog.dart`, `RenderRequestDialog`) is what *Render* on a place's add tile opens (`RenderAction`, id `render`), the one dialog a render is asked for with wherever it is asked. **Top to bottom**: the reading line, or that no model is ready, where either is said; **the stage** (below); the Prompt card or the box; the treatment's lines and box; the box's negative and the treatment's negative where there is no card; **Recommend** — one line, not editable, *Recommend: <model>, <place>, <settings>*, the model's name, the route's own label as *Runs on* names it (a server's name, never its engine's platform) and the chosen value of each choice among values in the route's order, whoever chose them (`render.recommend`), with an arrow, collapsed as the dialog opens and opened by its arrow, or by a refusal among the controls under it (no model ready, no route here, a route or a way not ready, a server gone, a value outside its bounds), once for each refusal, logged `render.recommend {expanded, reason: tap|refusal, code?, text}`, the line as shown, and `render.recommend {reason: shown, why, text, ms}` as the line first shows and each time its words change, while the plug-ins are read too, never per rebuild — `why` the last landing's reason (`ownServer`, `recorded`, `remembered`, `picked`, `ready`, `first`), `chosen` where the person chose the model, the route, the way or a value since, `serverGone` where the dialog moved off a server that went, or `ready`/`first` where it moved off a place on this computer that went; opened, it shows the model, the span, the servers, the route, the way and the other choices below; **Images** (`render.images`, a whole number, the route's default, else 1) — the route's own `num_images` choice where the way chosen offers it as a whole number, within its bounds, a change asking the price again as any priced choice does, else held at 1 for an image model, its tooltip saying the model renders one image at a time there, and not shown for a model that renders no images — logged `render.images.start {from, offered}` and `render.images {typed?, count?, offered, max?, outcome: set|refused|held|hidden, reason: accepted|below|above|notWhole|notOffered|notImageKind, kind?, ms}` — an emptied box `set` with no count, the route's default — the text typed, the parsed count and a code; **the price** beside it, once, the route's quote being for every image; then what a model needs below it — the first frame, the refusals, an outfit's prompt-only note, a voice's audition and line, the seed; and *Cancel*, *Render* and **Add to batch** (`render.queue`), held with *Render* and saying its reason, absent while a voice is designed (its previews are auditioned at once): it freezes the request *Render* would send, logged `render.answer {queued, model, platform, stage, target}` as *Render*'s is, and adds it to the viewer's pending render batch, inserting nothing — `queue.add.start {target, model, platform, request}`, then `queue.add {stem, n, target, model, platform, ms}` beside the pending set's own `set.written` — with the message *Added to the pending batch* and *Open* (`render.queue.open`), which shows the batch's *Pending* row; a refused add (the record changed meanwhile, another author's, a failed write) is logged `queue.add.failed {stem, cause, error, target, model, platform, ms}` and said under the place's tiles as the action's failure, nothing inserted; a page left before the add begins logs `queue.add.failed {cause: unmounted, target}` and adds nothing. It offers **the models of the kinds the place takes** — a picture's `image` entries, a clip's `video`, a voice sample's `tts` (`renderModelsFor`), grouped by kind and by name — that the dialog can give everything they require (the prompt, a negative prompt, a seed and a first frame — one of the version's pictures; a model that needs a reference picture, a last frame or a sound is not offered until the dialog can give it — an outfit's sheet gives its references, a take continued the ones it was made from, and a prompt the pictures it names with `@` (§9.8)) — at a character's, location's or prop's reference pictures or sheets and at a block's first or last frames only the `i2i` entries where the prompt names pictures, or a take continued carries them, and only the `t2i` where it names none — **the kind following the words** as they are typed, here or on the page, and as Generate writes them: where they come to name pictures or stop naming any, the offers are read again for the new kind and the model picker asked for it, *Render* held with the stage's *asking* reason until its answer lands, a model chosen by hand under the old kind replaced by the picker's (a catalogue entry is of one kind) — one chosen from the old kind's offers while the new kind is asked is shown, but *Render* stays held until the new kind lands (`render.kind.held {reason: modelOfOldKind, to, model}`) —, and a reading that fails held as a stage's re-pick that fails, where a model that needs a picture, chosen with words naming none, holds *Render* with its sentence (`references-needed`); logged `render.kind.start {from, to, reason: tokens}` and `render.kind.done {outcome: landed|superseded|failed|modelChosen|closed, offers, picker: asked, ms}` —, and at a character's motion clips only the `i2v` (the entry's `tag`), and of those only the ones some route here can run (`RenderBackend.candidates`). With none it says so and what would — a plug-in that renders, installed and on — with *Settings* (`render.settings`), which closes the dialog and opens Settings on *Plugins*. **While the plug-ins are read again** — one allowed, turned on or off, given a key or set a moment ago — what runs where is not known yet: the dialog says so in place of that (`render.reading`), says no route's not-ready reason meanwhile, and holds **Render**, its tooltip saying the same; when the reading ends it reads again what runs where — the models a plug-in allowed meanwhile runs among them — starting where a new dialog would where the person has chosen nothing, and otherwise keeping what they chose — the model, where it runs, its way, its values, the first frame — where it still stands, a server chosen and gone said as such, and saying that no model can render here where none runs any more. **The end of a reading never tears down what the person has open**: where it leaves the model and the place on screen as they are, they stay — the way, the values and the first frame with them, nothing asked again, a place that became ready asked what it offers over what stands — and any other change — another model, another place, the places' pull-down giving way to *Runs on …* where one place is left, a place no longer ready, which then says why in place of its way and values, the choices the person made kept for when it is ready again — waits while a menu stands over the dialog, a pull-down's open — a menu opened in the very moment counting — and lands once it closes, **Render** held meanwhile and until the reading that follows the menu's close has landed, a bound that passed under the menu included; a model or a place chosen in a menu that a reading landed under is the one offered now, each found by what makes it that model or that place — a model by its id, a place by its plug-in, its platform and its server, never by its words, which name a plug-in or a number where two places are alike and change when one of them comes or goes. Every reason to read what runs where again — a server started or stopped, a plug-in turned on, a reading ended — starts a reading of its own and only the last asked for lands, so one asked for while the end of a reading is taken in is read in turn; the line and the hold last until the last has landed, a change waiting for a menu included, and for no longer than 30 seconds, after which a reading that has not ended is not waited for. Otherwise it starts — **at a place with a kind, on the model picker's default**: the one model picker (§6.11) is asked as the dialog opens, for the place's kind at the dialog's stage (`renderPicked`), and again at a stage changed in the dialog unless the person chose the model — from its pull-down, or by continuing a take, whose recorded model is its choice; a route, a way, a value, the count or a frame is no model choice —, its answer landing as a new dialog's would, the way and settings of a row naming the model and route already chosen applied over them and priced, and the dialog opens on the model, the plug-in and the platform its row names where that route is offered here — ready or not, its reason said and *Render* held where it is not, unless a ready server the person started here (a session of theirs, not one its provider merely listed) runs an offered model, where the dialog opens on that server instead — with the row's variant as the way and its settings over the route's defaults where the route takes them, a setting it does not offer dropped and never sent (`render.picker`, `render.route` with its `decision` — `accepted`, `noDefault` or `noRoute` on the picker's default; where the picker was not asked, `recorded` for a take's record, `gone` for the model chosen offered no more, `unread` for the picker's answer not come — and the opened route's label, destination and why it opened there: `ownServer`, `recorded`, `remembered`, `picked`, `ready` or `first`, `render.picker.params`); applied again where a reading starts the dialog again with nothing chosen, never over a choice; the last options, below, stand before all of this, at a place with no kind too — else, at a place with no kind, where the picker names none, or where its route is not offered here, at the first model with a ready route, a ready server the person started first, then their own computer: **the model** (`render.model`), **where a GPU server is started** — in advance, on the rail's GPU page (§9.7a), never from the dialog, which holds no provisioning open: where a server provider is on and no server is starting, ready or costing, one line says so and *Open GPU* (`render.openGpu`) closes the dialog and opens that page; a server starting or ready is a route under *Runs on*; with every provider off, a server still costing is the GPU page's and the rail's badge's to say, and the dialog says nothing of servers. **The provider's window** (`ServerOffersDialog`), which *Start server* on the GPU page (`gpu.start`) opens — it says it is checking, then a search (`servers.search`), when the prices were read, that they hold five minutes, and *Refresh* (`servers.refresh`), and the catalogue's GPU configurations (`servers.offer.<n>`, scrolling within a box of their own with its bar showing, `servers.offers`) with their priority in the person's language, availability unchecked and **the whole hourly price**. Selecting a row checks only that GPU class, retaining the catalogue while it asks; the selected result says its count or that availability is unknown. Unknown or zero capacity and malformed or exhausted quota hold Deploy with their own explanation. Discovery, each selection and the explicit Deploy recheck each have a thirty-second budget, including their wait for admission; discovery shares it across capabilities and catalogue. A new gesture or closure cancels its preceding check, and only the current generation may end its spinner; an expired or obsolete recheck starts nothing. A host timeout says the provider did not answer in time, and cancellation says stopped; a provider message is never the only explanation of a host-generated failure. Where quota is absent, the window says quota is checked when the server starts; *Refresh*, disabled while the provider is asked, saying so; its title names the provider's platform (*SaladCloud*); the inactivity minutes (`servers.idleMinutes`, a whole number, 15 to start at, 5 to 60, or the provider's declared range), legible and read-only during a selected check, recheck or deployment so the pending explicit action keeps the lease shown, under them that a render keeps the server awake and the window does not; *Deploy* (`servers.deploy`, 44 px), enabled on a GPU whose selected check confirms capacity, usable quota, fresh prices and minutes in range, its tooltip saying why not, which rechecks the selection and starts one server and closes the window with a message saying when it stops by itself — or, where the provider did not answer, that FilmOpen is checking whether it started — and **a refusal stays in the window** (`servers.refused`), the provider's words where it gave them, else the refusal's in the person's, with where a server was made all the same that it is listed to be stopped; **an engine** (`servers.engine`) where the provider runs more than one, its first engine chosen when the window opens, and sent only there, and, once a GPU is chosen, **a model stack** (`servers.stack`) among the engine's stacks that fit its VRAM — each its label, its download in GB and *about N minutes to start* where the provider gives them, *The provider's choice* first, which sends none —, a GPU no stack is known to fit saying so and that *Deploy* lets the provider choose, one that cannot hold the stack chosen dropping it, so that a stack hidden is never sent, each choice logged `servers.window.choice` with what it left chosen; **the agreed price**: where *Deploy*'s recheck answers another hourly price than the one shown, nothing starts, the window shows the new price in its row and in the refusal line (*The price is now … Press Deploy again to start at this price.*, to the tenth of a cent where the price has one), and only a second *Deploy* at that price deploys, a further change asking again (`servers.window.recheck` {shownUsd, checkedUsd, decision: `deploy` | `shown-again` | `refused` with the code and the provider's words — quota, capacity, a failed quote, a stopped operation —, ms}; a failure nobody expected logged `servers.window.deployFailed` with its type and words); a provider pull-down where more than one is on (`servers.provider`); *Cancel* (`servers.cancel`), which leaves a deployment asked going on — the server started then listed on the GPU page; **where it runs** (`render.route`, a pull-down where there is more than one, else *Runs on …*) — a ready server the person started here first, since its minutes are paid while it waits, then the person's own computer, then each other server whose engine the route runs on (*SaladCloud · RTX 3090 (24 GB)*, not ready while it starts, or found at its provider and not started here), then what bills by the call, two named alike each with its plug-in's name — read again whenever a server starts or stops, the place chosen kept — a server past its lease offered no more, and a server chosen that stops or runs past its lease giving way to the first place that is ready, with a line under *Runs on* saying so and naming that place (`render.serverGone`), and **Render** held until the person chooses where a choice of places is left — and, where the route is not ready, why — never allowed, off, needing a newer FilmOpen, no key saved, a key not granted, no server, no way of running it ready, or the plug-in it runs through never allowed, off, needing a newer FilmOpen or not installed, named (`renderNotReadyText`); a key's reason names its platform, never the route's label, and the plug-in whose own key is missing, where it is a plug-in's, or the plug-in it runs through that may not use it yet (*The plug-in OpenAI may not use your OpenAI API key yet.*), no key saved being said before a key not allowed — with *Settings*, which opens *Plugins*, where a plug-in's key, its grants and its switch are; where no model has a ready route, its first line says that no model is ready yet. **A clip its entry says makes no sound** says so under the model (`render.silent`: *The clip is silent: this model makes no sound, whatever the prompt says.*). **At a block's clip, a model left out for want of a kept first frame** says how to have it, under the model menu or in the empty state (`render.keepFirstFrame`: *Keep a first frame to use it for a clip*). **At every place**, each model left out for an input the place cannot give is logged with what it lacks, what the place gives, the kind and a command's reference (`render.offer.leftOut`). A line's words are what speech is, not an input: a speech model declares no `text` input, and an empty `inputs` map still refuses every media name, where an entry with no map admits them. **The first frame** (`render.frame`), on a model whose entry takes one: the version's pictures — as the open form holds the version, typing included, else as its file does — that the entry's first frame takes (`firstFramesOf`): live and not archived, a file the film holds rather than an address, of a format the input lists and, where the import measured it, no larger than the input names (`inputRefusal`, §5); its reference images first, then its sheets — a character's or a location's — then its other places in the order of its file; each a small tile (`render.frame.<n>`, its picture `render.frame.<n>.picture`, its border its record's stage alone, never a review or a status) chosen by a tap, the chosen one outlined and checked, a screen reader told which, and named under them (*The clip opens on …; choose another picture to change it.*); the first chosen to start, and a picture chosen for one model kept for another that takes it too; where the version holds none, what to add — a picture of the formats the entry lists (*Add a PNG or JPEG picture to this version first: the clip opens on one of its pictures.*) — in the error colour, the model offered all the same. On a ready route it asks the route what it offers (`options`) and shows **its ways of running the model** (`render.stack`, where there is more than one, those not ready marked, the chosen one's issues under it — in the plug-in's words — and, where the chosen way is not ready on a ready route, *Settings* (`render.settings`) under them, opening *Plugins*, where a plug-in's key is entered on its page and where the plug-in's own actions, such as checking or fetching its files, are), **its choices** (`render.param.<name>`), each drawn by its kind — a pull-down of the values both the route and the chosen way's variant allow (§14.7), else the entry's (`renderValuesAllowed`), a switch, a box for a number within its bounds — read-only on the locked look, showing the one value, where its bounds allow no other — or a text — in the person's language as the host gives them, or, where the route names none, in the app's own for the catalogue's parameters (`labels.dart`'s `renderParamLabel`: *Resolution*, *Quality*, *Format*, *Expand the prompt*, *Variant*, *Background*), and its values likewise where the route gives no words for them — the qualities, backgrounds and formats the catalogue's image models share (`renderValueLabel`: *Low* to *Highest* and *Automatic*; *Automatic*, *Opaque*, *Transparent*; *PNG*, *JPEG*, *WebP*), the id sent as it is, another way keeping only the values it takes and giving the rest its own — and another route of the same model, chosen by the person or found anew when the servers change, keeping the values the person chose that it takes, so that a size or a quality chosen on OpenAI stays chosen on fal where fal takes it, and giving every other choice its own default, never the last route's, while another model starts afresh — a box the person empties leaving the choice to the route's own default, never sent, and a box holding text that is no value saying so under it; **the prompt** (`render.prompt`) — the place's own (`renderPromptAt`: a block's first or last frames or its clip that block's `prompts.first_frame`, `.last_frame` or `.clip`, a place whose prompt kind is a sheet (`placePromptKind`: a character's, a location's, an outfit's or a prop's `*_sheets`) `prompts.sheet`, every other place the document's `prompts.reference`; an outfit's sheets the outfit's `prompts.sheet` alone, its character's pictures sent with it) — starting — as the open form holds the version, typing included, else as its file does — at that prompt's `positive` for a picture or a clip — empty for a character's motion clip that opens on a first frame, whose picture shows the looks already (a block's clip shows its card on the block's own words instead), labelled *Motion prompt*, with a line saying to write what moves and how the camera moves and an example in the empty box, in italics and never sent — and, labelled *Words to say*, at what its voice says (`voice.audition_text`) for a voice sample, and never replacing words the person wrote; labelled *Your prompt* wherever it holds the person's words; **at a picture's place of a character, a location, an outfit or a prop — their reference pictures and their sheets, an outfit's on its own `prompts.sheet`, its words alone sent with the character's pictures first (§10's outfit composite), a prop's sheets on its own `prompts.sheet` (the writers' target `prop-sheet`) — and at a block's first and last frames and its clip (the block's `prompts.clip`), the prompt is a Prompt card** (`PromptCard`, the writers' target `promptCardTargetAt`, the prompt's place `renderPromptPathAt`), **bound to the page's own draft** of the file (`OpenDrafts.of`, an `EditableDraft`): the dialog's prompt and negative prompt boxes, inside the card, type into that draft as a keystroke on the page does, the page's boxes behind the dialog following — the prompt's box a `ProseEditor` whose `@` offers the media the card's kind may name, read and written by its source, the negative a plain box offering nothing —, and a changed word takes the prompt's `plugin` away (`withPromptWordChanged`, the one hand-edit rule the forms' boxes use too); its *Generate* is the one way the dialog writes a prompt, into the page's draft, the writer handed the place's media tokens of the draft as it stands (`renderWriterTokens`) — at a block's clip only where the selected model's row maps `reference_images`, else none, so a frames, voice or text way is never written a prompt naming pictures it would refuse —, *Render* held while it runs, saying *Writing the prompt…*, and sending what the draft holds once it ends (the card's `onGenerating`); a side whose place holds a value of another shape is locked with the page's reason (*The file holds this value in another shape; change it with Edit JSON.*, `canSetPathText`), never taking words it would drop, nothing coerced; where no page is open on the file in *Edit* the card is read-only, saying *Read-only here: open the page to edit this prompt.*, its words still sent; *Render* sends the draft's words as they stand when it is pressed, a draft closed meanwhile read as it stands. A voice designed shows the card on the character's `prompts.voice` (the writers' `voice`, no `@`), its audition a box of its own. A take continued, a line and a clip at a place with no prompt of its own (`motion`) keep the box — a block's clip shows its card on the block's `prompts.clip` —, and the dialog writes no prompt of itself. It logs `render.prompt.bound {target, editable, block, reason: pageOpen|noDraft|notEditable, mentions: <kind>|none}` where it shows the card, `render.prompt.box {reason: continued|line|motion|place|noSource}` where it keeps the box, `render.prompt.typed {target, side, block, text}` once a side is typed, `render.prompt.held {held, reason: generating, ms?}` as the hold starts and ends, `render.prompt.locked {target, side, block, reason: shape}` once a side is found locked, and `prompt.pluginCleared {stem, path, block, side, text, reason: person-edit}`, the words typed with each; **the negative prompt** (`render.negative`) where the model takes one, starting at that prompt's `negative`; **the film's treatment** on a picture's or a clip's model where one applies to the place's entity (`treatmentFor`, §5, as the open form holds the entity, else as its file does): its `prompts.treatment.positive` in a box of its own under the prompt (`render.treatment`), and, between the prompt and that box, where any of its primary leaves applies, the words they add as the model is given them (`render.treatmentFields`: *After the prompt, from the project: Camera: 35mm film camera; Lens: 50mm*), changed only where they are written, the line naming each document they come from once (*from the project, from Harbour*) — the treatment's `preset`, the style chosen, is stored and never added —, the box's help then saying its words come after them, and its `prompts.treatment.negative` under the negative prompt (`render.treatmentNegative`) where the model takes one, which the person may change or empty for this render alone, each box's help saying that its words are added after the prompt, or the negative prompt, and the document they come from — the project, a folder, a scene's Style — or, where none gives words, that what is typed is this render's alone, and where the place's entity stands ambiguously in the script, that no folder above it passes on its treatment there, and why; a line spoken and a voice designed take none; **the seed** (`render.seed`) where the model takes one, empty for a new one each time; and **the price** (`render.quote`): the route's quote — on a server, that it is paid by the hour while the server runs and the render adds nothing; *About …* for an estimate, the service's own, the catalogue's exact price, *Cost unknown until it runs* — else the catalogue's estimate on a route that bills, else that no price is known; on the person's own computer, nothing to pay. **The price shown, and frozen, is the one for exactly the way and the values shown**: a choice that changes the price asks again, the last answer winning; where an answer leaves a way or a priced value other than the one asked about — the way it suggests, a default filled in, a value the model does not allow replaced — the dialog asks again, twice at most, and a price that never matches the choices is unknown. A busy inspection keeps the chosen way and values visible, says that it is waiting rather than that the way is unavailable, and schedules another ask from the host’s retryAt: a positive remaining delay is clamped to 2–30 seconds, and an absent or elapsed hint uses 3 seconds. A cold busy answer whose only way is the empty, unlabelled default retains the previously completed labels and controls, even when the person selected a completed way whose id is default. It grants no readiness or invented price. Busy with the chosen way not ready shows the current asking sentence inline as well as on the disabled button. One timer belongs to the latest options generation; choosing another route or closing the dialog cancels it. One person-choice episode permits at most five automatic re-asks; another busy reply does not replenish that budget, while a new choice of model, route, way or priced value starts another episode. For the same unchanged episode, an automatic ask and its cold busy answer or exception retain already-earned Render permission, options and price, including an unrelated publication that recreates equal offers. Equality keeps the same model, complete route and key/way, destination and parameter values, with the candidate still ready; a changed choice or genuine nonbusy refusal holds Render normally. A paid speech line without a route quote keeps its catalogue estimate while a busy answer retains the chosen way as ready; the estimate is held only where the answer is busy and that way is not ready. A changed-parameter answer with no ready way therefore still holds Render without a price. A route that cannot say what it offers is not ready, and says so. **The prompt's pictures** (§9.8): the media its `@` tokens name are sent in order as `reference_images`, the words written in the chosen route's own form for them (the priced access row's `placeholders`, *image 1*, `@Image1`), the file keeping its tokens; *Render* is held, with a line in the error colour (`render.prompt.tokens.refused`), where a token names nothing (*The prompt names a picture that is not there: @reference-2. …*), the prompt names more pictures than the model takes, pictures where the model takes none or none where it needs one, or the route's row has no form for them; nothing is cut down to the bound. **The words as sent** — the prompt with its pictures' words written and the treatment's after it — **are held to the bound the chosen way states** (its effective `inputs.prompt.max_characters`, a way's in place of the entry's; none stated, none invented), counted in characters: above it *Render* is held, with a line in the error colour (`render.prompt.length.refused`: *The prompt as sent is {count} characters; this model takes at most {most} here. Shorten it.*), never cut; at a frame, a clip and a sheet alike. Each check is logged once per change of the words as sent, the model or the way, as `render.prompt.length {model, variant, chars, bound, outcome: ok|refused|unbounded, prompt, ms}` (`variant: none` where the model has no way, `bound: none` where none is stated), with the words it measured. **Render** (`render.submit`, 44 px) is enabled only on a ready route and a ready way, with the price answered for the choices shown, no box holding text that is no value, a first frame where the model requires one, and for a clip that opens on one the motion's own words — the treatment's alone say how it looks, which the picture shows (*Say what moves in the clip first.*) — a prompt — the one input every model offered is given: the entity's words and the treatment's, either alone, the treatment's primary leaves never being one alone, since they name the camera and the grade and not what is in the picture — and a seed that is one, its tooltip saying why not; a value the dialog itself changes is the one its box or pull-down shows; it closes the dialog answering the request, frozen: the model, the route with its key's id and the way chosen, the destination, the prompt composed once — the entity's words first, then the treatment's primary leaves that apply, each as *Camera: …* in their order and in English whatever the language, then the treatment's words, joined by `; `, the negatives the entity's then the treatment's (`composePrompt`) —, the choices shown with the negative prompt, the treatment that applied with the document each leaf came from, a leaf the person changed naming none and one emptied or not sent left out (`RenderTreatment`, which the batch item records, §6.15), the seed, the first frame by its reference under the entry's input name (`inputs`), the quote the person was shown, the place captured when *Render* was pressed, the version and the purpose, and when. *Cancel* (`render.cancel`) asks for nothing. **Where renders do not run** — a build with no render manager over a backend, the web — *Render* stays on the add tile, disabled, saying that renders run in the desktop app, and in a desktop session whose renders could not start, saying that restarting tries again (`RenderRunsHere`). **A line spoken in a character's voice** — a candidate that speaks in the voice the character keeps on its platform — says so under the route (`render.voice`: *Spoken in the voice the character keeps on …*) and sends the id `voice.provider_bindings` holds for that platform as the param `voice`; where the character keeps none there it says so (*The character keeps no voice on … yet.*), in the error colour, and holds *Render* with the same words. Such a route is offered on a character's places, and on a block's *Speech*: elsewhere there is no voice to speak in. **A dialogue block's line in its speaker's voice**: *Render* on a block's *Speech* (`media:<stem>:<block id>:voice_lines.action.render`) speaks the block's `dialogue` in the voice its speaker keeps — the speaker the scene's own reference names (`speakerOf`, §6.1: the `cast` entry's epoch, else the scene's), read from the scene as the person sees it, the open form's draft else the file (`blockLineAt`, which the button and the dialog both read). The dialog offers only the routes that speak in a voice the speaker keeps in `voice.provider_bindings` — a route on a platform it keeps none on, or one that speaks in no character's voice, is not offered — and shows the line in the prompt box, read-only in the locked look, whatever model is chosen: the line is edited on the block. The render lands in the block's *Speech* with its stage and `unreviewed`, and no `spans` and no `voice_binding`, which is a voice sample's alone; the voice a line was spoken in is its render record's request, as `params.voice`. *Render* there is disabled, after the build's own reason, with *This block has no speaker.*, *The speaker is not a character this scene can see.* (no such character, none at the scene's epoch, or several), *The speaker has no voice: keep one under the character's Voice samples.* or *This block has no dialogue.* **A line's price follows its words**: where its route names no price, the dialog shows the catalogue's estimate for the characters typed, as they are typed, and freezes that into the request. **The same dialog designs a voice**: *Render* (`media:<stem>:voice_samples.action.render`) on a character's *Voice samples*, while the character keeps no voice on any platform, opens it titled *Generate voice* — once one is kept, *Render* there speaks the audition text with it, as on any place; the samples have *Upload* and *Render* as every place has —, offering the models of voice design alone; the prompt is the card on the character's `prompts.voice` (the *Voice prompt*, its words alone, empty until written or generated; the box keeps `voice.description` as its start only where there is no source file), with a line saying what to describe, and under it the *Audition text* (`render.audition`), starting at `voice.audition_text`, with the model's bounds in its help and an error that holds *Generate voice* where the words fall outside them; empty, none is sent and the plug-in's previews say its own sample text in the person's language, which the help says — never that the service chooses the words. Its previews land in *Voice samples* as a render's takes do, each keeping the preview it is (§9.0). A server render with a missing required key says *A key required to render on this server is not saved yet.* and opens Plugins. Its readiness metadata does not distinguish a route key from a gateway key, so that sentence names neither platform. **The stage** a render is asked at starts at the version's own (`render_stage`, Project Specification §9.0), which its media card sets, and is chosen at the dialog's top with the same three-position control (`StageSelector`, `render.stage`, its segments `render.stage.<token>`) for this request alone — the version's `render_stage` is never written there — *Render* held while the picker's answer at a changed stage is asked (*Asking which model is recommended at this stage…*), a model the person chooses meanwhile ending the wait and kept when the answer lands, and an answer that cannot be read keeping *Render* held (*No model is recommended at this stage: choose one, or choose the stage again*, its tooltip and a line at the top of Recommend, `render.stage.noRecommendation`), Recommend opened, until the person chooses a model or the stage again — nothing asked again by itself; logged `render.stage.start {from, to}` and `render.stage.done {outcome: changed|same|failed|closed, picker: asked|kept, reason: same|noKind|modelChosen|noModelChosen|modelChosenWhileAsking|readFailed|dialogClosed, ms}`, where the picker is asked once its outcome is decided — the model landing, a landing that waited for a menu included, the reading failing, a model chosen meanwhile, or the dialog closing: read from the document as the person sees it — the open form's draft, typing included — else *Prototype*, a render continued from a take included, whatever the take was asked at, except at Final where *Render at final resolution* forces it (`source: forced`), the selector held there with why, logged `render.stage.held {stage, reason: forced}`; *Render* freezes it into the request (`stage`), and the preparation logs it, `render.prepare.stage {ref?, stage, source: entity|absent|forced}` (`ref` where a command prepares). **A block render's span** is read, never chosen here: where the block's place covers several blocks, a line under the model says *Through block {n}* (`render.span`), the position in the scene, from 1, of the last block covered, in the version the dialog opened on; none for the owning block alone, an extent, or a block no longer there. The chooser is the place's, at its add. **At a block's clip** the frames and the voice line are the block's own, never the scene's: *First frame* and *Last frame* tiles (`render.frame`, `render.endFrame`) of its `first_frames` and `last_frames`, and *Voice line* (`render.voiceLine`), a pull-down of its `voice_lines`, given only where the clip covers that block alone — each the ones the route's row maps and the way takes (format, size, a sound's seconds), the person's choice, else the continued take's, else the favourite, else the first; an optional input's row only where the block gives one, and a model that needs one the block lacks is not offered. Same-named clip models say their inputs (*from a first frame*, *from first and last frames*, *from a frame and a voice line*). One line says what the model does with sound (`render.sound`): *The soundtrack is the voice line.*, *The soundtrack is the voice line, lip-synced.*, *The model voices the dialogue itself.*, or the silent line. A choice that fails holds *Render*: *The clip's inputs could not be chosen.* (`render.inputsFailed`). The choice logs `render.inputs.start` (its trigger: `open`, `model`, `continued`, `route`, `stack`, `person`), `render.inputs.excluded` (input, bound, reason) for each candidate left out, `render.inputs.chosen` (input, `how`, reference, `unreviewed`, `favorite`), `render.inputs.given` (each input `offered` or why not, `sound`, `reference_images` — how many pictures the prompt's tokens send as the inputs are chosen, and `references`, those pictures, none where they are refused — and `tokens`: `offered` where the selected row maps `reference_images`, else `none`) and `render.inputs.done` with its milliseconds, or `render.inputs.failed` (stage, cause, the error's message, milliseconds); a route left out at a clip for a required input its row does not map, `render.offer.unmapped` with the model and the platform as they are.
- **The render dialog's last options.** A new render opens, in this order: on a take continued's record; on a forced render's Final default (§6.15); on the last options; on the model picker's default; on the first model with a ready route. A take continued and a forced render never read the last options, even where their own opening fails. The last options are the model, route with its destination, way and values last sent by *Render* or *Add to batch*, a take continued's included, never a cancel's, a forced render's or a sound's; the stage, the words, the negative prompt, the seed and the inputs belong to the place and are not kept. They are kept per machine in the preference `render.last`, one entry per media kind and stage (`image/<stage>`, `video/<stage>`), and read whether or not the place asks the picker, so a block's clip and an outfit's sheet whose words name no picture open on them too, as the render commands do (§6.15). A place reads the entry of each of `image` and `video` it accepts, and uses the first whose model, route and destination it offers, without changing what it offers; otherwise it opens as it would have. That is what they are: the last options, not options per mode, so someone alternating a words-only model and an edit model at different places gets the default where the last one is not offered. A remembered route that is not ready is treated as the picker's is: the dialog opens there, with its reason, unless a ready server the person started runs an offered model. The way and values settle like any choice, a value no longer taken giving way to the default, and the price is the settled choice's or unknown. *Recommend's* line summarises what is chosen, so it stands over a remembered model as over any other. A stage changed in the dialog reads that stage's entry only where the place has a kind and the person has not chosen the model; a block's clip, or an outfit's sheet whose words name no picture, keeps what stands. Logged `render.remembered`, what the memory supplied at the opening; `render.settled.changed`, every way or value the dialog removes or replaces, where it happens and whoever chose it, with why (`wayNotListed`, `notSpecified`, `valueNotTaken` as it settles, `routeReset` as a place or model is left, `picked` as a row is applied, `chosen` as the person changes it), and `render.remembered.dropped` from the preparation's (`wayNotListed`, `valueNotTaken`, `notOffered`); `render.remembered.write` and `.write.skipped`, and in the settings `settings.read` at the start and `settings.keep.refused` for a value JSON cannot hold.
 - **Analyze** (`filmopen_forms/analyze_action.dart`, `AnalyzeEntryAction`; the review `analysis_review_dialog.dart`; the proposal `analysis_proposal.dart`; the line `analysis_line.dart`) is an item of a media entry's own menu (`media:<stem>:<group>.entry.analyze`), injected through `MediaEntryActions` by the media card for the three places an analysis interface serves: a character's *Reference images* (a picture, `reference-analysis` with the kind `image`, `describeReference`), its *Voice samples* (a recording, `reference-analysis` with `audio`, `describeReference`), and a project's or a folder's *Reference images* (a picture's look, `style-analysis`, `describeStyle` with the scope *project* or *folder*). One registration stands per place of a version, however many cards show it — a comparison's column beside the page — passed to a card that stays when one goes, and removed with the last; registering asks nothing of a plug-in. It applies to the place's live entries of its kind, never to a sheet or another group. **Whether it may run** is read from what is already known — the plug-ins' listing, their grants and keys, a check under way — and is said under its name, one sentence per link (`MediaEntryActionReason`): plug-ins cannot run on this version (a read-only project, the library, which in development mode is writable and still runs none); no plug-in that analyzes media is installed; none for this kind; the one that takes this file is off, cannot run in this build, works through a plug-in that is off or missing, is not allowed the key it needs, or has none saved (each only where no route without a key is open); it is being checked; the file is of a type no analyzer takes; the version's own changes are not saved, being saved, or failed to save — the analysis is built from the saved document, so they go first; another analysis of the place is under way. Several analyzers that can run are a choice by name (*Analyze with*), asked again at the choice. **Chosen**, it freezes the entry, the analyzer (its tag and version), the version (its stem and its file's text), the film, and the settled draft, and asks the plug-in once, starting no check before it; *Analyzing kira-front.png…* shows in its review — under *Described by …*, naming the plug-in, with the host's progress where it reports one — and on the line under the place; *Cancel* while it works asks the plug-in to stop and frees the place. A failure is said in its own sentence from its code — a key not granted, none saved, the key store unreadable, the platform plug-in missing, off, unable to run or without what it was asked for, a kind or a function the plug-in lacks, the media missing from the film's folder, larger than an analyzer may be sent, not the type it is recorded as, changed while it was sent, or of a type the plug-in does not take, the key changed under the call, its code where it gave only one and that is a short lowercase word, else *failed* — never the plug-in's or the host's words, and the line keeps it, naming the entry; the log keeps them: `analysis.started {stem, kind, plugin, function, media}`, `analysis.proposed` with the paths, `analysis.edited` with the paths, each edited leaf's value and a refusal, `analysis.discarded {proposal, pending_text}` when the person discards the review's edits — the proposal's leaves as last checked and the words typed in the text and not read yet, as they are —, `analysis.dropped` with its sentence and `ms`, `analysis.failed {plugin, code, reason, message, path, file, line, answer, problem, fields, sentence, ms}` (`fields` the answer whole where a projection refused it at `path`; the code as it came), a call that throws `analysis.callThrew` with its error, and `analysis.applied` and `analysis.forkRefused` with the write's error; with nothing left to cancel the review's button says *Close*. An answer is checked before anything is projected: one naming which voice speech uses, the sample in use or a sample's binding is refused whole (*The analysis tried to change which voice or recording is in use…*), the version's own bindings left as they are; then its leaves are read (`analysisChanges`, `styleChanges`, `withAnalysisApplied`, §5) — a value no leaf takes, a leaf the file holds in another shape, an answer that tells nothing and one the file already says are each said, and nothing proposed. **The review** shows how many fields would change and, for each, its label with its whole new value and what it held before (*Was: …*), wrapped and selectable (`analysis.review.fields`) — what is written is read first — and, for a look, what one picture cannot tell, shown and written nowhere; below, the whole file as it would be, **editable as the JSON editor edits a file** (`JsonDocumentEditor`, the editor's own presentation over the proposal: a tree whose values are changed by tapping them, `analysis.review.tree` with the value box `analysis.review.value`, `.value-cancel`, `.value-apply`, or the whole text, `analysis.review.text`, the switch `analysis.review.mode`), its top level open and the changed leaves' branches opened besides. **Every edit is checked again** (`revalidateProposal`): on each value applied, at the switch back to the tree and at either *Update* — the text never per keystroke —, the text through the duplicate-aware decoder; the keys that name the file as the filename says them (a project's `forked_from` as it was), the bindings exactly as the file held them, by key and value at any depth, `validateContent`, and something still changed; the changed fields are found again, so the list and *Was:* stay true. A refusal is said under the editor and holds both buttons with the same sentence (*As edited, the proposal changes "author", which names the file: put it back as it was to update.*, *…changes which voice or recording is in use…*, *…changes nothing in this file…*). *Cancel* writes nothing and leaves the draft as it was; after an edit, it — or a step back — asks first (`analysis.review.discard`, `.discard-cancel`), and nothing closes the review while it writes. On the viewer's own version, which they may write, *Update this version* writes the proposal once: everything frozen is asked again first — the analyzer, the file, the draft's changes, the entry — and anything changed since the click refuses it, the draft left as the person had it; then the draft takes the proposal and the save goes out bound to the version's file as it was read and the film it was asked in, so a file changed on disk meanwhile is refused as stale, the save status offering *Discard my changes and re-read*, and a refused write keeps its bound writer for *Retry*; written, *6 fields filled from kira-front.png.* (or, for a look, where its leaves are folded). **Update a new version** (`analysis.review.fork`, the version row's fork icon) is offered on every version — beside *Update this version* on the viewer's own, alone on another author's, where the line under the proposal says the version is not theirs to update: it writes the proposal, as edited, as the viewer's own new version **in one write** (`ProjectController.fork` with the proposal as its content, in the film of the click): everything frozen asked again, then the writer re-reads the version against the text the click froze — a file changed since is refused as stale —, checks the keys that name it and the content, writes the new version once and picks it; a refusal creates nothing, picks nothing and is said in the review; written, *7 fields filled from kira-front.png into ch_kira_now_ann_v2, a new version of your own.*, the review closes and the page opens the new version. An edit of a key a new version writes for itself — `version_label`, `label`, `created`, `updated`, `forked_from` — holds it, saying so, *Update this version* left free. **An analysis is dropped** while it is under way or reviewed when the entry or the version changes, the card that showed it goes — the card that started it, even where another card of the version stays, which keeps the registration and may start its own — or **the analyzer itself changes**: the catalogue's notification, which the host sends after every re-read of the film, drops it only where the analyzer's own description differs from the one at the click — gone, another version, asking for consent, off, unable to run, its interfaces, its keys and grants, or what it may reach (hosts, servers, the plug-ins it calls and what they may spend, the AI, the upload) — each said by its own sentence naming it (*Vision was updated after this analysis was asked for: nothing was written. Analyze it again.*); a change of the film alone — the form's autosave just before the click, the look record, an import settling — keeps it, and a change of its words keeps it too. Otherwise *The plug-in, the file or this media changed since this analysis was asked for: nothing was written. Analyze it again.* A call still working is asked to stop; a failure already said keeps its reason. On another author's version, where plug-ins may run, the menu holds *Open* and *Analyze* (in development mode, where that version may be edited, its editing items too).
 - **The review of an import** (`filmopen_forms/import_review_dialog.dart`, `showImportReview`, `ImportReviewDialog`; §6.18, §7.1) is what the browser opens when a plug-in proposes an import. **The browser's listener** (`BrowserPage`) subscribes to `ImportProposals.changes` while the page lives and, after the frame and never during a build, opens the review of the first proposal held for the film open and its viewer whose origin names no operation and that was not put aside — a proposal made by the AI assistant's operation is its own dialog's —, one at a time: the next one held is offered when the review closes; a film still loading reads nothing, and a closed one is offered nothing. It offers one only while the rail shows the Project page, asked again as the frame runs, and offers again as the rail comes back to it: no review opens over Settings or another page. **Put aside**: a proposal closed with *Later* is flagged on the seam and moved last (`ImportProposals.defer`, `ImportProposal.deferred`) and is never offered again on its own — the proposal the review showed as it closed, so that one replaced or let go meanwhile is not put aside and another document held under the id as it closed is a new proposal, offered as one; the flag lives as long as the proposal, a session's memory that nothing writes. At the bottom of its detail region — below the page, never above its cards, in the theme's ordinary colours — a compact control says how many of those held for the film and its viewer are put aside (*2 import proposals waiting*, `import.waiting`) with **Review** (`import.waiting.review`), which reopens the first and whose tooltip says the imports wait only until FilmOpen closes; *Later* again puts it last, so the next *Review* reaches the next one. The control wraps in a narrow pane and is gone when none waits. The flag goes with its proposal on every road a proposal ends — applied, discarded, replaced by another document, dropped at read for another film or viewer, or with an explicit close of the project, even of the same film reopened —: the browser re-reads the proposals held, after the frame, on the seam's notifications, on a change of the film, the settings or the closed flag, as the rail comes back to the Project page, after every review and as *Review* reopens one, whether or not a review may open then — the read drops a proposal of another film or username, and every one after an explicit close (with no film), even behind Settings; a film still loading reads nothing. The browser says nothing of those dropped, which the log names (`import.dropped`); one put aside under the other development mode is not counted and is offered, and its review lets it go saying why. Each review the browser opens logs `import.review.opened` (the proposal's id, plug-in, function, film and viewer, whether *Review* asked for it and whether it was put aside) and `import.review.closed` (the id, how it closed and how long it was open); a *Review* that opens nothing logs `import.review.ignored` with why (`none_waiting`, `reviewing`). `showImportReview` answers how the review closed — applied, discarded, put aside, or dismissed, every other road out — and a proposal dismissed or gone is never put aside. The review (`import.review`, under the title *Import from …*, naming the plug-in, found in the catalogue by the tag its door passes) says first how many changes it proposes, that nothing is left to change, or that nothing in it can be written, then lists the document's problems as sentences and **each change** (`import.change:<id>`, the versionless id): its type and the page's name — the version in use's, a creation's own, a creation without a name its tag with *(new)* (*Scene · 8 (new)*), refused or not —, what it does in words (*Updates your version sc_1_ann_v1.*, in development mode another author's project file *Updates bob's version …, which development mode lets you write.*, *Forks bob's version into a new version of yours, which you then use.*, *Forks the library's version into a new version of yours, which you then use.*, *Creates it as a new version of yours, which you then use.*, *Already as proposed: nothing to change.*, *Refused:* and why — a new entry of a `children` list that names neither a scene nor a folder named as written (an entry that is no string in JSON), *An entry in this list is not a scene or a folder: ch_kira*, while a `children` of another shape as a whole keeps the shape sentence —; a scene or a folder created *is filed where the proposal lists it* where a project's or a folder's change of the same import, not refused, lists it in its `children`, and otherwise *lands unlisted, under Not in the script, until you place it*), its notes as sentences — the version exported no longer in use, changed since the export, a field changed since the export on both sides, no longer in the export's scope, another author's project file written in development mode, an era the project change declares (*Declares the era The war (1943).*, without a year *Declares the era The war.*) and one whose label the project already has (*The era The war is already declared; it is left as it is.*), the era's token unsaid —, and each changed field by its label — a key with no label as written, a list's entry by the value it is found by — with its new value and *Was: …*, a field removed as *Removed*; **a value reads as words** (`import_review_text.dart`, `importReviewText`): a text keeps its lines without its Markdown marks — a heading's `#`, `**` and `__`, a `*` or `_` paired around a word (never one inside a word, as in `old_coast`, nor one between spaces, as in `2 * 3`), backticks, a closed fence's own lines (a ```json body that parses reads the way an object and a list read here, under no word *json*; a body that does not parse, or of another language, keeps its lines as any text's; a fence not closed reads as text), a table's separator row —, a list's `-` or `*` as `•` and a table's cells apart (*Kira · the truth*); an object is one *Label: value* line per key, a key with no label as written, nested values indented; a list one entry to a line, objects apart by a blank line, so a scene's blocks read one after another and none as JSON; every key and value stays represented, and a long value stays inside the list, the buttons kept on screen; where a field's new value and what it held read the same as words but differ as stored — a change only in Markdown marks, `**Before**` over `Before` — both are shown as stored, marks included, a value that is no text as its JSON, so the change *Apply* writes is seen; the notes on the document as a whole are folded under one row. Every code reads as a sentence (`importProblemText`, `importNoteText`; the three refusals of *Apply* in `writeProblemText`), an unknown one as one generic sentence; no code, exception text or path reaches the screen. Each proposal shown is logged once as it is first drawn — opened, replaced under its id, or replaced by an *Apply* — as `import.review.shown` (the cause, the id, the plan's state and, per change, its id, action, paths, removals, each path's values before and after as they are, the words shown and whether they were shown as words or as stored, and ms). Nothing in a proposal is edited here. **Apply** (`import.apply`, 44 px) calls `applyImportProposal` with the proposal as shown and nothing else, and is held, saying why, unless the plan's state is *proposed*, while it runs, and while another import is applied (`importApplyRunning`, read as the review rebuilds); **Discard** (`import.discard`) lets the proposal go and writes nothing (*Import discarded. Nothing more was written.*), and is held while *Apply* runs, since it cannot stop writes already started; **Later** (`import.later`, between the two) closes the review with the proposal held as it is — what an earlier *Apply* completed included — writing nothing and saying nothing, since the control at the bottom of the page then shows it, and is held while *Apply* runs, with its own reason. The review re-reads the proposal on each notification of the seam and each change of the film or the settings, and after each *Apply*: a proposal replaced under its id is shown as it is now, and one gone closes the review with its cause — the film it was made in no longer open, the username changed, development mode changed, or taken away elsewhere; a notification during its own *Apply* waits for the answer. **The answers**: the plan found moved redraws the review (*The film changed since you reviewed this import, and nothing was written.*), each update of the viewer's own version whose file changed since saying *You edited it meanwhile*; written whole, the review closes and a message says how many changes were written, the page having opened what was written (§7.1) — naming the version it moved to (*…; sc_2_ann_v1 is open.*) where the *Apply* moved the page; with any change refused, the review stays with each change's outcome in place of what it does — *Written.*, *Written as …, now in use.*, or why not, a file edited since the review said as the person's own edit only for an update of their own version whose file shows one, else *The file could not be confirmed as written; Apply again shows what is left.* — over what is left to do, and **Apply again** (`import.retry`) on the same rule; a refusal before anything was written is said inside the review (`import.failure`) — a draft's failure with no sentence of its own as *See the page's save status*, never its text, and a draft's refusal with the way out, since the review covers the page (*choose Later, settle the page, then reopen the import with Review at the bottom of the page.*) —, except a proposal dropped, which closes it with its cause. There is no other way out but these: Escape and the system's back are *Later*, except while *Apply* runs.
 - **The visual treatment** (`filmopen_forms/style_card.dart`, `visualStyleSections`) is, on a folder, the last card before the plug-ins' (Project Specification §8.7, A.11); **on the project the *Style* stands in *General* and every other leaf in subsections of *Other*, folded** — **Camera** (*Camera*, *Camera mount*, *Lens*, *Lens character*, *Camera character*, *Depth of field*, *Colour grading*, *Look · Stock*, *Depth of field scale*, the grain), **Look** (*Description*, *Mood*, *Medium*, *Palette*, the colour temperature, the look's halation, contrast, saturation, sharpness and vignette), **Light and composition** (*Lighting*, *Composition*, *Texture*) and **Grading** (the grade's shadows, midtones and highlights), each a folded section (`visual_style.camera`, `.look`, `.light`, `.grading`) that Compare shows as any other, the boxes and chips as below. On a folder the card holds first **the *Style*** (`PromptBuilderField`, `filmopen_picker`) — the effective style's thumbnail and title, the folder's own else what it inherits, or *Select Style* where none is chosen or the chosen one is not found (nothing repairs it); a tap, or Enter, opens the popup — each enabled style builder's name and its categories beside the open category's tiles (a row above them under 600 px), the chosen tile outlined — and a tile's full-screen icon opens the full view: the full picture, or the thumbnail with *The picture could not be shown.*, the prompt read only, *Copy* (the button then reads *Prompt copied*), *Use this* and *Close*; on a folder that sets none, *Inherited from …* under it; locked in *View*; held, saying why, where no style can be read; written only on a pick, as the folder's own, never on opening or closing, and never typed (it has no setter); then **the five primary leaves**, each a box with suggested words (`EditableComboBox`, `filmopen_ui`) — *Camera* (*High-end digital cinema camera (ARRI ALEXA 35)*, *Consumer SLR (Canon)*, *Cell phone camera (iPhone)*, *35mm film camera*, *VHS camera*, then *Other*), *Camera mount* (*Tripod*, *Dolly*, *Handheld*), *Lens* (*28mm*, *35mm*, *50mm*, *85mm*), *Depth of field* (*f/1.4 very shallow*, *f/2.8 shallow*, *f/4.5 medium*, *f/8 deep*, *f/11 very deep*) and *Colour grading* (*Natural*, *Cinematic warm*, *Cool blue*, *Teal and orange*, *Muted/desaturated*, *High contrast*, *Black and white*, *Vintage film*), the words in the person's language where it has them. **The editable combo box** takes any words typed, as typed, and never makes them one of its words; its arrow, after the box's microphone (`<field>.suggestions`), named for its box (*Suggestions for Camera*) and saying to a screen reader whether its list is open, opens a list as wide as the box — the keyboard reaching the arrow by Tab after the box, the list opening on the words the box holds where they are offered, else on the first, Escape closing it with nothing written; Down or Alt+Down in the box opens it too, and a click back in the box closes it — and a word chosen (`<field>.suggestion.<n>`, from 1) fills the box as typing would, through the field's own change; *Other* (`<field>.other`) puts the caret in the box after what it holds — on a desktop and the web too, where a one-line box selects all of its words as it takes the focus — and writes nothing; a box read only has no arrow. Each leaf is written on its own, never as one prompt, and a box emptied removes its leaf; *Camera*, whose words run longest, has a row of its own, the other four two to a row where the form is. Under the card's title a line says what the five are for — each goes into the prompt of every picture and clip rendered, after the entity's own words — and where the rest went (*under Advanced treatment, below*), and that an empty box takes what is above it; the project's five have no such card or line. A box whose file holds a number, written by hand, shows it and says the treatment takes none of it (*Written as a number…*) — on a folder, that what is above it applies; where the box may be typed in, that words replace it — the reader taking words alone. **A folder's detailed leaves are folded under *Advanced treatment***, a card of its own (`form.visual_style.advanced`), closed on every entry to a version — each a box or a row of chips on its dotted path: *Description*, *Mood*, *Medium*, *Look · Stock*, the look's grain, halation, contrast, saturation, sharpness and vignette as chips, *Palette* one `#rrggbb` a line (at most eight; a folder's list replaces the one it would inherit; a list that is not one is said under the box — *Each line a colour as #rrggbb, eight at most…* — and not written, as a number box's text that is no number is not), the colour temperature as chips, *Lens character*, the depth of field's scale as chips (*Depth of field scale*, told from the words of the primary *Depth of field*), *Camera character*, *Lighting*, *Composition*, *Texture*, the grade's shadows, midtones and highlights. **The treatment's prompt is a Prompt card of its own** (above, under *View / Edit*), after the primary leaves' card and before *Advanced treatment* on a folder, after the media on the project, never folded — `visual_style.prompts.treatment.positive`, `.negative`, the writers of `treatment` (section `prompt.treatment`); a folder's card is the folder's own prompt, and shows none it would inherit. **On a folder, a leaf it leaves out shows what it inherits** (`treatmentFor(…, withOwn: false)`, the script as the viewer reads it, so a fork of a folder they picked is the one that passes it on): the value as the box's hint, in italics under a floated label, and where it comes from as its help — *Inherited from the project.*, *Inherited from Season 1.* — and a row of chips the value too (*Inherited: Fine, from the project.*); in *View* a locked box's hint is the dash, so its help alone says where; a folder whose place in the script is ambiguous says, under the card's title, that no folder above it passes on its treatment; a row whose file holds a word it does not know shows it chosen, as written, and says that the treatment takes none from it. A leaf emptied is inherited again. **Clear local overrides** (`style.clear`), on the title row of a folder's card — its words, on a line of their own where the title leaves them no room beside it, and its icon, with its tooltip, only on a card too narrow for them — removes its profile through the draft, its pictures kept, so that it inherits every leaf (*The folder's own treatment is removed: it inherits the treatment again.*); it is held, saying why, where the folder sets none or the form may not write.
 - **The voice panel** (`filmopen_forms/voice_panel.dart`, `VoicePanel`, `voice:<stem>`), under a character's *Voice samples*, shows the character's voice (Project Specification §9.1): for each platform `voice.provider_bindings` names, the sample the voice was kept from, playable, and *Speech on … uses the voice kept from this sample.* — or, where no sample names it, that speech uses a voice no sample here names — and, where the sample `voice.active_reference` marks as in use was kept as another voice, that speech keeps its own, in the tertiary colour; then **the previews to audition**, each a voice sample whose binding names a preview and no voice, live and not archived: its tile, played where it is tapped, its border and its lines as a place's tile's, and ***Use this voice*** (`voice:<stem>:preview:<n>.keep`). *Use this voice* asks first — *Keep this voice?*, saying that the platform keeps it as a voice of the account, which may take one of its voice slots — true of a platform that takes a slot and of one whose design already made the voice —, with the voice's name there (`voice.keep.name`, the character's name to start, at most 100 characters) and *Keep* (`voice.keep.confirm`, 44 px) — then keeps it through the plug-in whose render made the preview, the description it was designed from sent with it, and writes the choice into the file in one change (`withVoiceKept`, through the draft, the Voice section's boxes re-seeded): the sample's binding given the voice, `voice.provider_bindings`, `voice.active_reference`, and `voice.audition_text` as the preview's record holds it — and no prompt; a save whose record names no words names the character's `prompts.voice.positive`. **Recordings to keep**, where a design model keeps a recording — a catalogue entry of kind `voice` with `capabilities.keeps_recording`, with an access row on the platform of an enabled, runnable, consented plug-in's `voice` interface that serves it (an interface naming no models serves any there): each voice sample with no binding, live and not archived — an upload — its tile, and ***Use this recording*** (`voice:<stem>:recording:<n>.keep`), the offer logged once per display as `voice.record.offered` with the models, plug-ins, platforms and prices. It asks the same question, adding the price — *Keeping this recording costs … on ….* from the chosen model's single `clone` price on that platform, else *…'s price for this is not known here.*, since a row without one is unknown, never free —, a pull-down of the models where several keep it (`voice.keep.model`), and *What it says (optional)* (`voice.keep.words`), which nobody transcribes; each wording the question shows is logged as `voice.keep.wording`, and a question closed without keeping — *Cancel*, Escape or a tap outside, for a preview's or a recording's — as `voice.keep.cancelled` with the name, words and model it held and how long it was open, nothing sent or written. It then keeps the recording through that model's plug-in — `saveVoice` with the model, the sample's reference, the name and the voice prompt or else the name — and writes it in the same one change, the sample bound on the model's platform, its words, where given, on the sample and on `voice.audition_text`; a failed write keeps it with *Retry write*, as a preview's. A save the plug-in could not make says the plug-in's own words under the previews and writes nothing; one whose plug-in is not on says so; one the application refused before anything was sent says the voice was not kept; and one whose answer was lost, cut short or of another shape once the request may have gone says that nobody knows whether the platform kept it, to check its voices before keeping it again — never that it failed. **A save acknowledged whose write fails keeps its voice** on the panel — *Kept on …, but not yet written to this file.*, in the error colour, with ***Retry write*** (`voice:<stem>:kept:<n>.retry`), which writes the same change again and sends nothing — until the file holds it, however it came to. A save acknowledged after its page was left is written into the file it was kept for all the same. While a voice is being kept every button of the panel waits, saying so; on a version the viewer may not write, *Use this voice* and *Use this recording* are disabled, saying to keep a voice on one's own version.
 - **Copy-left** (`tabs/compare_copy.dart`, `filmopen_format/copy_left.dart`) — **both ways**: everything below about `<` into the left holds of `>` into the right, the format's rules being asked of the receiving column and the column copied from (`CopyDirection`). Each direction describes its rows from the column it copies **from**, and the other's field only where that column has no such row (the one copy that deletes): so `{custom: {x: 1}}` on the left against `{custom: {}}` on the right offers `>` and not `<`. A moved block's row at the source's place copies nothing — the row out for `<`, the row in for `>` — and a block's insert lands after the nearest block **of the receiving column** above its row, a row *Show changes only* hides included, found in one pass over the section's rows. A `>` is drawn only where the right column may be written; elsewhere there is no `>` and no box for it, and an agent's tap on one finds nothing (`not_found`). The controls are `compare.copy-row:<row>`, `compare.copy-section:<card>` and `compare.copy-all` for `<`, `compare.copy-row-right:<row>`, `compare.copy-section-right:<card>` and `compare.copy-all-right` for `>`, the chevrons pointing the way they copy. The answers are cached per direction by both drafts and both savers' revisions, so an edit in either column is seen by both. In detail, for `<`: a `<` in the gutter of every row that has something to copy, a `<<` on each section card's title row over the same gutter (`SectionCard.titleRow` lays the title out on the rows' grid, so the `<<` sits on the split and not at the card's right edge) and a `<<<` over the split in the header, drawn as one, two or three overlapping chevrons so the three read as one gesture at three scales. A row offers a `<` when the **right** version has a value at one of the row's paths that the left does not match, compared by content rather than by key order. **A copy never removes a key from the left**, and four things would: a key only the left has; an empty value of any kind — `null`, an object or a list with nothing in it, a string with nothing but space — which everywhere else in the app is how a key is deleted; a path that runs through a value the two versions disagree about the *shape* of, where writing the leaf would replace the left's list or sentence with a fresh object; and an object whose visible remainder is empty, which is on screen only so that nothing of the file disappears. None of them offers a `<` at all — the JSON editor is the way through a disagreement that deep. The keys it will not move depend on the file (`uncopyableKeys(entity)`): the identity the filename owns, `created`, `updated`, `forked_from`, `version_label` (with `label`, its 1.5 spelling), `by` and `source`, this version's own provenance, `deleted`, its recycle-bin mark, and `discarded`, a version set aside (Project Specification §7), and a prompt's `created` and `plugin` (§9) — words copied are a person's: a left prompt whose words a copy changes loses its `plugin`, keeps its own `created` for the writer to stamp, and a block copied whole carries its prompts' words alone — in every file; `epoch` only where the filename carries one, since on a scene or an episode it is the story's epoch (§10.2), a row that copies like any other; `contributors` on a project file, the roster and its director roles, which change through the JSON editor and never through a copy; and `next_block` on a scene, its own count of the numbers it has given out; everything else a row shows may travel, and `<<<` is exactly the rows' own copies applied one after another, so nothing the screen did not offer is ever written — the Notes card is about the file, not a field of it, and carries no control. Where the source's value is empty and the receiving column's is not, the row's arrow is still drawn, disabled, saying *Nothing to copy: this is empty there*, since an empty value never replaces one (a block's own *delete* stays enabled); a disabled arrow is no operation and logs nothing. A nested object copies path by path, which is a subtree without ever dropping the sibling a field of the form owns (`appearance.age`), and a list copies whole. A scene whose `blocks` holds the ids a cue writes (§10.3) has no script to compare: both columns then show `blocks` as an ordinary value row and no block moves either way, because the two columns must answer that question **together** — one drawing a *Dialog and actions* section of block rows while the other draws a value row would leave both rows without a twin, and the empty-handed side would say the key is not in the other version while it plainly has it. **A scene's script is copied by block id** (§13.3's table): `<` replaces, inserts — where the block diff drew the row, after the nearest block above it that the left version has (`CopyBlock.placed`), or first — or deletes that block, the one row whose right side is absent that still carries a control; a moved block's *in* row replaces the viewer's block where it stands and its *out* row carries nothing, since copy-left has no move; `<<` and `<<<` replace and insert — an insert where `mergeOrder` draws it — but never delete, so one press cannot take away every line only the viewer has written, and a block with no id, or an id that names two blocks in one file, offers nothing. **A block's copy never lowers the number the scene's next block takes** (`applyCopy`; Project Specification §10.3): where an insert, a replace or a delete would let it fall — the highest block deleted, or a block inserted and deleted again — the copy writes `next_block`, so a number given out once never comes back under a cue or a shot that still names it. A scene's `next_block` itself is never copied (`uncopyableKeys`): no row offers it, and copy-all leaves the left's where it was. Copies are not confirmed (§13.3): they are written through the left column's own autosave, so the identity lock, the content check, the stale refusal and the 700 ms debounce apply unchanged, a whole card or a whole file is **one** write, and copying an equal value writes nothing. A copy that would break the file is refused before the draft is touched, because a draft cannot be unwritten: a project copy that would drop an epoch some file is at is refused naming the epoch and the way round it — copy the other rows one at a time — (`filmopen_format/epochs.dart`'s `epochsDroppedInUse` over `FilmProject.epochUsage`, the same question the epochs editor asks of one row; a file is at an epoch its filename carries, the story epoch its content names, its `location_epoch`, and every epoch a cast or props entry of it names, each counted once per file), and so is anything `validateContent` would reject, in the writer's own words.
