Each row's answer is kept until the left draft (its identity and revision) or the right one changes, so a rebuild that changes neither — a theme, a resize — asks no row again; `FormPane` indexes its sections and fields and `mergeOrder` is linear, since nothing bounds the blocks of a scene. One message names what moved. A copy is logged as `compare.copy.start {stem, from, direction, how, row, kind, after, block_case, values: {into_before, from}}` — its inputs, taken before anything can refuse it, `stem` the column written and `direction` `left` or `right` — then `compare.copied` with the same, the keys and `ms`, or `compare.copy.refused {stem, direction, row, reason, ms}` (`nothing_to_copy`; `epoch_in_use` with the first epoch and every one `dropped`; `invalid_content` with the refusal's `code`, its type, and the writer's `message`). Where the project cannot be written, or the file is the library's, every control is still drawn over what differs and every one of them is disabled with the reason. Where either column that writes is the version official names, the §13.2 banner sits above the rows as it does on the page — a copy is an edit, and it changes what everyone sees — and its Fork forks **the left version**, after writing what a copy left pending; the version row above the tabs forks the page's version, which is the right column, so a banner over an official right column has no Fork of its own. A column that takes no typing shows its save status once a copy has given it something to write, so a failed write of a copy into a scene or a location is shown with its way out.
 - **Other attributes in Compare** — the trailing card is a section like any other here, one paired row per key, because the keys no field names — on a project the contributors, the tracks and the script's `children` — are shown here and a `<` needs a row to sit on (View/Edit hides `children` on a project, `entityDataSpec`'s `hiddenKeys`, since the tree's Script branch is where the script is read; Compare keeps its row, which is how one project version takes another's script); the tracks and `children` copy, and the contributors never do (the roster is the project file's own, and its row offers no control). The rows carry the paths the card actually shows, so copying a character's `appearance` moves what is in that card and not the age the form holds.
 - **When Compare cannot open** — (enforcing; in development mode the tab opens on the same reason as a red line, §9.5) the tab is greyed with a tooltip saying why: the selected version *is* the viewer's latest, so there is nothing to compare it with; there is no username in Settings; or the project is read-only (or the file is the library's) and they have no version of their own. A greyed tab stays hoverable so its tooltip shows, and a tap on it puts the page back where it was. When the only thing missing is a version of their own, the tab looks open and asks instead (`tabs/compare_fork_dialog.dart`): *You don't have a working version to compare against. Would you like to fork one?* **Yes** pins the selection to the version on screen — without that the new fork becomes the version in use and the page would re-resolve onto it, disabling the tab it was asked to open — then forks that version without navigating, and opens Compare once the re-read makes it openable. **No** goes back to the file's own data, wherever Compare was tapped from. A refusal is shown inside the dialog, and the buttons are dead while the write is in flight, because a fork is not idempotent. The tabs have an explicit controller (`detail/entity_tabs.dart`) rather than a `DefaultTabController`: it also carries the selected tab across a change of the tab set, falls back to the first tab in the same frame when the current one stops being openable, and refuses a swipe, which would change tabs without ever asking.
 - **Takes** — every take discovered for any version of the entity, as cards: the one tile media is drawn with (`MediaTile`, `take:<take stem>`), square at the card's width, wearing its own batch's provenance strip and opening the viewer, above the caption — deliverable, take number, source version, batch chip (`take:<take stem>.batch`, opens the batch), renderer, path; "picked" marks takes the chosen version's `picks` name. Its border is drawn as a place's tile's is: always its record's stage; the review and the status only where the version shown places the take exactly once — the live entries naming it, in either spelling, across the version's groups — and otherwise none, *Placement status unavailable* in its tooltip.
 - **Commentary** — all authors' commentary consolidated: a Picks card, then entries newest first with rating icon, author, date, `on` chip, block/field chips, text.
- **Batch detail** — header chips (stem, author, kind, tier, status, created, item count, total cost) and a table of items; the source column links to the entity. `BatchDetail` also dispatches a pending set's record, and `PendingSelection`, a session's record aside (its review, below), to **the one set page** (`filmopen_browser/sets/set_list_page.dart`, `SetListPage`), the same for a comp, an assistant session and a render queue: a header with the type's icon and the title (the comp's title, *Pending comp*, *Pending render batch*), chips for the stem, status, author, created and item count, the primary action the type supplies (none until the type's own page gives one), and the items, each drawn by the type's row builder. **The × removes an item only on the viewer's current pending record of its type** (`project.pendingOf(type, viewer)`) — never on an older pending record, another author's, a posted or a closed one — through `PendingSets.remove`, the outcome a message. Every empty page says what fills it: *Add media to a comp from any medium's menu.*, *Start the assistant to collect a session.*, *Add to batch from the render dialog.* It logs `set.page.open {type, stem, status, items, removable, reason}` and `set.page.remove.requested {type, stem, n}`.
- **An assistant session's review** — a session's record (its row in the tree's *Chats*, the open one's included) opens on its own page, `SessionPage` (`filmopen_compare`), not the set page: a header — over it, what the chat was asked (`session.origin`), the record's `action` by its label in the record's own assistant's catalog, else its id, and for a field's or a form section's action `: ` and the entity's name, else its id (*Edit character: Julius*), none on a record that names no action (the origin and each fallback's reason on `session.review.open`'s `origin`); then *The assistant's changes* and *Your current picks* side by side as the two columns' headings, the summary under them — drawn from the items, not the record's own `summary`: one mention per stem, *Created* where any of its items is a `create`, else *Edited*, the project as *Wrote Project Summary*, a patch sharing a create's `operation` (its listing) left out, grouped by verb and type in order of first appearance, named as the tree names them (a stem with no version left by its stem), the type's word plural where several, the groups apart by semicolons: *Wrote Project Summary; Created characters Julius, Gabriela, Adrian; Edited character Kira* (a later edit of a stem whose first item was a listing patch is not mentioned, its row still on the page) — then **Pick all** (`session.pick-all`) and **Discard all** (`session.discard-all`) beside *Show changes only* — that stays above the rows while the pane is tall enough for both and scrolls with them below that height (the same height as Compare's headings), and under it, per item in order of `n`, a rule, a title row (the type's icon, the name in use — `groupName`, the last-updated version's where every version is discarded —, the item's `summary`, a **Pick** over each column that holds a file — `compare[<n>].pick-left`, and `compare[<n>].pick-right` for a fork's base — and **Discard** `compare[<n>].discard`) and the Compare form embedded, its height its content's, every identifier under `compare[<n>]` — each column's cells built under an `IdentifierScope` (`filmopen_ui`) of `compare[<n>].left` or `.right`, which every control in them reads through `scopedId` (fields, relationships, cast, garments, media tiles with their actions and their jobs' buttons, voice, analysis, a scene's clear buttons, the prompt card, a style's clear button, the *Other* card), so a patch's two columns of one stem and two items of one stem are each their own; outside a scope an identifier is unchanged. An item whose version the folder no longer holds shows *This version is not in the project.* with its buttons and no comparison; **a period the chat declared** (`kind: epoch`, §7.4) has a row of its own — the project's icon, *Period: The war · 1943* (its label, its year after it, else its token), the line *Declared by this chat. Discard removes it, or gives back its earlier name, where nothing is set at it.*, its Discard and no Pick and no comparison —, its answer *Removed from the project's periods.*, *Given back its earlier name.* or *Kept: something in the film is set at this period.*, and the summary leaves it out; *Discard all* publishes the film once, at its end, so the page takes the pass in one snapshot; a record with no items says *Start the assistant to collect a session.* **The conversation** follows the items, read-only, where the record keeps `messages` (`session.history`, and none on a record from before them): under *Conversation*, the person's words under *You* and the assistant's under *Assistant* (`session.history.<n>`), and each tool call a closed `DisclosureCard` named by the call (`session.history.<n>.<call id>`) that holds its arguments and the tool's answer, wrapped; an empty history says *No words yet: the conversation appears here as the chat goes on.* On a live chat's page the section is the record as last written, the dock beside it live. Opening or closing a call is logged `session.history.toggle {stem, n, call, name, open}`, and `session.review.open` carries `messages` as the record holds them, and `messagesUnread: not a list` where the member is no list, which shows no section. **The left column** is the version the session wrote, editable as Compare's left is — Discard sets it aside as it then is, an edit written there included; **the right** is, for a `fork`, its recorded base, whatever is in use now, so the pair stays put while the person picks back and forth; for a `patch`, the version as it stood before the session, read from the item's `before`, locked, never copied into and with no Pick, since it is no file (*Before this session*); for a `create`, nothing — the left's rows beside a blank column with no heading, under *Show changes only* only its rows that hold a value, a block's included, a card with none hidden, logged at each build (`compare.filter {stem_left, stem_right: null, changesOnly, created: true, hidden, hidden_sections, ms}`). Each item's rule and title row stay under the header while its comparison scrolls under them, until the next item's push them off (`PinnedSection`, `filmopen_ui`: Flutter's `SliverMainAxisGroup` and `PinnedHeaderSliver` in one `CustomScrollView`, the items keyed, so that a form keeps its controllers, text, selection and focus across a scroll and across the height line; below `Breakpoints.headingsStayPut` the titles scroll with the rows, as the header does). The item's name is a link to the object's page (`compare[<n>].open`), through `browserProvider.navigate` as the tree opens it, logged `session.item.open.start {stem, item, from, to}` then `session.item.open {stem, item, from, to, now, ok, ms}`, a failure with its type and error, said on the page; a version no longer in the folder has no link. **A Pick** is the version row's check with its word, the check left of *Pick*: grey, and green with white text (black on the dark theme's lighter green) where `pickAt` resolves to its version — plain official included, which carries no check on the version row —, so that one of an item's two is green where either is in use, neither where a third is; *Pick all* is green where every item's left is. Every press goes through `SessionReview` (§7.4), which decides: Pick makes that column's version the one in use and decides nothing, so it stays pressable; Discard sets the version aside (never deletes), its tooltip saying it ends the chat; while one of an item's operations runs its Picks and its Discard are disabled with *Working…*, and the *all* buttons while any is; **on the open session's page with its chat still live**, a Discard, once any left draft is written, moves the page onto the record (`BatchSelection` of its stem: the same page goes on, where the pending page would fall to its empty state once the record is closed), then closes the session (`closeSession(id, 'reviewed')`, the record taking the items' words; the dock, told the close began, ends its chat and joins it), then reviews, *Working…* through it all; a discarded item's are disabled with *Already decided.* (a legacy `accepted` item reads as open); a patch's Discard is offered as every item's is, the review deciding what it writes (§7.4). Before an item's Discard the left column's draft is written (`DraftSaver.settle`), and a write that fails stops it there, said on the row. Each answer is said on its item's row (`compare[<n>].answer`) in words (`sessionReviewAnswerText`) — an *all* pass's skip of a decided item keeping what an earlier press said, its skip of a version gone or discarded meanwhile saying so —, and a `stale` one offers *Re-read files* (`compare[<n>].reread`). It logs `session.review.open {stem, viewer, items: [{n, kind, left, right, right_reason: base|before|none, outcome}], kinds, ms}`, per press `session.review.press {stem, item, verb, how, side?}`, the move `session.review.move {stem, from, to, moved, reason: record closes|page left}` — a page the person left while a draft was written is not brought back, and the session closes all the same — and the close `session.review.close {stem, session, live, reason, outcome: closed|still-live|not-live, ms}`, before a Discard `session.review.settle {stem, item, flushed, outcome: proceed|refused, reason?, ms}`, each answer `session.review.answer {stem, item, verb, how, side?, outcome, code?, kept_earlier, ms}`, `session.review.reread.start` and `session.review.reread {stem, item, read, ms}`, and an unexpected failure `session.review.failed {stem, item, verb, how?, stage, type, error, ms}` (`verb: reread` for a re-read), said in a message, beside the review's own lines.
 - **The text boxes on a session's review are dormant.** `SessionPage` marks its list with `DormantFields` (`filmopen_forms`), which sets `FormScope.dormant`; every other page, an ordinary comparison included, draws its fields live. A dormant field (`DormantField`) is its text in a box that looks like the field — the field's own decoration and lines, its label, hint, locked fill, microphone and (for a box with suggestions) arrow, its chips by the one helper the editor uses (`proseChip`, §9.8), clipped beyond the editor's maximum lines — and holds no `EditableText`. A click, a tap or a right-click turns that one box into its editor in place, the caret at the point clicked; Tab or keyboard focus reaching it opens it with the caret at the end; the locked column's box opens its read-only editor. Leaving the field, Tab or Escape (after any menu of the field's own) turns it back; the window going to the background does not, so the focus returns as it was. The form's controller and draft stay as they are, so the autosave is untouched: nothing is written on leaving. Pull-downs, chips and checkboxes are unchanged, and the boxes with editors of their own that this does not wrap (an epoch's label, which keeps its own focus rule; the script's rows, a frame's prompt, an outfit's prose, a voice's and a plug-in's fields) stay live. The box's text is laid out by the render object the editor uses, so it wraps and places its chips as the editor does. A box's Semantics carries its field's identifier and its value, each mention by its name; an agent's `tap` opens it and `set_field` writes through the scope. Logged: `form.box.activate {stem, key, value, by: tap|focus|agent, column, localOffset?}`, `form.box.open {stem, key, displayOffset, by, column, ms}` or `form.box.refused {stem, key, value, by, column, reason: cancelled|gone|not_focused, ms}`, `form.box.close {stem, key, value, reason: blur|tab|escape|dispose, openMs}`, `form.box.edit {stem, key}`; and `fields`, the text boxes the items seed, on `session.review.open`.
- **The pending render batch** (`filmopen_browser/sets/queue_page.dart`, §6.15) — the queue record's page on `SetListPage`: *Render* (primary, 44 px, `queue.render`) with *Render this batch from one app at a time* under it, *Finish the interrupted pass* (`queue.finish`) on an own record a pass left `submitting`, and *Your other batches waiting:* with *Open* (`queue.other:<stem>`); one row per item — the place's name in use with its group or block, the model, platform, stage, frozen price and when it was added — and its state on `queue.item:<stem>:<n>`, with that state's actions (`.mark`, `.readd`, `.job.<action>`, `.record`, `.place`); the × (`set:<stem>:<n>.remove`) on never-submitted rows of the viewer's current pending batch. Rows and header wrap; nothing overflows at 375 px.
- **Comps** — *Add to comp* is an injected item of every placed medium's menu (`MediaEntryActions`; `filmopen_browser/sets/comp_action.dart`), held by the browser page while it is mounted. It appends `{n, reference, batch?, item?, source, deliverable?, added}` to the viewer's pending comp through `PendingSets.add`, creating the record where none is pending — the source and the purpose the take's, else its record's, else the version it is placed on and the purpose an upload of its kind takes there — and says *Added to the pending comp* with *Open* (`comp.pending.open`) to its page. It refuses, writing nothing, a reference already in the pending comp as decisions read it (`PendingSets.pending`, §7.5: an add a moment ago included, while its index is still being published) (*Already in the pending comp*), a medium without a file on this computer — an address, bytes held only in memory or in the bundle, as the web's sample — (*This medium has no file to upload*; the sample extracted on a desktop or Android build has its media on disk, which a comp takes), and the formats the comps service does not take, GIF, BMP, MOV and FLAC, read from the take's file (*A comp cannot take .gif files*). The comp is the viewer's own record, not the medium's version, so the item is not refused by the version's access (`gatedByEntityAccess` false) but disabled, its reason under its name, where the project may not be written (a read-only project shows no media menu at all) or there is no username (`noHandle`), and while this app posts a comp (`compPosting`, *Wait for the comp being posted*), since the comp being posted is no longer pending and an add would make a second pending comp beside it. The comp's page (`sets/comp_page.dart`, `CompPage`) is the set page with the comp's fields in its `header` slot, scrolling with the items: on the viewer's pending comp **Title** (`comp.title`, one line, 160 characters), **Description** (`comp.description`, 2,000) and *Publish this comp for the public* / *Keep it a private URL* (`comp.visibility.public`, `comp.visibility.private`) with *Unlisted · anyone with the link can view* under it — absent, the visibility reads private — each written through `PendingSets.commit` 700 ms after the last change, on leaving the page and on turning to another comp, a refused write said; an edit not yet written is an open draft (`OpenDrafts`), so closing the film and changing the username write it first, as the one who typed it, and a write whose record is no longer the viewer's pending comp is refused with nothing written, and said; the rows are `MediaTile`s, a tap opening the viewer, each with ×; **POST** (`comp.post`, a primary button, 44 px) is disabled, its tooltip the first reason that holds: *Add media to the comp first*, *Give the comp a title first*, *Sign in to post* (the account's click is the sign-in gesture), *This project is read-only here.*, *Posting…* while a post of this app runs, and it stays drawn, disabled, with *Posting…* on the record this app is posting. Pressed, it writes the fields first — where they cannot be written it posts nothing and says *Not posted: the comp's fields could not be written. Try again.*, in a message and inline (`comp.post.error`) on that comp until its next press, never on another comp the page turns to — then the record's status `posting`, the page following the record to its own page (`BatchSelection`), its fields locked, with *Posting…* and the publish's progress (`comp.posting`); then it prepares and publishes through `CompPoster` (§6.17). A receipt makes the record `posted` with `posted: {at, url, revision}`; a refusal or a failure makes it `pending` again with `error: {code, at, detail}`, said above the fields until the next post (`comp.post.error`: *The last post did not go through (code). Posting again starts a new post.*, `account-mismatch` in its own words). One post runs at a time: `compPostingProvider` holds the running post's stem for the app's run, from before `posting` is written until the post ends, so a post outlives its page, and a second press does nothing. A record of the viewer's found `posting` in a film that may be written, whose stem is not held there — a crash or a quit during the upload — is `pending` again when its page opens, with `error.code` `interrupted`, and says *The last post did not finish in this app. Posting again starts a new post.*; the service publishes a revision only once its upload is complete, and a new POST is a new revision. Another author's record found `posting`, or one in a film that may not be written, is left as it is. Another author's or an older pending comp, and one in a film that may not be written, is read in the locked look. A posted comp's page is read-only: its title, description, visibility and URL, with *Open in browser* (`comp.open_url`, a web address only, since the record is a project file another author may write) and *Share* (`comp.share`, the URL on the clipboard, then *Link copied*), a refusal said; no POST is offered on it. It logs `comp.add.start`, `comp.add.resolved {reference, path?, extension?, location, source, deliverable?}`, then `comp.add {stem, reference, batch?, item?, deliverable?, n, created, ms}` or `comp.add.refused {reference, reason, extension?, pending?, ms}`, an unexpected failure `comp.add.failed {reference, stage, code, error, ms}`, rethrown for the menu to say; `comp.add.unavailable {reference, reason: compPosting, posting}` as the menu asks while a post runs; `comp.page.open {stem, items, status, mine, editable, title, visibility, error?, stoppedCleared?}`, `stoppedCleared` the stem whose stopped POST the page stops saying; the page's `comp.post.start {stem, items, title, visibility, description, film, poster}` and `comp.post.step {stem, step, ms}` (the poster's lines of the same names carry a `stage`, §6.17) as `posting` is written (or a warning with the poster's unexpected error), `comp.post.refused {stem, reason: already-posting, running}` for a second press, `comp.post.failed {stem, code, error, ms}` where a record write is refused, and `comp.post.done {stem, outcome: posted|refused|failed, code?, url?, ms}`; `comp.post.posting {stem, running, own, canWrite, decision}` as a record found `posting` opens, then `comp.post.interrupted {stem, outcome: returned|unchanged, status?, ms}`, or a warning `{stem, outcome: failed, code, error, ms}` where the write is refused; per autosave `comp.field.save {stem, why, changed, pending?}` and `comp.field {stem, field, value}`, the values as typed, or `comp.field.refused {stem, field, reason: record-changed, pending?, values, ms}`, `values` the three fields as the refused save held them; `comp.field.written {stem, written}` should another record be written; `comp.draft.settle {stem, clean, dirty, failed, ms}` as a close or a change of username settles the page's edit, a refused write answered unclean once; `comp.page.access {stem, editable, reason: viewer|film-access, mine, canWrite}`; `comp.open_url.start {stem, url, web}`, `comp.share.start {stem, url}`, then `comp.open_url` and `comp.share {stem, url, outcome, reason?, code?, error?, ms}`.
- **A discarded version's page** (Project Specification §7) opens with a notice, *This version is discarded* — nobody uses it, and it is still its author's to edit — with no action: nothing brings a discarded version back (§7.4's one door only sets aside). The star is drawn unlit there, and the official-edit banner is not shown, since a pointer naming a discarded version counts as none. The version pull-down lists the author's discarded versions greyed, after a divider where retained versions of theirs are above, so it is never blank on a discarded page, and its check is unlit where the viewer's pick names a discarded version. A refused discard of an official version or of the project file of record says *the director moves or clears the star first*; a reference nothing resolves — no version, or every one discarded — says *No version of "…" is in use here*.
- **Other files** — one list of files the project loader does not understand, including misplaced documents, nested files and non-JSON companions. The page reports their paths and never changes them.

**A pick the project overtook** (§13.2, §13.4): when `Pick.staleSince` is set for the viewer at the epoch on screen, a `StalePickBanner` sits above the header on every tab of that entity — where `PageAccess.canPick` holds, so never on a plug-in, whose old pick chooses nothing, nor on a page whose pick could not be written — the error-container card with the sentence *Yes, abandon this* (the pick and its date are removed, the viewer follows official, and — as the question says, *go to the official* — the page moves to the official version and the message names it; where there was no pick of the viewer's own to abandon, nothing is written and the message says so) and *No, keep this as my pick* (the pick, in use before and after, is re-dated, so it is no longer stale and the banner goes). Both go through `WriteActions`, so a refusal is a message and changes nothing, and both are followed by the re-read; the buttons wrap at a narrow width.

**A version in the recycle bin** (Project Specification §7; `entity_detail.dart`'s `_DeletedBanner`): when the version on screen carries `deleted: true`, a card in the error container's colours sits above the header on every tab — after the guard lines and the stale-pick banner — saying the version is deleted, that whoever uses it has no such entity, and that the file is kept; with **Restore** (`entity.restore`) and, under it, its last answer (`entity.restore.said`). Restore is `restoreEntity` (`filmopen_state/entity_restore.dart`), one rule with two doors, ownership read from the filename's author and never from development mode's `PageAccess.editable`: **the person's own file** is saved through the controller's own write with the `deleted` key removed — never set to `null`, which the format does not allow —, the open drafts settled first and the writer's stale check kept, the person's draft and a v1 the assistant created included, which are theirs and stay unpicked; **any other file** — another author's, an older `<owner>.ai` version included, the library's — is forked as the person's own with the key removed (`ProjectController.fork(…, content:)`), which picks it, and nothing of another author's is written. After the fork's re-read the fork is checked to be the person's pick; where it is not, that is said as a long message, which outlives the card the re-read may take away (*Restored as your new version …, but your pick of it could not be written: pick … to make it the version you use.*, which says nothing of the version the page shows — a page opened on the deleted version's own stem still shows it), and under the card where it is still there; nothing is retried, forked again or repaired. Every answer is said on the messenger and the container retained before the write, since the re-read can rebuild the page. Its tooltip says which door it is; without a username, or on a project that cannot be written, it is disabled with the reason as its tooltip. A restore in place says *Restored.*; a fork says *Restored as your new version …, now in use.* and the page moves to it; a refusal (`RestoreRefused`: a draft that could not be written, the file gone, the version no longer deleted) or the writer's refusal stays under the card, as a sentence (`restoreRefusalText`, `writeProblemText`). *Use the version before* is not offered: no history of earlier picks is kept. Its log: `entity.restore.start {stem, path: own | fork}`, `entity.restore.stage {stem, stage: settle | write | fork | refresh | pick, outcome, ms}` — the write's or the fork's outcome its own result, then the controller's re-read after it as `refresh` with its own outcome and time, a refused write logged `failed` once, a pick not found after the re-read `not-picked` with `reason: no-own-pick | other-pick` — and `entity.restore {stem, fork?, path, outcome: restored | forked-picked | forked-not-picked | refused | failed, reason, stage?, type?, ms}` — a refusal with its `stage`, a failure with its `stage` and its `type` alone, a restore whose film was not re-read after the write `reason: not-reread` —, the stem as a digest.

**The media card in a list** (`filmopen_forms`' `MediaFormCard`, the Workflow tab's row): a version's media as one card that sits in a scrolling list rather than on a page of its own — the version's form state (`FormSide`) with one card and no page around it. An entity's card is its media card limited to the type's named places (`workflowMediaGroups`: a character's *Reference images*, *Character sheets* and *Voice samples*; an outfit's, a location's and a prop's *Reference images* and their sheets), each under its Prompt card, with no other place and no loose preview; `referenceMediaSection` and `MediaCard` take those `groups`, every page passing none and drawing every place as before. A scene's card lists its blocks in script order — those with an id the scene gives once —, each headed by its name and first words, with that block's places (`BlockMediaPlaces` — the Script page's own block places, lifted whole — given `workflowBlockGroups`: *First frames*, *Last frames*, *Clip*, and *Speech* where the block has a speaker), and no *Add a place…*. A card's controls name its version first (`<stem>.draft.retry`, `<stem>.draft.discard`), so several cards share a page; a card whose draft is dirty, saving or failed stays alive when its list scrolls it away, its failure and its way out still there when it comes back, and a clean one goes with nothing to write. **The place's checkbox**: under a `PlaceSelection` (`filmopen_insertion_ui`) — one notifier of checked places and the set offered — a `MediaPlace` that is offered and editable leads its title with a checkbox (`<place>.check`, its label the place's title); every other place, and every place with no selection above it, draws none.

### 9.4 Settings

Appearance (System / Light / Dark), Language (a pull-down drawn as the microphone's beside it is, `settings.language`: while FilmOpen is in development English is the one language shown (§10), and the control reads *English*, held, its tooltip saying *Other languages return after development.*, a press opening nothing; with more than one language shown it lists *System* first, chosen while no language is and keeping none — never the language it resolves to — then each language by its name in its own language, the chosen one checked), **Dictation** (`filmopen_dictation`'s `DictationSettingsCard`, §9.6: the one place the microphone every box records from is chosen — *Microphone*, a pull-down naming the one chosen, which asks the system for its microphones when it opens and never before; none on the web), Identity (signed in, the username chosen from the account's names, never typed — `AccountUsernameChoice` as the new-project director draws it (§9.2), `settings.handle` its identifier, the pull-down setting the owner alone, a workspace dropped — with the same refusal and *Try again*, and the claim of another; signed out, the username in a `ShortNameField` labelled *Username* that takes a workspace and allows an empty box — `default` its hint, shown at rest too, an emptied box staying empty while the username is `default` —, with helper text naming the username in effect and, for a text that is not a handle, why — `official` is reserved, one dot at most, a name on both sides of a dot —; under it, while the box holds a text that is not a handle, *Your username is still "{handle}".*; a change refused by an open page's draft, with *Try again* (`settings.handle.ask-again`), which asks the box's text again; and a username not saved, with *Try again* (`settings.handle.retry`). The box follows a username changed elsewhere — an agent, a dialog — and never one it asked for itself, so an answer that comes late never writes over what is being typed), **Shared library** (what it is; the folder in use with a show-in-file-browser button; *Choose folder…*; *Use default* when a folder was chosen), **Export encoder** (on Linux, Windows and macOS, where the dock exports; not on the web, Android or iOS: *Automatic* / *Software only*, `settings.export_encoder`, with the helper *Automatic uses a graphics-card encoder when one is available and works, otherwise the software encoder. Software only uses the software encoder.*; `Settings.exportEncoder`, `auto` or `software`, kept in the shared preferences as `exportEncoder`, an unknown stored value read as `auto` and logged `settings.export_encoder.unknown {stored, read_as}`; a choice logged `settings.export_encoder.start {old, new}`, then `settings.export_encoder {old, new, outcome, code, cause, ms}`, a value other than the two refused, after its `.start`, and logged `settings.export_encoder.refused {old, new, outcome, code: unknown_value, reason, ms}`; a choice the preferences could not keep holds for the session, marked in `Settings.unsaved` until a write of it succeeds, and the card says under its helper *This choice could not be saved: it holds until FilmOpen closes.* — no *Try again*, the next choice writing it again; the dock's MP4 export asks for it, §19), **Logs** (what is logged; the *Verbose logs* checkbox; the log file's path and a *View log* button that shows it in the file browser; on the web, a line saying entries go to the console), **Development** (only in a build that has development mode: what it is, and the *Warn instead of refuse* checkbox with a line naming the three rules it governs), About (the build's stamp, §6.6a, selectable; specification version, URL, the preferences folder with a show button, *Open-source notices* → Flutter's licence page, which includes the vendored API Dash licence).

**Remote control** (`filmopen_relay`'s `RemoteControlCard`, §6.6c) follows *Identity* on General where the root made a relay client — not on the web, nor in a test: the switch *Let my chat app use this device* (`settings.remote`), disabled with the tooltip *Sign in to use Remote control.* while nobody is signed in, or on a build that registers no device — a switch left on stays turnable off, and a signed-in device that a refusal left unregistered can turn it on, the name's claim registering it again; under it the state — *Off* (the switch off, whatever socket lending holds), *Connecting…*, *Connected*, *Stopped (<code>)* or *Another copy of this device took over* — and the device's name, *This device's name: <slug>*, or *This device has no name yet: Remote control asks for one.* — with the switch on on a registered device without a name, as after it was forgotten on the website, *Turn the switch off and on to name this device.*; a stop with `403` or `4003` reads *The website no longer admits this device: check its Devices tab, then sign out and in again.*; while connected with a name, *Connector address* and `https://<kFilmOpenDomain>/v1/mcp/<username>/<slug>`, selectable, with a copy button (`settings.remote.copy`): *Address copied* once the clipboard took it, *The address could not be copied.* otherwise. A switch whose setting could not be saved says *This setting could not be kept: it lasts until FilmOpen closes.* Turning the switch on opens the device-name dialog first where the device has no name (§6.6b), and a cancel leaves it off. The card adds no device card to Settings.

**Sharing** (`filmopen_sharing`'s `FilmOpenSharingCard`, §6.10, §6.14) follows *Remote control* (or *Identity*) on General, at most 620 px wide. Under the title, the open film's name — or *Open a film to share it* where none is open; on a session copy *Copy the film to a folder first*, with no account line, and beside it only what belongs to no film — the step under way, a notice about no film in particular, and a sign-in under way, whose wait and *Cancel* stay whichever film is open; on the web *Sharing needs the desktop app* and nothing else. The state in one line (`sharing.state`): *Not shared*; *Shared on Google Drive, in FilmOpen/<name>*; *Shared to <path>*; or *Shared to a folder that this computer has not chosen*. Under it, while the film is shared, two independent sentences count its jobs from `syncJobsProvider`. The first appears only when it has send-kind jobs and counts files waiting to send, sent and needing a person; the second appears only when it has fetch-kind jobs and counts files waiting to receive, received and needing a person. Queued, working and waiting jobs are pending, done jobs are complete, failed and attention jobs need a person, and cancelled jobs add to none of those counts. A done fetch whose explicit outcome is `keptLocal` or `alreadyAgreed` adds nothing to the received total; missing or unknown outcomes retain the normal done count. The receive sentence still appears when these are the only fetch jobs. Counts describe final job outcomes, not an audit of physical transfers across interrupted attempts: recovery may finish with agreement after bytes landed in an earlier attempt. Each sentence has its own singular and plural forms. A film no longer shared shows neither sentence; its remaining jobs belong to *Share*, and the card draws no job. Where a folder dialog exists (`folderDialogsAvailableProvider`), a row holds the box for a folder of this computer (`sharing.folder-path`), typed or filled by *Browse…* (`sharing.folder-browse`, the folder picker); under it the buttons: *Share on Google Drive* (`sharing.drive`), *Share to a folder…* (`sharing.folder`; *Choose folder…* where the film is shared to a folder this computer has not chosen) and, where the film is shared, *Stop sharing* (`sharing.stop`). A share stays offered on a film already shared: pressing it again is how a person restarts the sends they stopped, and sharing elsewhere moves the film to that target. Each disabled button says why in its tooltip: no Google client in this build, no sync in this session — the application could not start it (*Sharing could not start in this session. Restart FilmOpen to share.*) — no username (the manifest is written as the viewer), no folder typed yet, or a step under way (*Wait for the step under way to finish.*), which *Browse…* and the box wait for too, the box saying so in a tooltip of its own. While a gesture is under way or queued the card says so inline (`sharing.working`, *One moment: FilmOpen is on it.*), whichever film is on screen, or none.

*Share on Google Drive* asks for the sign-in first (below), then finds or makes `FilmOpen/<tag>` in *My Drive* — the folder the manifest names where it is still there, else found by name, else made, over one client asked for within 30 seconds — clears the stopped sends of that folder, writes the manifest's `sync` with the folder's id and name and forgets this machine's folder for the film. *Share to a folder…* takes the path inside quotes where it is given in them, as a file browser copies one, and forward slashes for backward ones on Windows; it refuses, before anything is written, a folder not given in full on this computer's file system (on Windows a leading `\` alone is the current drive's root, not a full path), one that does not exist or cannot be listed, and the film's own folder, one inside it or one that holds it — the two folders compared as the file system resolves them, their links and `.` and `..` segments followed, without case and without Windows' extended-length `\\?\` — logged `sharing.folder.check {folder, resolved, film, filmResolved, outcome: taken | refused | unreadable, reason: notFull | missing | filmFolder | holdsFilm | noFilmFolder, code?, error?}` — saying *That folder cannot take the film: give the whole path of a folder that exists, neither the film's own folder, one inside it, nor one that holds it.*; then clears the stopped sends of that folder, keeps the path in this machine's preferences by the film's key — before the manifest is written, so that the reconcile the write starts finds it, and put back as it was where the write does not happen — and writes `sync` with the target `folder` alone. *Stop sharing* removes `sync` and this machine's folder, and the reconcile that follows retires every sync job of the film the engine holds — a film whose restore failed at opening keeps its journaled jobs until an opening whose restore succeeds (§6.14); the shared folder keeps what it holds. The manifest is written through the state's writer, as the viewer and with the film's guards, and a film without a manifest or without a username is said before anything is asked of Drive or a folder. **The manifest's re-read reconciles the film**: the state's binding does (§6.14), and a gesture adds no pass of its own — one behind the binding's would list the target while its first sends are under way and queue again a send done meanwhile, a second *Sent.* in *Share*; so a reconcile that fails is the binding's to log (`sync.reconcileFailed`), after the card has said *Sharing started*. A reconcile retires the jobs of a target the film named before and queues what the new one lacks. **An explicit Share restarts stopped sends**: before it writes the target it waits up to thirty seconds for that target's sends already stopping, then dismisses its cancelled sends — a person's earlier Stop among them — reading every acknowledgement, each dismissal logged as it is sent (`sharing.jobAction`: the job's id, kind, phase and state, the action, the target and the reason, `restart`) and as it ends (`sharing.jobActionDone`: the outcome, the code and the time, a refusal or a failure a warning, a failure with its error's type and text); a send that offers no dismissal, a refused dismissal or a wait that runs out is said as such (*The stopped sends could not be cleared. Try again.*) and nothing is written. Background reconciliation respects a person's Stop. Gestures run one after another, in the order they came — a stop pressed while a share waits runs after it — and each checks, before it writes, that the film it was pressed on is still the open one: another film opened meanwhile writes nothing to either. The state's binding, not the card, delivers after Settings is left.

**FilmOpen Cloud and the record on the card.** Beside *Share on Google Drive*, *Share on FilmOpen Cloud* (`sharing.filmopen`), and after the others *Record on {domain}* (`sharing.record`), both on every film with a folder. Each disabled says why: signed out of the account (*Sign in to {domain} to use FilmOpen Cloud.*, *Sign in to {domain} to record this project.*); for the share, a balance known to be zero (*You have no balance. Top up at {domain}.*) or not known yet (*Your balance is not known yet.*); for the record, a project already recorded from this device (*This project is recorded on {domain}.*); then the reasons above — for the record only no username and a step under way, since it writes no manifest. The card follows the account as it changes. A film on FilmOpen Cloud reads *Shared on FilmOpen Cloud as <username>/<tag>*, and *Share on Google Drive* is disabled on it (*This project is shared on FilmOpen Cloud.*). What became of a record is said under the buttons (`sharing.record-outcome`), on its own film only: *Recorded on {domain}.*, *Recorded again.* (the account had it from a press whose answer was lost), the confirmation this device could not save, a record under another username or tag, or *Its record could not be written ({code}).* — never claimed when it was not. On *Share*, a row of a film on FilmOpen Cloud asks *Sign in to {domain} to continue syncing.*, its sign-in opens the account's popup on Settings with that sentence, and its wait reads *Signing in to {domain}…* until the account is signed in, *Cancel* is pressed or 30 s pass.

Under a divider, the Google account, drawn from the `driveSignedIn` preference the last sign-in or sign-out left, never from the credential store, since Settings is built at the application's start: *Signed in to Google* with *Sign out* (`sharing.sign-out`), or *Not signed in* with *Sign in* (`sharing.sign-in`, disabled where the build has no client). A sign-out forgets the Drive folders' lookups too (§6.10), and so does a sign-in — Google's account chooser can land a consent on another account with no sign-out between — so a film is looked up afresh under the account signed in next. **One sign-in for the card and *Share* (§9.7)**, the model's: a share, or *Share*'s *Sign in*, asks whether the machine holds a sign-in Google takes (`ensureSignedIn`). Nobody signed in opens the consent page. A sign-in held is tried with a forced refresh within 30 seconds, its client closed as it comes: taken, it opens no consent page; unreachable, too slow or refused, the credential store is asked again — empty, a sign-out came in meanwhile, and the answer is no, with no consent page; tokens still held, Google unreachable or too slow lets the action go on, the send saying what it meets, and Google refusing them is a sign-in Google refused, which the consent page renews. A second sign-in asked for while one is under way joins it. While it waits the card says *Signing in to Google: finish in your browser if it opens.* with *Cancel* (`sharing.sign-in-cancel`), and *Share* says the same under the rows that asked, with a *Cancel* of its own: either stops the one wait at once, at either stage — the consent flow is abandoned, so an answer that comes after stores nothing, and a refresh still out has its client closed when it answers. Back cancels nothing; the wait ends by a *Cancel*, by the flow's own end or with the application. The model is one for the application's life (`sharingModelProvider`), not one per card, which Settings takes away with its tab: coming back finds the wait, its *Cancel* and the last notice where they were.

Notices stand inline in the card (`sharing.notice`), above the account's line, until the next gesture or sign-in, on the film they were raised on — a refusal of one film never stands under another's card — while the account's, the session's and *another film was opened* stand whichever film is open; a cancellation is drawn in the neutral colour, the person's own doing, every other notice in the error colour. There are eighteen: sharing could not start in this session; no Google client; a sign-in that failed; a sign-in Drive refused after it was refreshed (*Sign out, then sign in again*); a sign-in cancelled; a folder that cannot take the film, or cannot be listed; no manifest; the manifest changed meanwhile; Drive unreachable; another film opened before the share finished (*nothing was written*); no username; a manifest that could not be written; stopped sends that could not be cleared; for FilmOpen Cloud, signed out, a balance known to be zero, a username that is not one of the account's (*Your username in Settings is not one of your usernames on {domain}.*) and the account's usernames not readable (*Your account on {domain} could not be reached. Try again later.*); and a failure nothing foresaw (*Sharing did not work. Try again.*), logged by its type and said, never thrown at the card. The two confirmations, *Sharing started* and *Sharing stopped*, pass through `showMessage`. **Every gesture is an operation of the log**: `sharing.start {op: shareOnDrive | shareToFolder | shareOnFilmOpenCloud | stopSharing, film}` at the press, each stage reached as `sharing.step {op, film, stage, outcome, …, ms}` — Drive's `signIn` (`ok`, or `refused` with the notice's code), its `folder` found with its `id` and `name`, a folder's `folder` listed with the path and the target's identity, and `manifest` written — and one `sharing.done {op, film, outcome: done | refused | failed | dropped, code?, cause?, ms}`, `ms` counting from the press, the wait in the queue included, `code` the notice said and `cause` the decision behind it where one code has several: the restart's `timedOut`, `notDismissable` or `dismissRefused` behind `restartRefused`, the folder check's `cannotTake` or `unreadable` behind `folderUnavailable`, Drive's own code behind `signInRefused`, `driveUnconfigured` or `driveUnavailable`; a sign-in that answered no with no notice (a sign-out came in while the held sign-in was tried) ends `refused` as `notSignedIn`, the cloud's record not made as the record's code, a failure nothing foresaw `failed`, and a gesture the model's disposal kept from running `dropped` with the cause `disposed`. The restart of stopped sends logs `sharing.restart.start {film, target, stopping}` with the ids still stopping, `sharing.restart.step {…, stage: wait | dismiss, outcome, job?, ms}` as they settle and per dismissal, then `sharing.restart.done {…, dismissed, ms}` or `sharing.restart.refused {…, stage: wait | dismiss, reason: timedOut | notDismissable | dismissRefused, job?, ms}`, a dismissal that never answered within the deadline refused at its own stage with its job. A sign-in logs `sharing.signIn.start {mode: consent | ensure}`, its decisions `sharing.signIn.step {mode, stage: held | refresh | heldStill | decision, outcome, code?, ms}` and one `sharing.signIn.done {mode, outcome: signedIn | refused | cancelled | failed, code?, ms}`, the askers that joined it adding no line; a sign-out `sharing.signOut.start` and `sharing.signOut.done {ms}` (`{outcome: dropped, cause: disposed, ms}` where the disposal kept it from running). Beside them `sharing.started {target, ms}`, `sharing.stopped {target, ms}`, `sharing.signInCancelled`, `sharing.notice` with a notice's code, and warnings — `sharing.failed {op, film, type, error, ms}`, `sharing.signInFailed`, `sharing.signOutFailed`, `sharing.writeFailed` — with an error's type and words; the folder ids and paths they worked on are logged, never a token; building the application and the card reads no credential.

**Pages** (*Plugins*): *General*, these cards with the assistant's text model and the speech service — the app owns no key (§6.7) — and *Plugins*, each plug-in's own keys on its page, the speech services' providers among them; this device's usage is Activity's (§9.7). At `Breakpoints.treeInDrawer` (700 px) of **window** and more, a list beside the page in the browser's sidebar and its divider — the title, then one row per page with its icon, the page shown drawn as the tree draws its selected row; a list, not a tree, with nothing to expand — starting at the sidebar's narrowest, 220 px, so that a 700 px window leaves the page some 410 px; a width dragged to lasts while Settings is shown. Below it, the title and a labelled pull-down above the page (*Settings page*) with the same two. Settings opens on *General* each time the rail shows it, since leaving Settings puts the page back (`Dashboard`); `settingsTabProvider` holds the page, so a link elsewhere can open Settings on the page it needs, and a resize across the breakpoint keeps it — the page keeps its element too, so what is typed on it survives the resize. **The list is a tab bar to the semantics** — each page a tab with its selected state — and **the pull-down an ordinary labelled one** (`settings.page`), whose value is the page shown: the state's `tab` and the recorder read the selected tab, or the pull-down's value (`currentTab`, `settingsPickerPage`), and the agent's `set_tab general|plugins` taps the tab, or opens the pull-down and chooses the page from its menu by its name, as a person does. General's list is the scrollable that holds its cards; the list beside it scrolls on its own where a window is too short for it. A page's name too long for its row wraps inside it.

**The keys** (§6.7) are a plug-in's, each on its page (`KeyCards(owner:)`, one owner's cards from `PluginCatalogueView.current.keyOwners`); the app owns none. **General's keys section** (`GeneralKeysSection`, below the logs and above Development and About) holds no key card: a card on how keys are kept; then the cards of whoever uses the keys (`GeneralKeysSection.leading`, a slot that knows none of them: `settings_page.dart` gives it the *Assistant text model* and dictation's *Speech service*, whose *Configure* opens the selected service's provider page, §9.6); then a line saying the other platforms are pending and the website lists what stands in their way, with *See the platforms*. **A plug-in's page under Plugins** draws its own cards while it is on, through the builder the app hands `PluginsTab` — the speech services' keys on `fo-openai`'s and `fo-xai`'s. A door elsewhere opens a plug-in's page through `settingsTabProvider`'s `openPlugin(tag)`, which `settings_page.dart` hands the tab as `openPlugin` and takes back once the page has opened (`settingsPluginToOpenProvider`); a tag not installed leaves the list. A page has no owner's header. There is a card per key slot whose platform the catalogue lists, or else the owner's own platform file defines, titled with the slot's label or else the platform's name, the slot's help under the title, while the sentences around a card — Save's question and Remove's, what a save or a removal says — name the platform, since each says *key* itself and a slot's label is a whole phrase (*OpenRouter key*); a plug-in turned off takes its cards away and leaves its keys in the store, its page saying to turn it on to enter them. A catalogue that yields no platform says so, and that installing FilmOpen again restores its list. Each slot's card holds:

**The keyring line** (§6.7). Where the credential store returned none of the keys FilmOpen saved, one line stands above either page of Settings, and in the assistant dock under the assistant's name, saying that the store, named as the platform's (*the system keyring*, *FilmOpen's protected file*, *the Keychain*, *the Android Keystore*), returned none of FilmOpen's keys and that nothing was changed, with *These keys are gone: start again*, which asks before it acts; it goes once a key reads again. A card whose key its record names but the store no longer gives back says *Missing from this computer's keyring* and *Enter it again in the box below*. In the assistant's chat a request refused before it left the computer reads *Not sent: …* — for a missing key with *Open Settings*, the dock's way to the provider's key card — and never as an outcome nobody knows.
- **its state:** *No key*, *Stored*, *Verified on <date>*, *Rejected by <platform>* or *Could not check*, with *ends in <last four>* where known — and since a save stores only a key the platform accepted, the last two reach a card only from a state an earlier build wrote, which the next save replaces; under such a state, a line saying why and what to do (a 403 has its own); while a check runs, a line saying the key is being checked with the platform;
- **a box for each credential** the platform file lists, labelled with FilmOpen's word for the value where it knows the file's (*API key*, *Secret key*, *Key ID*, *Key secret*), else the file's own, obscured — with an eye at its end that shows what was pasted until the eye or a save hides it, since a phone's clipboard is not always what it seems; a refused key stays in its box as it was, shown or not; the box's identifier stays outside `valueIdentifiers` (§4.3), so what the eye shows never travels in the data an agent or the tape reads; a picture shows what the screen shows, so while the issue recorder runs (`recordingProvider`, §7.1) every box is hidden and the eye says so — with no suggestions or autocorrection, and replaced by an empty one as *Save* reads it. A helper line in its own colour warns when the value looks like another listed platform's key or, once what was typed can no longer become the platform's `prefix`, when it does not start with it; *Save* still works (warn, don't enforce);
- **the actions:**
 - ***Save***, the only door a key goes through, checking with the platform file's `verify`, else with the owning plug-in's `verifyKey` (§6.11), else storing unchecked: disabled while a box is empty, saying *Paste the key first*, and pressed by Enter in a filled box. One press checks the key with the platform and stores it where the platform accepts it; a key it refuses, and one whose check has no answer, are not stored, and the box keeps the paste so that it can be put right. Its answer is a line under the boxes — accepted in the positive colour, invalid in the error colour, a check that could not be made in the warning colour, each saying what became of the key — and the line goes as soon as a box changes. *Save* asks first, with `keys.replace.cancel` and `keys.replace.confirm` (a 44 px target), when it would replace a stored key, naming its last four, or when the value looks like another listed platform's key or does not start as this platform's keys do; cancelled, the boxes keep what was pasted;
 - ***Remove***, disabled while no key is stored, saying so. *Remove* asks first, in a dialog whose *Remove* is a 44 px target;
 - while an action runs, the boxes and these three are disabled, each saying why;
 - ***How to get a key*** (the platform's guide on the documentation site, `kFilmOpenDocsUrl`, §3) and ***Open the keys page*** (the platform's own), each saying so when the page cannot be opened;
- **messages:** a transient message after a save that stored the key, and after a removal; the answer to its check is the line under the boxes, not a message that goes by. Inline until the next action: a store that cannot be used, a value no key could hold, or an action refused because another was running.

There is no way to show a saved key. On the web General's keys section is one sentence saying that keys are kept by the desktop app, and that FilmOpen on a computer adds them. Identifiers: `keys.<owner>.<slot>`, `keys.<owner>.<slot>.<credential>` (`keys.fo-openai.openai.key`), `.key.show` (the eye), `.save`, `.remove`, `.guide`, `.keys-page` and `.answer`, the line a save's check leaves; `keys.remove.cancel` and `keys.remove.confirm` in *Remove*'s dialog, `keys.replace.cancel` and `keys.replace.confirm` in *Save*'s; and `keys.platforms`.

**Plugins** (`filmopen_plugins_ui`, §3.11). The list: a line on what plug-ins are and that one not from FilmOpen stays off until allowed; *Install from file…* (`plugins.install`: a zip unpacked into the library, then the plug-in's page and its consent; a zip refused says the file is not a plug-in FilmOpen can install, with its code, and an install that failed in FilmOpen — a write or another step — says *Installing this plug-in did not work*, with `writeFailed` or `failed`, and one whose files are written but whose plug-ins could not be read again says the plug-in is in the library, that the plug-ins could not be read again, with `reloadFailed`, and that it shows once FilmOpen starts again); *Preferred providers*, *Text* and *Images and video* (`plugins.prefer.text`, `plugins.prefer.media`), each a pull-down of the enabled plug-ins providing `complete` or `render`, by name and platform, or a line saying none does, redrawn as the plug-ins change while the list stays on screen — **showing *None: ask me* where the person chose none**, and offering it to take a choice back. The general provider chooser asks only where more than one plug-in remains after named-model and local ranking. For LLM calls, *Text* supplies the provider preference to the shared decision (§6.11): among the decided model's reachable providers the preference wins, else stock, else first by tag; leaving Text unselected neither asks nor advances to another model. The list then has a card per plug-in (`plugin:<tag>`), **the cards a tree** (below) — its name, author, version and where it came from (*Comes with FilmOpen*, *In your library*, *In your development folder*), its description, why it is off or cannot run, how many problems its files have, its switch (`plugin:<tag>.enabled`, held with the reason where it cannot run, and **on from the moment an On is accepted**, as its line says *Checking…*, the plug-ins read again only confirming it or turning it back) and *Configure* (`plugin:<tag>.open`), and under them **its status line** (`plugin:<tag>.readiness`, `plugin_status_line.dart`, a live region that a screen reader hears change): what is known of whether it can work, read from the runtime's record (§6.11) and **always in words, with a colour and a mark**, so that the colour is never the only sign — *Checking…* in the primary colour while it is asked (turning it on, or *Check*), *Turning off…* while it tidies up; its state in the app's words — *Ready* in green, *It works, less well than it could* in amber, *It needs something from you first* and *What it needs is not there* in red — then the plug-in's own words and, where it gave them, what it measured with the app's labels and units (*GPU*, *VRAM* and *RAM* in gigabytes of 1024³ to one decimal in the reader's notation, *Version*), each one it did not give said *unknown*, never zero, and a label never parted from its value at a wrap; *Enabled — open to check.* in the muted colour where nothing is known, which is every plug-in that is on at a start; a failure of turning it on or of its check in red, in the plug-in's words where it gave some, with a small *Try again* (`plugin:<tag>.retry`); and for a plug-in that is off, nothing beyond its switch, but an amber caution where its clean-up was not confirmed, it did not stop when asked, it did not come on, or its switch — an Off or an On — could not be saved, that last beside whatever its clean-up left, neither hiding the other; nothing either for a plug-in on that cannot run, whose notice says why. It wraps, and the card holds no address, box or setting: those are on the plug-in's page. A switch or an *Allow* whose write fails is said there, not thrown. Turning on a plug-in that needs consent opens the **consent screen** instead — what it asks for: its own keys, which spend money, and the keys it wants to use — **each named in words, its slot and whose it is**, never by its id — each key it wants followed, indented, by what it may spend **in the app's own words** — *It can use your fal.ai key, the one fo-fal holds. FilmOpen adds the key to each request it sends; what it asks for is charged to your fal.ai account.*, said whether or not that key's owner is on — the text model when it declares `capabilities.ai`, the hosts it reaches without a key, **the servers whose address the person types on its page** — which the plug-in can also read — each in its own words with the default it starts with, **the plug-ins it uses**, grouped by the plug-in called and each function said in that plug-in's own words — a function of the contract, which no plug-in labels, in the app's: a platform's `run` (*run jobs on its service*) and `request` (*send its service requests this plug-in writes*), and any plug-in's `status` (*ask whether it is ready*) — or a line saying that plug-in is not installed, and **under a call, the app's line for each platform whose key the called plug-in may spend** — *It can make fo-fal use your fal.ai key. FilmOpen adds the key to each request it sends; what it asks for is charged to your fal.ai account.* — since a function reached through `ctx.call` runs as its owner with everything its owner may send: **its own keys, the keys it asks to use, and, through the plug-ins it calls in turn, theirs**, the chain walked once and the caller's own keys, said in its first line, not said again; derived from the manifests, never from an author's words, and nothing under a call on a plug-in that may spend none. Each line's mark stands in a column of its own, so a line that wraps goes on under its own words, the types it reads and writes, and the film it came with — with *Cancel* and *Allow* (`plugins.consent.cancel`, `plugins.consent.allow`, a 44 px target); only *Allow* turns it on. A plug-in's **page**: *All plug-ins* (`plugins.back`), which returns to the list where it was (the tab keeps the list's offset as *Configure* opens the page, and the list comes back at it), its name and switch, *Docs* (`plugin.<tag>.docs`) where its manifest supplies a valid guide path — opened in the browser on `kFilmOpenDocsUrl`, the development documentation site in a build with development mode and the release documentation site otherwise; no link for an omitted or invalid path, and a message in the app's language where the browser cannot open it, the address it opened logged (`plugins.docsOpened`, a public page's) —, *Settings on this computer* — each machine-scoped setting drawn by the app's own widget for its type (`plugin:<tag>.<key>`: a box saved 700 ms after the last change and when the page closes, and showing a value stored while the page is open, as the plug-ins are read again, where the person has not changed it, a number box saying inline when its text is not a number, a switch, a pull-down; a model's choices are the catalogue's entries of its category on its platform, each of a text, picture, clip or music model saying where no plug-in that is on can use it — a plug-in naming no models counting for a picture, clip or music only on a row marked `render` — *Nano Banana Pro (Gemini 3 Pro Image) — no plug-in that is on can use it*, as a card on the project's page says it too (§6.11); **a `server` box** — one line, a URL keyboard, the address saved **normalised**, text that is no address said inline and saving nothing as a number box does, and a plain `http` address at a certainly public IP address warned in the tertiary colour and **saved all the same**, since FilmOpen warns and does not enforce), registered in `FieldRegistry` under `plugin:<tag>` so that `set_field plugin:<tag> <key> <text>` types as a person does — *Actions* (`plugin:<tag>.action.<id>`, one run at a time, with progress and *Stop*, `plugin:<tag>.action.cancel`, and the answer as a line, `plugin:<tag>.answer`: the plug-in's message, or why it did not finish — a key not entered, a key not allowed, a limit, stopped, a code — **an action that answers `state: 'failed'` said as a failure in the error colour, in its own message or its problem's words, never *Done.***, its code logged as `plugins.actionFailed` and never its words (§6.11)), its own key cards while it is on (the cards above), and under them one line on FilmOpen credit (`plugin:<tag>.credit`) from the app's funding resolver (§6.6) for its slots' platforms, asked once per resolver, generation and set of platforms and never as the page draws: *Sign in to <the website> to use FilmOpen credit.*, *Ready on FilmOpen credit.* or *FilmOpen credit: top up on <the website>.* (`kFilmOpenDomain`), and no line where the person's own key is stored or credit is not configured or not known; then *Keys*, which, while it is off, says to turn it on to enter them, and holds a checkbox per key it asks to use, `plugin:<tag>.grant.<owner>:<slot>`, with consent's line for what that key may spend under it and, in the tertiary colour, which of the two it still lacks — no such key saved, *No OpenAI API key is saved yet: save one in Settings → General or on its plug-in's page under Settings → Plugins, then allow it here.*, or, the key saved and the plug-in allowed to run, the box, *Your OpenAI API key is saved: tick the box to let this plug-in use it.* — the key named by its platform, else its slot, and nothing once both are there), **models it speaks for** (the `models` of what it implements, with a line naming any the catalogue does not define), **what it offers other plug-ins, and uses of theirs** (each function it offers, in its own words with its help, and a line per plug-in it uses saying what it uses it *for*, in that plug-in's words, or that the plug-in is not installed, with consent's line for what the call may spend under it), *Check* (`plugin:<tag>.check`, §6.11), held while a check or turning it on or off is under way, its answer (`plugin:<tag>.status`) **the same record in the same words, colour and mark as the card's**, *Try again* beside it where the card has it, nothing while nothing is known, and for a plug-in whose code has no `status` the sentence that api 1 asks for one and its other functions still run — a check left by going back to the list goes on, and its answer is on the card and on the page when it comes — its actions and *Check* held, with the reason, where it is off or cannot run — and *About* (its stem, its files' problems, its README) — a problem **said in the app's words where `labels.dart` has a sentence for its code**, and never by the code: `badCodeFile` in each of its four cases (a path that is no `.js` file of the folder or is named twice, an empty `entry`, more files than a plug-in may have, code larger than it may be) and `archiveTooLarge`, naming the folder's two limits from the issue's `args`; a code with no sentence yet is shown as it was, `path: code (field)`. The tab redraws on the runtime's `changes`. On the web it is one sentence: plug-ins run in the desktop app. A machine setting on a plug-in's page is drawn by the same plug-in field widgets as a plug-in's card on an entity's page (`filmopen_forms/plugin_field_widgets.dart`): a switch, a pull-down of the field's choices and a box for text or a number, its help up to eight lines, and an agent's typing parsed the same way in both places — `true` or `false` for a switch, one of the choices or nothing for a pull-down, and for a `server` an address `ServerAddress` reads, normalised, anything else refused (`pluginServerValue`). A `server` never reaches a card on an entity's page: it is always this computer's, so the manifest reader keeps it machine-scoped and the card leaves it out.

**The list as a tree** (`plugins_tab.dart`, `plugin_card.dart`). The cards stand as the manifests' `x.dependencies` declare (Project Specification §14.4; `PluginManifest.dependencies`, carried to `PluginSummary.dependencies`): a plug-in that names none is a root; one that names others stands **once**, indented under the first of them by displayed name, its card saying *Also needs …* with the others' names; one that names a plug-in that is not installed stands as a root, saying *Needs fo-cui (not installed)* — the tag, since an absent package has no name to show; one whose needs lead back to it stands as a root with the problem line *Needs …, which in turn needs this plug-in*, on each plug-in of the loop, which is walked with a set of those reached and so ends. Each level is alphabetical by displayed name, case aside, the tag the tie-break. A plug-in standing under others is drawn grey while one of them is off, saying *Off while ComfyUI is off*. **The tree is presentation alone**: each card keeps its own switch, *Configure* and identifiers; nothing turns a parent on with its child or grants a child the parent's keys; and nothing reads `x.dependencies` when a plug-in is called, which keeps its own guards. Where none names a dependency the list is one level, alphabetical; the empty state is unchanged.

**A plug-in's rewrite** (`filmopen_forms/plugin_hook_dialog.dart`). The file menu (`file.menu`) offers *Plugins…* (`page.plugins`) where `PageAccess.canRunPlugins` holds — a handle, a project that can be written, a file not from the library; from anybody's version, since the run writes beside it, never over it — and an enabled plug-in's `transform` applies to the page's type. A *Stop* pressed after the plug-in answered still stops: nothing is written. It writes the form's draft first, then opens a dialog titled with the version's name: a line saying the result is a new version under the viewer's and the plug-in's names and that this version is not changed, a button per such plug-in (`page.plugins.<tag>.transform`: *<plug-in>: rewrite*), and while one runs a progress bar the plug-in's `ctx.progress` fills, *Running…* and *Stop* (`page.plugins.cancel`). The answer is a line (`page.plugins.answer`): *Written: <stem>.* with *Open it* (`page.plugins.open`, a 44 px target, which shows that version and closes the dialog), or why nothing was written — stopped, the file changed meanwhile, no handle, a limit, a key not entered or not allowed, the plug-in's own words, a code. Closing the dialog while a run goes on stops it. This progress and cancellation are the ones *Generate* will use.

**A plug-in's card on a page** (`filmopen_forms/plugin_sections.dart`). The characters', locations', folders', scenes' and project's forms end with a card per enabled plug-in whose `entitySettings` name the type — for the project file, its project-scoped settings — drawn by the form's own fields and stored in the version being edited under `plugins.<tag>.<key>` (`field:<stem>:plugins.<tag>.<key>`): a text box, a number box, a switch or a pull-down, autosaved by `DraftSaver`, locked on a version the viewer may not edit, and paired row by row in Compare, where copy-left moves it. An emptied box removes its key and an object left empty; a pull-down takes only its own tokens and a switch `true` or `false`. A plug-in turned off, or missing, leaves its values under *Other attributes*. Every plug-in's model setting, fo-assist's `textModel` included, keeps its selector on its card, its row in *Other attributes* where the plug-in is off, and its row in Compare. The types without a form show their `plugins` object read-only (a scene did until it was given a form). The cards are the ones the runtime answers when the page is built.

### 9.4a The plug-in developer page — `filmopen_plugin_dev`

A large dialog, opened by *Plug-in developer page…* (`settings.plugin-dev`) on **Settings → General's About card** and by *Open in the developer page* (`plugin:<tag>.dev`) on a plug-in's own page, which opens it on that plug-in. **In every desktop build, release builds included, with no switch before it and no consent question**: the page is for people who write code, and they will not normally have a development build. Neither entry appears where plug-ins do not run — a phone, the browser — and the app hands the opener to `filmopen_plugins_ui`, which imports neither this package nor the host.

Under its title, **a plug-in of the development folder that could not be read** is named with its path (`plugin-dev.folder-problems`, in the error colour), and **one standing sentence** says what the page does: *This page runs a plug-in's code as it is on this computer, without the question FilmOpen asks before it runs a plug-in you installed, and with every key its manifest asks for. Run only code you wrote or trust.* Then: **Plug-in** (`plugin-dev.plugin`), a pull-down of the plug-ins installed — the development folder's or the shared library's — each by name, tag and where it is, with every problem the reader found listed by file beneath it and, for a stock plug-in's installed copy, the line that FilmOpen replaces those files when it starts; **Function** (`plugin-dev.function`), a pull-down of what that plug-in exposes — **`status` first**, then the functions of what it implements, its actions and what it offers other plug-ins, **one entry per name**, each with its kind in words — *every plug-in's* for `status`, *hook*, *action*, *offer* — the first road kept and its example taken from a later road where the first carries none — with *Other…* and a box for any name (`plugin-dev.function-name`); **How it is called** (`plugin-dev.mode`), three chips that wrap at a phone's width — *As FilmOpen calls it* (`plugin-dev.mode.app`, the default), *Exactly what I typed* (`plugin-dev.mode.typed`) and *Console* (`plugin-dev.mode.console`) — each saying beneath what it does, and, for `status` or an action as FilmOpen calls it, the line *FilmOpen calls this with ctx alone, so the box is not sent*; **Arguments** (`plugin-dev.args`), one JSON box filled with the chosen function's shape — everything that crosses into a plug-in is JSON, so one box serves every function — text that is not JSON said inline with *Call* waiting; **in the console** the box is JavaScript, labelled *JavaScript* and held to nothing, the **Function** pull-down gone and *Call* waiting, with the reason in its tooltip, until a plug-in is chosen and the box holds a script: the host loads the plug-in's own files and then one more, `console.js`, whose value is a plug-in of one function, `$console`, whose body is the box on that file's first line — so a failure's line is the box's and the plug-in's top-level names are there — and calls it with `ctx` alone, bound to the open project as every run is, the entity the page was opened on as `ctx.selection`; its result, its lines, `last-run.json` (`function: $console`, `mode: console`, `args` the script) and the developer log are a call's, and the app's log has `plugins.devConsole` (the script, the entity, the files) and `plugins.devCalled` (the script and the value whole); ***Save what it makes to*** (`plugin-dev.source`), the entity the caller passed or nothing, **only as FilmOpen calls it**, since as typed nothing is saved — **three modes**: a render as FilmOpen calls it **with an entity is a place's render**, the request the app would make handed to the insertion to takes alone (its takes and its record, no entry, the item with no group) and run by the render manager through the page's working copy while the page waits for it, its calls in the log and *Stop* stopping its job, the result naming the job and its take, or the job's code with the app's sentence for it; a capability (`describeReference`, `describeStyle`) with an entity takes the app's own door — its media granted, its answer's leaves in the document's spelling and held as the app holds it — and is refused without one, the app never calling one for no entity; **with none**, the outputs are answered as the plug-in gave them and nothing is saved; **as typed**, nothing is saved either; ***Call*** and ***Stop*** (`plugin-dev.call`, `plugin-dev.cancel`), each *Call* reading the plug-in's files from disk again, so a developer edits, calls and reads with no restart and no *Allow* for every save; **Result** (`plugin-dev.result`), the outcome as one plain line — a failure's code, reason, **for an answer of the wrong shape the path in it** (*the whole answer* where it is the answer itself), the author's message and **its file, line and column** — the time taken, and the value as formatted JSON;
