**Log** (`plugin-dev.log`, a row `plugin-dev.log:<n>`), the run's lines **as they happen** — the seam's `devRun` is told of each one, so a call waiting on a door shows what it has done so far, and **a plug-in the code called is in the log too**, its lines and its doors carrying that plug-in's tag (`PluginDevLine.by`), since the frame carries the page's log down the chain — with *Clear* (`plugin-dev.log-clear`), the box following its newest line **only while the reader is at the foot**, and the run's own list as the record once it ends; and **the two files** (`plugin-dev.result-file`, `plugin-dev.log-file`), the paths of `last-run.json` and of the developer log, to open or to `tail`.

**A server provider's `deploy` and `wake` are refused here**, as on every other road to a call — an action, another plug-in's `ctx.call` — in the app's words (`pluginsServerSessionOnly`) and before anything is sent: each would start or keep up a server billed by the hour, and only a server's own screen, which shows it and stops it — the GPU page's *Start server* and its servers' window (§9.7a, §6.15) — may ask for one. A server provider is a plug-in that declares `server-provider`, its declaration faulty or not; its other functions — capabilities, lists, resolving, a stop — run as any other (`PluginManifest.startsAServer`).

*Not built*: *Watch*, a history of calls, the page on a phone, and the plug-in's machine settings drawn on the page itself.

**The page follows the host**: it listens to `PluginDevBench.current.changes`, so a development folder named on the card behind it — the box applies on losing the focus, which is the gesture that opens the page — re-reads the plug-in on screen and the list beside it; a plug-in that is gone stops being the page's choice, and a function it no longer offers becomes *Other…* with its name in the box.

**For an agent** the page registers the stem `plugin-dev` with `FieldRegistry`, so `set_field plugin-dev plugin <tag>`, `… function <name>`, `… args <json>` and `… mode app|typed|console` type as a person does, and `tap plugin-dev.call`, `find plugin-dev.result` and the two files do the rest with no new op; the agent channel's `valueIdentifiers` carry `plugin-dev.plugin`, `plugin-dev.function` (with *Other…*'s `plugin-dev.function-name`), `plugin-dev.args` and the page's two paths, `plugin-dev.result-file` and `plugin-dev.log-file`, so that an agent finds the files; a **recording** replays the choices and the arguments and **not the paths**, which the tape refuses as it refuses dictation (§6.8), since an app-support path names the person on Windows.

**Settings → General's About card** also carries ***Plug-ins in development*** (`settings.plugin-dev-folder`, with `settings.plugin-dev-folder-choose` and `settings.plugin-dev-folder-clear`): the folder of §6.11 in a box that is **typed — entered, or left behind — or filled by the folder picker**, an empty box meaning *none chosen*, as the × writes, and saying what would fill it.

### 9.5 Behavioural rules

- **The username** is `default` (`kDefaultViewerHandle`) until the person chooses one. A fresh installation keeps `default` in the preferences, and a stored username that is missing, blank, not a text or not a handle (§5.6, `ShortName.validateHandle`: `owner[.workspace]`, the owner a username of 3 to 32 characters, never `official`) is read as `default`, its reason logged by its code (`settings.viewerFallback`: `missing`, `blank`, `invalid`, `unreadable`) and `default` written back. `Settings.resolveHandle` is the one rule the username is read by, and `Settings.viewer` is therefore never null: the app always browses and writes as a handle, and the refusals for no handle that a page's access and the writer keep (`PageAccess`, `NoViewerException`) are reached only by a caller that passes none. `default` is a literal author, not an account: two installations that write as `default` both own what `default` wrote, and on the sample a first run still shows the format's rule that official wins, `default` having no version of its own there. Choosing a username renames, moves and hands over nothing: what was written as `default` stays `default`'s. **A change of username is one transition**, `SettingsController.setViewerHandle`, the box's and the agent's (`set_viewer`) alike: an empty box is `default`; a text that is not a handle is said under the box and changes nothing, the last valid username staying in effect; every open draft is written first, as the username it was typed under, pass after pass while a pass leaves one to write — a form opened while the last pass wrote, or a draft typed into during or after its own write — up to five (`OpenDrafts.settleUntilQuiet`, `maxSettlePasses`), each pass waiting for every write in flight, a form's own timed write included, and the username is taken only then: a draft that cannot be written refuses the change — *Your username is still {handle}:* and the save's own sentence under the box — and typing that outruns the fifth pass refuses it too, in a sentence of its own, since no save failed (*…a page was still being saved. Try again in a moment.*, `DraftsNotSettledException`); a change asked for while another waits on the drafts decides instead of it, while a text that is not a handle is no change at all and leaves a waiting one to land. **No dialog that creates a file asks for a username** — *New project*, a new character or location, *Add folder…*, *Add scene…*: each writes as the username in effect, `default` where none is chosen, read once a change in flight has landed (`SettingsController.viewerSettled`); one changed while a character's, a location's, a folder's or a scene's dialog is open is said in it, what was typed kept and nothing written, and the next *Create* writes as the new one. Nothing dismisses such a dialog while it creates, and it closes by its own answer — its own route removed where another stands over it. The agent's `quit` writes the open drafts as a change of username does, puts the session's username back before anything else ends — refusing at once, with nothing written, where it cannot — and, where the username changed during the session through any door (the agent's `set_viewer`, Settings' box, its own restore, remembered for a `quit` asked again), waits for the preferences to keep it (`SettingsController.viewerStored`), writing it again first where the store refused it before; it refuses to quit where either fails, as it refuses for a draft that cannot be written. A username the preferences could not take stays for the session, said under the box with *Try again* (`Settings.unsaved`, `retryWrite`; `settings.notSaved` logs the preference, the error's type and its message). `settings.viewer` names the username taken, and `settings.viewerFallback` the value it replaced. A choice written in the background — the theme, the language, the sidebar's width, a sharing folder, the microphone and the others — is logged `settings.keep.start {key, value}` as its write starts and `settings.kept {key, value, outcome, code?, cause?, ms}` once the store answers. The username box validates the owner as a username — 3 to 32 characters, never starting with a dash — and says the username's rule when it is not one, validates the workspace part at 20 characters and names that bound in its length refusal, and counts up to 53 characters for the two parts and their dot; a segment box validates and names 32 characters and counts up to 32. The username is visible in the tree header.
- **Development mode: warnings, not locks**. While the application is being built, a rule about who may do what warns and lets the person continue: another author's version, official without being a director, the shared library. One switch governs it — Settings → Development → *Warn instead of refuse*, on by default in debug and profile builds, absent from a release build, which reads no switch an earlier build stored — and one place applies it, `Guards` in the writer. The rules that protect files are not governed and refuse in every build: a file changed on disk, duplicate keys, the identity fields, the content the format requires, the epoch guard of copy-left, a missing handle, a folder the system will not write. The bundled sample opens as a session copy on every platform, in either mode, editable, with *Copy to a folder…* in the project menu to keep it. The pages follow the same rule as the writer: `PageAccess` (`filmopen_state/page_access.dart`) answers what a page may do — whether the form writes, whether the star, the check and the label can be pressed (`canPick` and `canSetOfficial` are false on a plug-in, where `choosesNothing` holds) — and which red lines it shows, and the page, the version row, the file menu and Compare read nothing else. `PageAccess.canCreateVersions` answers, without an entity, whether the viewer may make new versions in the project — declare an epoch, or make a version at a missing one: the project's own folder can be written and a viewer is set. It is the destination's permission alone; the library is never the destination, and where the copied version comes from, another author or the library, is no part of it. A red line (`filmopen_forms/guard_line.dart`, `GuardLine`) sits above the page's header in the scheme's error colour, one per notice, with a tooltip naming Settings → Development: *Editing maria's version as john123* on another author's version, the project's directors beside a star someone who is not one may press, *Writing to the shared library* on a library file the library lets be written, *No handle* with a link to Settings, *A session copy* with *Copy to a folder…* where folders can be made, a library file the library cannot take (it stays *View*), and a source that says beforehand it cannot be written (a folder on disk says so only when a write fails, in that write's message). A star pressed, a label written or the JSON editor's save past a governed rule says the same sentence as a message; the autosave does not repeat it, since the line is on the page. Compare's left column follows `PageAccess` too. Compare's tab is never greyed in development mode: where there is nothing to compare it opens on its reason as a red line, and the question to fork a version of your own stays.
- Refresh never blanks the screen.
- **Paste as plain text** — Control-Shift-V, Command-Shift-V — pastes into the focused field as Control-V does, through the field's own paste (a prose field's included, §9.8): `PastePlainText` wraps the navigator, so a dialog's box pastes too, since a dictation tool sends those keys after transcribing. It logs `app.paste.shortcut {keys, target, handled, reason?, ms}`, `target` the nearest semantics identifier and `handled` whether an enabled paste ran, never the clipboard's text, since a person may be pasting a key; where no paste ran, `reason` says why: `no-focus`, or `no-paste` (a focus that is no text field). A read-only box's paste runs and changes nothing, so it logs `handled: true`; the prose editor's own `prose.clipboard` says `refused`, `read_only`.
- **An amount is shown one way** (`labels.dart`'s `amountText`, which takes an exact decimal): to the cent at a cent or more (`$1.23`, half away from zero); under a cent and above zero, rounded up to the next tenth of a cent (`ExactDecimal.ceilTo`) and written `$0.00` followed by that tenth as one raised digit, a Unicode superscript — `$0.0004` and `$0.001` read `$0.00¹`, `$0.0037` reads `$0.00⁴`, and `$0.0099`, rounding up to a cent, reads `$0.01` —, the digit there only to show that the amount is not zero, smaller and higher so that it is never read as a cent; zero as `$0.00`; below zero a true minus before the currency mark. Dollars are written as the language writes them, another currency by its code. Every price, cost and total on screen goes through it — the render dialog's price, a server's and the GPU page's cost, a lent offer's price per image, the speech service's price of a minute, Activity's Usage, the recording microphone's running cost, a batch's total, a media tile's cost and the account's balance in dollars. Not through it: the cloud's published tariff, fixed text already to the cent.
- Every empty state says what would fill it (no takes yet, only one version, no dialog or actions).
- Every write goes through `ProjectWriter` and is followed by a re-read; the UI never edits the index. Writes happen only under the viewer's handle; the star is for directors; the library is never written.
- **A preference that could not be saved** stays for the session and is said where the person can try again: the username under its box (above), and the folder *New project* proposes next (§9.2) at the top of *General* — *FilmOpen could not save the folder your last project was made in…* — with *Try again* (`settings.new-project-parent.retry`), which writes it again (`retryWrite`). The export's encoder and development mode's switch are said under their own cards in the error colour — *This choice could not be saved…* and *This switch could not be saved: it holds until FilmOpen closes.* — with no *Try again*: the next change writes it, and a write that reaches the store clears the line (`Settings.unsaved`).
- On the web nothing reaches a disk: the sample and new projects are session copies, and the tree header says so.
- Every transient message goes through `filmopen_ui/messages.dart` (`showMessage`), so they all look alike and can carry one action (for instance *Settings* when a username is missing). A message goes by itself: one with an action stays at least `kLongMessage`, ten seconds, or longer where its caller asks, then goes like any other if it is not tapped; one without keeps its caller's duration. An optional `actionIdentifier` names that action for an agent; the action retains Material's label, theme, once-only press and dismissal with the action reason. Without an identifier the ordinary snackbar action is unchanged.
- A copy-left gesture (§9.3) is checked before it is applied and never after: what it would make of the draft is built to one side, and a refusal is a message with nothing written. What it does write goes through the same autosave typing does, and is logged as `compare.copied` with the two stems, whether it was a row, a section or the file, the keys — a block as `blocks.<id>`, its own id whatever it says — and **the values** the copy read on each side, as §9.3 lists them: nothing is masked.
- `epoch` is a header field only where the filename carries one (§7): on a scene it is content, the epoch the scene is set in (§10.2), and the writer lets it be written — as it does on a folder, where the format gives the key no meaning and a reader keeps it like any key it does not know (§10.1; an *episode* is a folder since the format's 1.12). `Entity.headerMismatches` and `ProjectWriter` apply the same rule.

**The project's `next_epoch` is the writer's**: the owner's epoch allocation floor (Project Specification §8.2) is among the project's `keptOut` paths, so no *Other attributes* draws it on *Edit* or *View* and no comparison gives it a row; a save of the form keeps it in the file as it was. The JSON editor still shows it.

**A dialog's write acts in the film it was asked in.** *Move to scene…* and the file menu's *Edit JSON…* and *Plug-ins* take their film as they are asked — before the open drafts are written — so a film left meanwhile, or one holding the same stem, is never read or acted on: the file menu opens nothing.

**The copy stands a dialog over the app.** *Copy to a folder…*, from the project menu (in its drawer below 700 px) or from a page's line, shows *Copying the project to the folder…* once the folder is chosen and before the open drafts are written, and keeps it until the copy is open or has failed: neither its barrier nor Escape nor Back closes it, and it holds the focus, so a field losing its focus to it is written with the drafts and nothing a person types or clicks lands in the session after its files were read. It is taken away by its own route — never the drawer or the page beneath — and the outcome is said as before. One copy runs at a time: a second ask, from the other door or a double click before the picker answers, does nothing; and closing the window waits for a copy under way — from the folder's choice, through the drafts' settle, the copy and its open — within the exit's bound, as for the other exit tasks. A write not made by a person (an agent's field, a plug-in's run finishing) can still land in the session while the copy runs.

### 9.6 Dictation — `filmopen_dictation`

`EditableTextTarget` in `filmopen_ui` is the shared editable-selection contract for the editor, dictation and the assistant. It contains no controller, widget or Riverpod dependency. It captures the current selection as UTF-16 base and extent offsets and an opaque target identity plus revision. One `ProvisionalTextEdit` replaces its own provisional span as partial text changes and seals before committing or cancelling, so reentrancy and a thrown callback cannot commit twice. Each mutation rechecks generation and read-only state; a stale handle never edits or cleans up a replacement target. Target implementations preserve ordinary field validation, caret and undo semantics, and invalidate and remove their provisional spans after external deletion, target switch or a read-only transition. Dictation reaches editable fields through `DictationTarget`, whose optional editable target uses this capture for inline insertion; plain controllers use `ControllerDictationTarget`.

- **Where the microphone is.** A small microphone at the right of every box a person types into — a form's prose, names and numbers alike (`FormScope.dictate`, which places the AI icon after it where the assistant's catalog offers an action on the field, §9.3), every `ShortNameField` (through its suffix builder), the tree's filter, the new-project and new-entity dialogs, the version label, the epochs editor, a relationship's two boxes, the boxes of a scene's open block (§9.3), the JSON editor's value dialog, and the description of a recording (*Describe the problem*, §6.8). **It is one widget, and every microphone looks and behaves the same** — the AI assistant's placeholder microphone aside (§9.3), which is no box's, dictates nothing and is disabled on every build: a box only places it — `DictateButton(target: …)`, or a form's `FormScope.dictate` — and how it looks and behaves is decided in one place, `DictationLook` in `filmopen_dictation`: **one control in every box**, with no microphone menu beside it — **which microphone records is chosen in one place, Settings → General → Dictation** (`DictationSettingsCard`), which keeps the chosen microphone's name beside its id so as to name it without asking the system, and logs both — and **the microphone not a Tab stop**, so Tab goes from box to box and the microphone is a click away. A microphone chosen there records from then on, a dictation under way included; one no longer offered records from the system's, the choice kept for when it returns. The height of the box beside it is the theme's (`InputDecorationTheme.suffixIconConstraints`: an icon at a box's right never makes a box taller than its neighbours). `scripts/tests/test_microphone_one_place.py` fails where a box decides any of it for itself. It is left off secrets (the key cards' boxes and the e-mailed sign-in code), boxes speech cannot fill (the sign-in e-mail, folder paths and the JSON editor's text mode), a box that is read-only or disabled, and the web build. The microphone is 32 px in the theme's compact suffix slot, so it never makes a box taller. **While it records it is a square stop** (`Icons.stop_rounded`), in every box alike, with a ring around it whose width follows the voice's level, so the person sees when they are too loud or too soft: `DictationController.level`, the current frame's loudness from 0 to 1 against the room's level as the speech gate judges it — a quarter at what the gate hears as speech, 1 at four times that —, set every frame and 0 once capture stops, never logged frame by frame; whether a build shows one at all is `DictationLook.available` (not the web), which a box that keeps room around its microphone — the tree's filter, beside its clear button — asks rather than decides.
- **Settings' *Microphone*** lists the microphones the system offers, asked when it opens, anchored at the pull-down. The choice is a preference (`dictationDevice`), the chosen microphone's name kept with its id (`dictationDeviceLabel`, read back only beside that id — every build shares the preferences, and a build that chose another without keeping a name leaves a name that is no one's), both put back by the agent's `quit`; with no name kept the pull-down says *The microphone you chose*, and opening it brings the name back where that microphone is offered; `settings.dictationDevice` logs its id, its name and whether it is the system's default. A microphone no longer offered falls back to the default, which the menu then checks; a second press while the menu is opening opens no second one. A microphone chosen while a dictation listens takes over at once, in the same session and chain (through *Stop* and *Resume*, whose rules then hold); one chosen while it is stopped, or while its session opens, records from the next start.
- **The speech service** (`dictation_engine.dart`, `DictationEngineCard`). Dictation transcribes with one of two services, each a row of `dictationEngines` — its id, its catalogue entry, its platform and the host its sessions open on — a catalogue entry with its platform's file, and a `Transcriber` that speaks its messages: OpenAI's `gpt-live-transcribe` and xAI's `grok-voice-transcribe-2.0`, the Grok default at about a fifth of the price by the minute. Nothing else knows there is more than one: the controller, the speech gate, the microphone and the usage log are the same for both. The choice is Settings → General's *Speech service* card (`settings.speech-service`), a pull-down that names each service as the catalogue does — its platform, the platform's name for the model and the price of a minute — drawn where the catalogue serves two or the selected service is unavailable. An unknown saved identifier stays selected under an unavailable-service label; a known unavailable service keeps its compact brand. Its disabled row and repair sentence remain even with no served alternative. Only choosing a served row changes the preference; dismissing a highlighted alternative restores the selected row’s label. Catalogue changes refresh its availability and words. An unknown choice or a previously unavailable row retains its disabled repair during loading and catalogue failure, without stale model, price or usable alternatives. It is a preference, `dictationEngine` (`Settings`, by the engine's id; null alone selects Grok; every nonempty saved identifier remains exact and an unknown or unservable one refuses dictation), which the agent's `quit` puts back with its device choice and label; it takes effect with the next attempt, and a dictation under way keeps the service it opened with. Each service's key is its provider plug-in's (`fo-openai:openai`, `fo-xai:xai`, §6.7), entered on that plug-in's page, and only the key of the service in use is read; the card's *Configure* (`settings.speech-service.configure`) opens the selected service's provider page under Plugins, or the list where the provider is not installed, and changes no choice. A provider that is not ready refuses the press before its key is read (`provider`). The recording's commentary inherits the same default. A press captures its selected identifier before awaiting catalogue setup; changing Settings during that wait affects the next press, even when the selection returns to its original identifier before setup answers. An attempt captures one validated setup for its label, endpoint, key slot and Usage identity; a missing or pending platform listing, including an unknown status, is unavailable before any credential, audio or Usage activity.
- **A press** starts inline dictation immediately on down and focuses its box. A focused box supplies its current selection; an unfocused box captures a caret at its end. An unfocused plain box restores that captured caret after desktop focus lands, while the same unchanged attempt remains active. Another tap stops audio, waits for final turns and commits their ordered aggregate once at the captured range. Prose targets replace one provisional span as words are revised, so the words show in the box as they are spoken and Stop settles them there. The chat's reply box does the same with one difference: the person's own edit during dictation ends the attempt and keeps the box as they left it, the dictated words included, while a read-only transition — the chat stale, completed or finished — removes the live words as in a prose field. Other plain controllers keep their committed text and show those words in a small wrapping caption beside the microphone. Neither kind publishes provisional words to a file, callback or issue recording. Empty final text cancels without a change. Selection-only movement preserves the captured range.
- **Click, hold and keyboard** share the captured attempt. A short press toggles: its release keeps recording until the next press. A starting press held for at least 300 milliseconds stops and commits on release; the release of a stopping press never starts again. A cancelled pointer discards its starting capture, and another pointer cannot operate it. The microphone is the theme's primary blue throughout returned audio capture, including pending Usage or service acceptance and until physical stop completes, and stays out of the Tab order; a stop that fails or passes its limit is said as a failure (below). Inside the focused editable box, Ctrl+Shift+Space follows the same short or held rule, named in the tooltip. One early focus handler finds the mounted microphone by the focused controller and consumes Space down, repeats and matching up; hardware observers still receive them. Repeats never toggle, and an ordinary Space cannot become a shortcut by adding modifiers later. A held chord retains its original target even when focus or modifiers change. Cancellation invalidates the captured press, so an old release cannot stop a replacement attempt. Hiding a retained field cancels capture even when its animations continue. Pending physical or session cleanup names its stopping state instead of inviting another dictation; completion restores the ordinary state and shortcut hint. The handler is installed with the first microphone and removed with the last. Click and hold are always available; no preference changes the gesture, and an old stored hold-switch value is ignored.
- **A failure** remains in the microphone's error colour and tooltip until the next attempt. A missing key or a provider that is not ready (`provider`, §6.7) on a page also offers *Open <provider>* — *Open OpenAI*, *Open Grok / xAI* — on its transient message, with `dictation.open-keys`; a refused client handshake offers the same page action without claiming the key is wrong. Inside a dialog, a missing key or a provider not ready shows that identified text action beside the microphone instead of posting a message behind the modal barrier. Other dialog failures remain in the tooltip. Open keys follows each dialog's guarded pop before Settings → Plugins opens on the provider's page, where its key is (`openPlugin`, taken once by the tab); a refused pop leaves its page and settings untouched. The dialog action stays keyboard reachable while the microphone remains out of the Tab order, and clears at the next attempt. Costs are shown in Usage and Activity, with no counter in the field.
- **Text the person edits is theirs.** An external text edit, a switched target, a read-only transition or a removed microphone synchronously seals the capture. Leaving its rail page or tab, hiding its page or covering its route also cancels, even when the field stays mounted. A tab transition refused by its guard keeps the capture. Late session events cannot overwrite that text. Cancellation ends audio and removes the provisional span or caption; restoration requested during a widget build waits until after the frame, with recorder protection retained through the restoration frame. If the same controller receives a new field identifier during cancellation, that identifier also stays protected until restoration has reached semantics. A pending start is joined before its controller is disposed or another attempt can begin. A failed setup releases only its still-owned capture and keeps exception text out of the failure tooltip.
- **Applying** goes through the box's own formatters, length limit and `onChanged`, as typing does (`applyDictation`). At the captured range the words are spaced from the surrounding text and shortened to the room a length limit leaves, so the limit never cuts what follows the caret. Words the box's rules empty leave it unchanged and give the microphone a refusal tooltip; a box already at its length limit says that no room remains (`DictationResult`). The plain adapter retains that result for the microphone's sentence. A rejected provisional edit or a throwing acceptance callback cancels with the same inline refusal; the publication guard clears on every path, and changes made by target listeners are rechecked immediately after a partial. A finalization is sealed before waiting for final turns, so duplicate stops and reentrant callbacks cannot publish twice.
- **A box bound to what it belongs to** (`DictationTarget.onStart`, `DictationTarget.changed`): `onStart` runs before the selection is captured or anything is heard, and `changed` is asked while the attempt is live and before final words go in. Where it answers, the words are dropped, its sentence becomes the microphone's error tooltip and nothing is written. A scene's block binds its boxes so (§9.3); a form's field, whose box is its key's, has no need to.
- **The session** (`DictationController`). The key is read on the gesture, and a stored value under twelve characters counts as no key. The microphone starts before anything is written or sent, so one the system refuses costs nothing. Its observable capture state is independent of transcription phase and remains active until recording stop completes. Its capture-cleanup future joins pending audio starts and returned recordings, so editing retirement does not detach recording indication before physical cleanup. Each audio start first acquires a fresh microphone-owner token, whose kind is dictation or recording commentary, independently of the Usage field. A busy microphone refuses before audio, transcription or Usage starts, naming a known owner kind and otherwise speaking generically. Stop seals frame delivery and releases only after recording stop completes; Resume joins that cleanup and reacquires with a fresh token, remaining stopped if another owner acquired first. Concurrent shutdown callers join the same stop, which waits at most three seconds for the recording to stop. A stop that fails or does not answer in that time is logged with its error (`dictation.captureStopFailed`) and answered with the microphone-failure code — `close()` completes false, and the field's microphone and the recording's commentary say so — and the owner is released all the same, so the next press acquires the microphone afresh; nothing retries the stop. Text and Usage finalization have bounded waits and happen once. A field that departs closes its controller and then disposes it; the app's exit still waits for its last record. The chain's `started` record, with the first minute as its estimate, is written before the session opens, and a log that cannot take it stops the dictation. `running` follows each full minute of audio sent, and `succeeded`, with the minutes sent to six places, whenever an accepted session closes — by finalization, cancellation, the silence limit, the platform's limit or a dropped connection — `canceled`, with no cost, when the inline attempt ended before the platform accepted the session, since nothing was sent; or `failed`, with its code and no cost, when the session was never accepted otherwise (`connection` where the end gave no reason). Closing stops whatever is still starting where it is: before the key or the microphone, without a word or a log line; once the first record is written, with that chain ended `canceled` and logged. A microphone started for a session not yet opened goes off at once, even while the first record waits for another copy's lock. Nothing starts after a close. While a session is ending — its end come, its last record on its way — the controller refuses another resume until that end is recorded, so a resumed microphone always has a session that can stop it. *Stop* takes effect at once, so no frame after the press is sent, and a session that ends meanwhile keeps its end; a second *Resume* while the first still starts the microphone starts no second one. *Stop* turns the microphone off and keeps the session; *Resume* starts it again, or, after an end, opens a new session with a chain of its own while the text stays. Five minutes without speech end a session, and so do 60 minutes — OpenAI's limit for a session, which the controller keeps for both speech services, xAI publishing none — with the controller raising its near-limit state five minutes before. The inline field shows no session status, near-limit warning or cost counter. A microphone that fails while recording ends the session with its code (`mic_rate` where the system changed the format asked for). Closing waits at most three seconds for the chain's last record, which is retried until it is written (§16). Closing the window, or the agent's `quit`, ends every open dictation too (`ExitTasks`, in `filmopen_state`), within the drafts' ten seconds and waiting for each chain's last record, so a normal exit leaves no chain pending; a crash still does (§16).
- **On FilmOpen credit** (§6.6, `DictationController`). Where the speech service is OpenAI's and no key is stored, the gesture asks the account for credit for `transcription` on `openai` (`ProviderFunding.funding`). With credit the session opens OpenAI's own protocol at the website's transcription pass-through, `wss://<kFilmOpenDomain>/v1/portal/transcription` (`portalTranscriptionEndpoint`), its upgrade carrying the portal's headers for a fresh operation (`portalHeaders`) and that operation, a v4 UUID, in `X-FilmOpen-Operation` (`transcriptionOperationHeader`); the provider plug-in is checked again after those waits, as after a key's read, before the microphone; everything after the socket opens is the session as on a key — the same messages, the same 24 kHz audio, the same ends. Without credit, or without the operation's credential, the gesture is `no_key` as before. Credit pays OpenAI alone: where the chosen service is Grok and its key is not stored, the same credit is asked for OpenAI's `transcription` and, with credit, the session runs on OpenAI's validated setup from the catalogue (`dictationCreditSetupProvider`: its model, entry, platform and estimate in the records) exactly as above, the active field naming OpenAI's service and the recording commentary's provider refusal naming OpenAI's plug-in, and `dictation.funding` saying `reason: fallback` with `selected: xai`; the saved choice stays Grok, and a stored Grok key is used as before, credit never asked. The chain's records say `route` `filmopen.ai` and carry no key; its `running` records carry no cost, their basis `unknown`, and the live cost (`DictationController.cost`, the recording commentary's tooltip) is unknown — *Cost unknown* — never the catalogue's price. After the session closes, its operation is ended once (`end`) and its receipt read once (`receipt`): the last record's cost is the receipt's charge in US cents, on the provider's basis, or unknown where no receipt came — a session refused, failed or canceled before it was accepted included, never free once anything was sent. The End, the receipt and the last record are one write, counted before anything is awaited, so that the app's exit waits for it while the field's close stays bounded at three seconds and the microphone goes off at once; a second close repeats neither. A refused upgrade on credit is `credit_ws_<status>` — the session refused with its status, with no door to a key, whatever the status (`402`, `503` and the rest), never the refused-key sentence; a refused credential (`credit_ws_401`) is let go (`portalRefused`) and nothing is sent again, neither End nor receipt, while any other refused status still ends the operation and reads its receipt once. A credential that expires during a dictation is the server's to close, said as a dropped session; a receipt that never comes leaves the cost unknown.
- **Turns** (`SpeechGate`). `gpt-live-transcribe` takes no voice activity detection, only commits, so the device decides where a turn of speech ends. Each 100 ms frame sent is measured by its level, the root of its samples' mean square, against the room's, which the gate follows as it goes: a quieter frame brings the room down to it at once, and a louder one lifts it by at most 3 % a frame, so that a steady noise becomes the room within seconds. While the last two seconds are as uneven as a voice — their loudest frame at least twice their quietest — the room rises no higher than their loudest frame's level divided by four and a half, so that a voice which talks on without a pause keeps being heard, even one whose syllables vary by less than three times; a steady noise that has sounded two seconds on its own is learned. The room starts at 50 on the 16-bit scale and never goes below it, so that digital silence does not make every sound speech and a voice already under way when the audio starts — a held microphone's, as a rule — is heard as speech. It is kept across *Stop*, *Resume*, a new session and a change of microphone, whose own room it learns as it learns any other. A frame three times louder than the room is speech, and speech keeps the session from its five-minute limit, as the platform's `speech_started` did. A turn ends after 0.8 s of quiet that follows its speech, or after 30 s without such a pause. It is committed (`input_audio_buffer.commit`) where it held at least 0.2 s of speech — a short name — and cleared (`input_audio_buffer.clear`) where it held less, as a click does, or where it held a second or more of speech and was still even when it ended, its loudest frame less than twice its quietest: a noise that went on sounding while the room learned it, or an even sound cut by *Stop*. The quiet that ends a turn counts, so an even sound that stops — a beep — is committed, as a cough is, and so is a flat voice followed by a pause. Quiet with no speech is cleared every ten seconds, so the platform never holds long silence. *Stop* ends the turn in progress the same way, or leaves it alone where it held no speech, and nothing is committed or cleared before a session is accepted or after it ends. Stopping inline insertion while listening or while a committed turn's text is on its way first waits for the final text of every turn committed (`settle`: at most three seconds, then the text as it stands; what got no answer in that time is not waited for again, though a late answer is taken). A commit refused for want of audio (`input_audio_buffer_commit_empty`) and a turn the platform could not transcribe wait for nothing more, the latter keeping what arrived of it, and a session's end ends the wait. The defaults are untried on real microphones (§16).
- **For a commentary** (`cut`, `commits`, `stretches`; the recording's, §6.8 — field dictation uses none of them). `cut()` ends the turn in progress where it holds speech, sending one commit while the microphone stays on, and in silence commits nothing and clears the quiet; while connecting, stopped, ended or failed, once `close()` has begun, and from a listener of `commits` while it is raised, it does nothing. `commits` counts the commits sent, raised as each is sent, before any answer, and a turn the platform ended by itself while no commit waited; it is never reset, across sessions. `stretches` gives every commit once, in the order sent, with the words of its own turn (after those of any turn the platform ended by itself while it waited); a refused commit, a turn that could not be transcribed, the commits a session leaves unanswered at its end and, at `settle()`'s limit, those still unanswered are given with nothing, and a late answer adds none. It is synchronous, so `settle()` and `close()` return after what they gave is delivered.
- **The connection** (`dictationSetupFrom` out of `catalogueProvider`, for the engine in use; `EngineTranscriber`). The URL, the model and the price per minute come from the engine's bundled entry — `stt-gpt-live-transcribe` on `openai`, `stt-grok-transcribe-20` on `xai` — and the setup refuses an endpoint that is not `wss` on one of its platform's hosts with nothing before the host (`PlatformFile.mayReceiveCredentials`), since the key goes wherever the endpoint points, and a platform file that takes its key other than as a bearer token (`Authorization: Bearer <key>`), which is how both services' sessions send it. `main` installs one transcriber, `createDeviceTranscriber`'s `EngineTranscriber`, which opens each session with the engine whose host the endpoint is on (`dictationEngineAt`; OpenAI's for the website's transcription pass-through, by name; the first engine's elsewhere): it chooses messages only, and where a key may go stays the setup's rule. A session's upgrade carries exactly the headers the controller gives it (`Transcriber.open`'s `headers`) and none of its own: the key's bearer, or credit's headers. What follows is OpenAI's session (`OpenAiTranscriber`). The handshake follows no redirect: dart:io would drop the key's header on the way to another host, but the audio would follow. It is logged as it begins (`dictation.handshake`), with the URL and the names of its headers, never a header's value, and a failure with its error and code. A close under way is what ends a session, even when its connection then fails or its time for accepting runs out. The session message asks for a transcription session of 24 kHz PCM for the engine's model and the language, with turn detection off (`"turn_detection": null`), and the audio frames are that PCM. A session turned down before it was accepted ends `refused`, and the log keeps the platform's error type, code and message and the field it named (`dictation.refused`), as they came.
- **xAI's session** (`XaiTranscriber`). The session is asked for in the URL's query — the model, the microphone's own 24 kHz PCM, interim results, `endpointing=2000` and the app's language where xAI formats it — and no message sets it up: `transcript.created` opens it. Audio goes as binary frames. An utterance is a turn (`turn_<n>`): words that can still change and chunks the platform has locked are sent as the turn's whole text so far (`TurnText.whole`, which replaces where OpenAI's text only grows), and the utterance's end completes it; whether that end carries the whole utterance or only its last chunk, which xAI's pages leave open, is told by where each result says it starts, so that neither reading repeats or loses words. The device still decides the turns: a commit sends `finalize`, past whose 0.8 s the platform's own 2 s ending is only the fallback, so both services cut the speech the device commits into the same turns; a clear sends nothing, since xAI keeps no buffer. xAI names no turn for a commit, as OpenAI's `item_id` does, so each commit waits on a name of its own (`commit_<n>`), released with no words right after the words of the end that answers it: an end releases every commit made before its utterance began, which no later end can answer, and then the oldest left — so two commits at once are two waits, and a `finalize` left unanswered holds up none after it. A commit with no audio after the last end, nothing shown and nothing waiting is refused here, as OpenAI refuses an empty one; the frames behind an end come from where xAI says it ended. Closing sends `audio.done`. A refusal carries no code, since xAI's error is a sentence: its message is the refusal's, for the log (`dictation.refused`); an error once the session is open changes nothing and is logged as `dictation.platformError {operation?, event, message, ms}`. Nothing here has spoken to xAI: what only a real key can show is the results note's §5.
- **The microphone** (`RecordAudioSource`, `filmopen_platform`): 24 kHz mono 16-bit PCM through `record`, cut into 100 ms frames by `PcmFramer`; its codes are `mic_unavailable` and `mic_rate`. Native shutdown shares one published future among concurrent callers and reports success only after native Stop and disposal both succeed. Failed Stop or disposal is answered `mic_unavailable` and keeps the same recorder; cleanup never retries itself. The first consumer observes the original automatic-stop result, and only a later deliberate Stop retries once. Closing frames does not confirm physical retirement. Native errors are caught and logged by code, without their text.
- **Seams**. `AudioSource`, `Transcriber` and `UsageLog` are unavailable until an entrypoint installs one: `main` installs the device's (for `Transcriber`, `createDeviceTranscriber`), and the agent build and the test harnesses install `FakeAudioSource`, `ScriptedTranscriber` and `MemoryUsageLog` — the agent build the first two unless live speech is asked for (`FILMOPEN_AGENT_LIVE_SPEECH` `1`, §4.3); the usage log stays in memory. The scripted session answers every ten frames with a turn, in two halves and then its final text, and a commit with the turn in progress at once — or, with nothing since the last turn, with a refusal, as OpenAI answers an empty commit; a clear is only counted. `FakeAudioSource` records silence unless it is given a `sound`, each frame's loudness by its place in the recording; the agent build's talks in phrases, 2.5 s of syllables and then 1.5 s of quiet.
- **Words.** A code's sentence is `labels.dart`'s `dictationCodeText`. The sentences are short, and speak of *the speech service*, whichever it is: none names a platform, the 60 minutes or the 24 kHz (`labels_dictation_test.dart` pins it in both languages). The microphone tooltip also names its validated service by compact brand and model before Start, alongside the shortcut. Pending or unavailable setup shows no stale service or model; an active attempt retains its captured identity. Other platform names appear where a person chooses or pays — its key's card, the *Speech service* pull-down and the totals of Activity's Usage. The log carries `dictation.ended {field, minutes, code, turns, cleared, quietClears, speechFrames, room, loudest, text}` — six of them how the device heard that session: its turns committed and cleared, its clears of quiet, its frames of speech, and the room's and the loudest frame's levels; `text` the words dictated so far, which the issue recorder's tape leaves out (§6.8) — and for what went wrong `dictation.refused {type, code, param, message}`, `dictation.notStarted`, `dictation.recordLost`, `dictation.setupUnreadable`, `dictation.targetRejected {field, words}`, `dictation.setupUnusable {code, engine}` and `dictation.devicesUnreadable`, a failure with its type and message: never the key. Each session's start and end are logged too, the controller's lines with its operation on credit — after `dictation.funding`, which decides it — and `ms` since the gesture: `dictation.funding {engine, outcome, reason}` (`own`, `credit` or `unavailable`, and why), `dictation.socket.opening {operation?, route, endpoint, platform, entry, model, language, device, credential: key|portal, ms}` — the call's values, logged just before its credential enters the upgrade's headers —, `dictation.closed {outcome: stopped|stopFailed, ms}`, `dictation.session.accepted`, every phase as `dictation.phase {phase}`, `dictation.socket.refused {code}` or `dictation.socket.closed {code?, seconds}`, and on credit `dictation.end` (`status: unsent-refused` for a refused credential, which sends nothing) and `dictation.receipt {outcome, chargedCents?, receiptId?}` (the receipt's outcome as the website wrote it, `none` where none came). OpenAI's session adds, with the operation its upgrade names and `ms` since the session opened, `dictation.session.update {message}` (the configuration as sent), `dictation.socket.open`, `dictation.first_event {type}` (the platform's type as it came) and, for a turn it could not transcribe or an error once the session is open, `dictation.platformError {operation?, event, item?, type, code, param, message, ms}`, the platform's words as they came. An unexpected failure is `dictation.failed {operation?, stage, type?, error, ms}` — at the handshake (with its `code`, `ms` since the connection began), the open, either platform's connection or stream, the end or the receipt —, and a Close that throws or never answers is `dictation.closeFailed {operation?, code, type, error}` with its error's text, and its type beside it but at the handshake; never a credential or a header's value. `credit_ws_<status>` reads as the session refused with its status.
- **Identifiers**: `<box>.dictate` names each microphone, and `<box>.dictate.caption` names a plain box's provisional caption, never a value for the recorder. `dictation.open-keys` names the page message action or the inline repair inside a dialog. In Settings → General, the microphone choice is `settings.microphone`, its menu uses `dictation.device:default` and `dictation.device:<id>`; on General's keys section the service choice is `settings.speech-service`, and its *Configure* `settings.speech-service.configure`. The version label's box is `label.text`, and the account popup's username `account.username`, with `account.username.retry` and `account.username.ask-again`.

### 9.7 Activity — `filmopen_jobs_ui`

The rail's *Activity* (`nav.activity`; `NavIndex.activity`, which `NavIndex.nameOf` names `activity` for the agent's `state`, the issue report's tape and its replay), `ActivityPage`, in two tabs (`TabBar`; the agent's `set_tab` names them `now` and `files`), *Now* on arriving and again after leaving the page, each change logged at its start `activity.tab.start {tab, from, reason, shownMs}` and, once its first frame is drawn, `activity.tab {tab, from, reason: chosen | leftActivity, shownMs, ms}` — the time the tab left was shown, counted from arriving on the page, and the switch's own. **Now**: this device's usage, the media being added to the open film and, while any is, what of the film is syncing, in one scroll view. Its title, *Activity*, then *Usage*, then *Adding media* with *Dismiss finished* (`activity.dismiss-done`) beside its heading, which dismisses every addition that is done or stopped and offers a dismissal, and is disabled with the tooltip *Nothing is done or stopped yet. A failure is dismissed on its own row.* where none is: a failed job stays until it is dismissed on its own row. Under *Adding media*, while the open film has any, *Syncing* lists its sync jobs queued, working or just done (`syncJobsProvider`): one line each with the file's name, a bar where its progress is measured and a check once done; a send waiting to retry, held, failed or stopped is *Share*'s, which draws the same jobs in full. The lines carry no sentence and no action, and are logged `activity.sync.rows {film, rows: [{name, kind, phase, progress}]}` each time their files or phases change.

**Files**: every file of the open film against its cloud copy (`syncStatusProvider`, §6.14). Under *Compared with the cloud at <time>*, the groups in this order, each a heading with its count and drawn only where it has a file: *Changed on both sides*, *Problems*, *Waiting* — each with *Share says what each one it still lists needs; a conflict dismissed there stays here until the copies agree.* under it, since Share draws those jobs in full with their actions —, *Sending and receiving*, *To send*, *To fetch*, *In the cloud only*, *In sync*. A row is the file's project-relative path, wrapped rather than cut, with a bar where its progress is measured; rows carry no action. With no film: *Open a film to see where its files stand against the cloud*; with a film no pass has compared: *Nothing has been compared with the cloud yet. A film is shared from Settings → General → Sharing.* The rows are logged `activity.files.rows {film, at, counts, rows: [{path, state, code, progress}]}` once each time a row's path, state or code changes, and each step of a bar as it is drawn `activity.files.progress {film, path, progress}`, for the files whose progress moved alone.

The rail's *Share* (`nav.share`; `NavIndex.share`, named `share`), `SharePage`: the open film's sends and receipts — the sync's jobs for the open film (`syncJobsProvider`), newest first, under *Sharing*, *Nothing is being shared* where there are none — with its title, *Share*, and its own *Dismiss finished* (`share.dismiss-done`) beside the *Sharing* heading, as Activity's stands beside its list. With a film and nothing listed, the empty state says where a film is shared — *A film is shared from Settings → General → Sharing; what it sends and receives is listed here.* (`labels.dart`'s `sharingPlace`) — and *Set up sharing* (`share.set-up`) opens Settings on *General*. With no film it says *Open a film to see what it is sending and receiving*, a sign-in under way keeping its wait and its *Cancel* above that line. How the film is shared — the target, the account, *Stop sharing* — is Settings' *Sharing* card (§9.4). Both pages draw their rows through the one row logic of the package (`job_lists.dart`): what follows of a row, an action, a refusal and a sign-in holds on both.

**Usage** (`filmopen_keys/usage_tab.dart`'s `UsageSection`, §6.7, plan), which the application hands to Activity as a builder — read once as Activity is first built at start, and again each time a section is built anew — since the jobs' pages do not import the keys' package: a section with no scroll view of its own, drawn first, with or without a film — the totals of this device's usage log by month and platform (a free call on the person's own key — a status check, a key check, any server-provider function such as a server's offers, deployment, keep-alive or stop, and a plug-in's switch on or off — is in the log of events only, never here, §6.7) — spent, pending or possibly charged, and the uses whose cost is unknown (*1 use, cost unknown*), where anything is, *Spent* left out where nothing was spent and a cost is unknown — and each use: what it was and the box it was for, when, the minutes, its state (a failed dictation with its code's sentence; a failed render as *The render did not finish (…)*, by its plug-in's code or the application's, and any other plug-in call as *It did not finish (…)* — `usageErrorText`, never a code bare, and said once: in place of the status word, as a clause of the line without its own period; the code as the tile says it, `bad-result` and not `bad_result`), what it counts as, where that cost comes from — *estimated*, *at the catalogue's price*, *as the platform reported* or *charged to your FilmOpen credit* (the portal's settled receipt, its id after it as *receipt …*), or *not yet settled by filmopen.ai* for a use on credit whose receipt had not settled (`labels.dart`'s `usageBasisLabel`, the counted money's basis) — and the platform's own id of its request, job or session where any record of the use names one (*request …*), by which the platform's usage page finds it; under a use on FilmOpen credit, one button per operation, *operation …*, which copies the operation's id — the one the filmopen.ai portal's ledger shows — and says *Operation id copied.*, or *The operation id could not be copied.* where the clipboard refused, logging `usage.operationCopy` with the id before the clipboard is asked, then `usage.operationCopied` with the id, its outcome and the milliseconds; the card above says that each cost comes from one of the four. Each use is named by its model as the render dialog names it — the catalogue entry's `name` (`ProviderCatalogue.models`), a dictation and a server's time keeping their own words — and by the box a person saw (`labels.dart`'s `usageBoxLabel`: a form field by its label, a project's name as its *Title*, a render's group by its place's title, another box by its own) and the file it belonged to — a render's the version it was of, never the project file — a voice kept adding *· a voice kept*, since it holds a slot at its platform; and a failed dictation's sentence there offers no *Resume*. A use that asked no model — a plug-in's *Check* that reads its key, an action or a request of its own, whose record's `entry` is `unknown` (`unknownUsageEntry`) — is named by the plug-in that made it and what it did, from the record's `purpose.plugin` (`labels.dart`'s `usagePluginUse`: *OpenRouter · Check* for its `status`, an action by its label, a server provider's seven functions in words — *SaladCloud · Prices of servers*, *Start a server*, *Keep a server awake*, *Stop a server* and the rest — and an entity hook's likewise, *Template · Rewrite*, any other function by its name, a developer's own as the developer page shows it, from the plug-in's page or the developer page alike), the plug-in found by the tag its stem holds, so that a use of an earlier version bears the name the plug-in has now, and one no longer installed is named by that tag; its cost is what the platform said, which for a check is nothing, so it reads *Cost unknown*. Each use's cost is in one of two columns headed *Your Key* and *FilmOpen Credits* (each on two lines), by how the record says it was paid (its `route`: `direct` is the person's own key, the cell shaded with the theme's primary colour at low alpha, legible in light and dark; `filmopen.ai` is FilmOpen credit), the other cell empty, and a use whose record names neither in neither column, saying *paid how: unknown*. The totals are split the same way: under the same two headings, each month and platform's line puts, in each column, that route's own *Spent*, *Pending*, *Possibly charged* and *N uses, cost unknown*, one under the other where any is (`usageTotals` of the uses of each route; the own key's cell shaded), nothing combined beneath, and a route whose only calls of the month cost what nobody knows, in a currency the month has no line in or with no estimate, counted on the month and platform's first line; a use whose record names neither route counts in neither, and a line under the totals says how many (*1 use is in neither column: how it was paid is unknown.*). Under the totals, a line says what *pending* and *possibly charged* mean wherever either is shown, and another what *cost unknown* means wherever it is; such a use's cost reads *Cost unknown*. The log is read again each time Activity is shown again (the dashboard calls `UsageSection.refresh` as the rail enters it; never on the web), since another copy of the app may have written and nothing of this app's says so, and after each append of this app's own, keeping the lines on screen meanwhile; a month filter applies on the device, and a call of a version this app does not know is left out and said. The web build says it keeps no log. Identifiers: `usage.month`, `usage.totals`, `usage.uses`, and `usage.operation.<id>` per operation. *Activity → Usage* is where a sentence elsewhere says a cost is read (`labels.dart`'s `usagePlace`). Money and decimals are written from their exact decimal, with each language's mark, money as every amount is (§9.5; `labels.dart`'s `amountText`, `decimalText`). Under this device's cards, **Shared plug-ins on your account** (`usage.shared`), read from the account's ledger (§6.6, §6.7) for the month shown: one row per lending settlement or earning — *Earned from a shared plug-in* with a plus sign in `AppColors.positive` (green in both themes), *Paid for a shared plug-in* with a true minus sign in the text's own colour, since spending is not an error; the words and the signs carry the meaning, never the colour alone (`labels.dart`'s `signedMoneyText`: `+$0.40`, `−$0.10`, `$0.00`, written as every amount is, §9.5) — under it the date and time and *request …* with the lending job's id, and no offer, quantity or other party, which the wire does not carry; then the totals as three texts in a wrap, *Earned +$0.40* (green above zero), *Paid −$0.10* (plain) and *Net +$0.30* (green above zero, plain otherwise), the net covering the shared plug-ins alone, since this device's spending goes to a platform and not through the account. One line otherwise: *Sign in to see what you earned and paid for shared plug-ins.* signed out or refused; *Your account's earnings and payments could not be read just now.* where the ledger could not be read; *Nothing earned or paid for shared plug-ins.* where it holds none for the month; and under the rows *Only the newest records are counted here.* where older pages were left unread. A re-read keeps the rows on screen. The month filter lists this device's months and the account's together — the website lists every month it has a record in, whatever month was asked — and shows whenever any month exists; a month with no use of this device's shows this device's empty state in place of its cards, the account's card beneath. This device's part and the card never hide each other: a log that cannot be read still shows the card. The web build keeps its sentence and no card.

**Adding media**: insertion's jobs whose place key's first element is the open film's key, together with generic unplaced application rows; *Nothing is being added*. With no film and no unplaced application jobs it says *Open a film to see the media being added to it*, the usage above it staying. Supplied jobs with no place stay visible with or without a film; malformed non-null places grant no placement. The list is newest first — the service answers in the order the jobs were submitted, and the page reverses it. An addition a place has said done is dismissed by that place (§9.3), so the list holds the additions that run, wait, fail or stop, and one done while its own place is not drawn (§6.13). The page listens to insertion's views from its first build, which is at the application's start, since the rail builds its pages together, and sends an addition's actions to that service. The installed service seeds each subscriber with its current cached snapshot, then publishes changes. A cached job predating the listener is shown immediately; placed jobs are filtered to the open film, while supplied unplaced jobs remain visible without one.

Leaving either page stops neither service, and each keeps its state while another page is shown. A render for a film with no folder that another film has since replaced (§6.13) is that film's and shows in neither list; the application says so as each such job ends — *Something you asked for finished after you left its film, which had no folder, so nothing it made stays in a film. What it cost is in Activity → Usage.* — once for each (`filmReplacedRendersProvider`, heard by the dashboard), and the job's own sentence says the same (`jobFilmReplaced`).

A row is a `JobRow`, a semantics node of its own, so that a screen reader reads its name and its sentence together, one row at a time, and an agent's `find` answers one node per row. It shows the job's display name, else its reference's last segment, else its kind's word (*A document*, *A media file*, *An addition*). `isSyncFetchKind` recognizes exactly `sync.document.fetch` and `sync.media.fetch`; both use the same document/media fallback names as sends. `jobStateText(code, phase:, name:, sending:, receiving:, syncOutcome:)` selects the direction's state and phase words. Receiving phases say *Waiting to receive…*, *Receiving…*, *Received.*, *This file could not be received.* or *This file needs a person before it can be received.* Sending phases retain *Waiting to send…*, *Sending…*, *Sent.*, *This file could not be sent.* and *This file needs attention.* Additions retain their own words, including *Adding…* and *Added.* Unknown waiting phases say the common waiting sentence; cancelled jobs say *Stopped.* For a done fetch only, the row supplies its checkpoint outcome: `keptLocal` says *Local copy kept.* (*Se conservó la copia local.*), and `alreadyAgreed` says *Already in step.* (*Ya está sincronizado.*). Unknown or absent outcomes preserve *Received.*; these markers change neither a send nor an unfinished fetch. A resolved conflict which sent the local copy therefore never claims that a file was received. Render additions use their supplied safe phase and failure sentence as their insertion tile does: a queued render says *Waiting to render…*. A before-send binding change says that nothing was sent and asks the person to open Render to choose again; it grants no retained-receipt action. The labels also map pending local acknowledgement to an accepted result whose saving or cleanup is waiting, and an unread carried journal to a saved render needing attention. The application's insertion adapter associates readable render attention by the insertion-owned validated recovery id, preserving insertion placement and presentation. Tile and Activity route only offered manager actions, without resubmitting accepted work. A render journal entry this build cannot read has no row: the manager logs it and drops it (§6.15). The row draws only the actions its supplied view offers. A refused Dismiss-only insertion continues to say its refusal after collection. A producer binding refusal needing attention uses the existing action-neutral insertion sentence about the changed film or settings whenever Retry is absent, including Dismiss alone and Stop alone. With Retry offered, the row retains the render recovery sentence and exactly its offered Retry/Dismiss actions. While its exact render still offers Stop, the row and tile offer that Stop through the manager instead of removing the insertion; ordinary dismissal returns after terminal cleanup. Activity's *Syncing* lines are not `JobRow`s: a name, a measured bar and a check, a semantics node each, with no sentence and no action.

Shared sync codes keep their common sentences for sign-in, film closure, unavailable or busy targets, refusal, missing folders, missing configuration, metered holds, full Drive and unsafe checkpoints. A full Drive or changed upload position distinguishes an automatic retry from a job requiring *Retry*. Invalid paths, transfers, lengths and content use *The file could not be received as it is.* for fetches and the existing placement sentence for sends. A received mismatch says the target changed while receiving and retries; after retries stop it asks the person to press *Retry*. A target file gone, a local name already taken and an unreadable received document each have their own sentence. Additions continue to interpret their own codes, so their `unavailable` and `busy` use their phases rather than a target sentence. *Paused.* and *Stopping…* apply to every direction. An accepted Stop whose cleanup binding fails continues to say *Stopping…* without another Stop button while the saved binding retry is pending.

A conflict row is named by its file reference, with *Changed here and at the target; nothing is sent or received until you choose.* and the offered *Take theirs*, *Keep mine* and *Dismiss* actions. Its hashes in stateArgs are never displayed. Every row uses the error colour when failed or needing a person, a progress bar while working or waiting with a measure, and one text button per offered action under `job:<id>:<action>`. The actions wrap below the sentence on narrow pages; receiving, received and conflict rows fit at 375, 700 and 1200 px in English and Spanish. Share routes these actions through the existing sync service door.

An action goes to the service that owns the job: the sync's for a `sync.` kind, insertion's for the rest. An action under way is drawn disabled, with *Wait for the step under way to finish.*, until it answers, so a second press sends nothing, while another action of the same row goes (*Stop* while the row's sign-in waits). Every action, on Activity and on Share alike, is logged as it is sent (`activity.action`: the job's id and kind, the action's code, the job's phase and state) and as it ends (`activity.actionDone`: the id, the action, its `outcome` — `accepted`, `refused`, `failed`, or `unsent` where the page closed first — its `code` and its `ms`). A refusal, or an action that threw (logged as `activity.actionFailed` with the job's id, the action's code and the error's type and text), is said under its row — *That could not be done now: the job may have moved on. Try again.* — and kept beside the job as it was drawn then: its phase, its state and the actions it offers, not its progress, which a send moves every few percent. It is erased at the next action pressed on the row, once the job moves on, once the job is no longer listed and when a row is no longer listed; a job that moves on and comes back as it was shows no old refusal, and a film opened again shows none of before. A row that no longer offers the action when the answer comes — stopped meanwhile — shows no refusal: its state says what happened.

A **`signIn`** action runs the application's sign-in first: `beforeSignIn`, chosen by the open film's target when the row asks. For a film on FilmOpen Cloud it is the account's — its popup on Settings and a wait of at most 30 s for the account, its own wait line and *Cancel* (§9.4) —; for any other it is the Sharing model's `ensureSignedIn` (§9.4), and what follows in this paragraph is that Google sign-in: which tries the held sign-in with a refresh and opens the consent page only where there is none or Google refuses it — one wait for the card and *Share*, which a row's *Sign in* joins where the card started it. While it waits, every row that asked says *Signing in to Google: finish in your browser if it opens.* beneath it, and the first such row drawn carries the one **Cancel** (`activity.sign-in-cancel`) — where every row that asked has left the list meanwhile (stopped, dismissed, another film opened), the wait and its *Cancel* stand under the *Sharing* heading instead — which calls the application's `cancelSignIn`: the wait ends at once, `beforeSignIn` answers false, no action is sent, and an answer that comes after stores nothing. The card's *Cancel* ends the same wait. Only a sign-in that answers true sends the action to the sync, which lifts the sign-in hold of the job's lane — every send of its kind that waits on it; one that does not finish leaves the hold, and the row says *The sign-in did not finish, so nothing was sent.* beneath its own call for one. Back cancels nothing; there is no dialog. Drawing the page, which the rail builds at the application's start, performs no sign-in and reads no credential. The page draws public `JobView`s and sends public `JobAction`s; it never reads a journal or starts a runner.

### 9.7a GPU — `filmopen_render_ui`

The rail's *GPU* (`nav.gpu`; `NavIndex.gpu`, named `gpu`), `GpuPage`, has the owner's three tabs in their order — **Servers**, **Share my plugins**, **Using remote plugins** — in a scrolling tab bar, so that the third is reached at 375 px, over a body that is a `switch` on the tab, as Settings builds its own; the tabs carry no identifier, and an agent opens them by name, `servers`, `share` and `remote` (`set_tab`; `state.tab` answers the one open). The page opens on *Servers* and goes back to it when GPU is left, as Settings goes back to *General* (§9.4): the dashboard keeps the page alive in its stack, so the page listens to `navIndexProvider` itself, and a link to the page — the render dialog's *Open GPU* — lands on *Servers*. **Arriving on the page is its refresh**, since it has no *Refresh*: each arrival asks every enabled server provider for its servers again (`ServerSessions.check`, reason `gpu-page`, §6.15) and this computer's render engines for their status (`refreshLocalStatus`, §6.11), and reads the lent offers for GPU's two lines (below), once, in the background; staying on the page asks nothing, and nothing polls. The page has no heading: the rail and the tab name it.

**GPU's two lines.** Under the rail's *GPU* label two `labelSmall` lines, one line each, ellipsised and centred — a lone line has the room of two, as the account's lone status line has —, name the one option a render will use: the first that holds, in the owner's order (`gpu_status.dart`: `gpuStatusOf`, read through `gpuStatusProvider`). **A server** the sessions keep that is not over — started here or found at its provider — line 1 its provider's own name (`ServerSessions.labelOf`); line 2 *unconfirmed* where its end is unconfirmed past its deadline (§6.15), whatever its phase, else *active* where a render holds it, *ready* where it runs, *unsure* where it was made and its provider no longer lists it, *starting* (*waiting for a GPU* while its provider allocates one), *stopping* — chosen in that order, the oldest first within each; the badge and *Servers* keep the others. **This computer's render plug-in**, among the enabled platform plug-ins `localPlatformsOf` names — its name; *active* where a render under way runs on its local route (the plug-in itself or one that calls it, no destination or a local one), *ready* where its status is ready as *Servers* reads it, else *offline*. **This computer's graphics card**, where one was read — its name without the vendor's words, *available*. **Another device of the account** — *Your device* / *ready* — then **someone else's shared plug-in** — *Shared* / *ready* —, each only where a lent offer is eligible as the render dialog decides: published, available, a render of a model an installed, enabled sharing plug-in renders, and a price that can be computed — judged again against the plug-ins whenever they change, so that an offer whose plug-in is turned off stops counting without a new read; a read that was refused or late says *unknown*, never an earlier *ready*, and a read that lands after a newer one was taken is dropped. Else *none* where the card was read and there is none, and *not detected* where it could not be read. The card is read once a session through `nvidia-smi` (`filmopen_machine`, Linux and Windows): NVIDIA only, so a card that could not be read claims nothing and a remote offer still shows. The button's tooltip says its name, the badge's sentence, the two lines and, where they speak of the card, that cards are detected for NVIDIA only; the lines' own text is left out of the semantics, so that they are said once, in the tooltip. The answer is made again whenever a server, a render job, a plug-in or its readiness changes; the lent offers are read, bounded to three seconds, at start once a plug-in that could render one is there, at each sign-in — a change of the account's authentication into signed in, its read's reason `signIn`, each change logged `gpu.lending.account` with where it came from, where it went and whether it read; a session restored at start reads so too —, once more when the device's registration lands after a read refused `device-not-admitted` — heard through the funding resolver's notice, which the registration raises, its read's reason `deviceAdmitted`, each notice logged `gpu.lending.funding {awaitingAdmission, registered, device, read}`; any other funding change reads nothing, and a refusal is not retried otherwise — and on each arrival at the page; nothing polls. A sign-out lets the former account's offers go at once — the two lines and *Using remote plugins* stop counting them, a read still under way is dropped — logged `gpu.lending.cleared` with how many it let go and the read numbers dropped, even where no read had landed yet. Each change of answer is logged `gpu.status` with the facts it weighed; each lending read, numbered, `gpu.lending.read.start`, then `.done` with every offer as returned and its eligibility and why, or `.failed` with its code and message, or `.skipped` where no plug-in could render one; an older read dropped is logged `gpu.lending.read.superseded`, and an offer's decision changed by a plug-in change `gpu.lending.rejudged`. The app names no provider: the names are the platform file's and the plug-in's.

**Servers**, the first tab (`servers`): every server the application's sessions keep (`ServerSessions`, §6.15), the newest first, ended and found ones included — nothing is filtered. Page and badge read one projection, `gpuSessionsProvider` (`gpu_sessions.dart`), seeded with the sessions kept, so that a restored one shows at once, and made again on the sessions' `changes` and on the plug-in settings' `changes`, so that *Start server* follows a provider turned on or off though no session changed. Each row says of its server: its GPU, its phase (`serverPhaseText`, an unsure server as such; a starting one whose provider is still assigning it a GPU — its `providerState` `allocating` — *Waiting for … to assign a GPU*, `serverWaitingForGpu`), its model stack where it names one (*Model stack: …*), *Found at …; not started from this FilmOpen* for one its provider listed that this app did not ask for, its lease, a stop its provider took and did not carry out, a stop still owed past its lease (and, its provider's plug-in off, that it cannot be asked), past its deadline with its end unconfirmed *Its time ran out at …. … has not confirmed it stopped.* in place of its lease (and, its provider's plug-in off, how to stop it), though a server starting or running past its lease with its provider's plug-in off says instead *Its time without work ran out at …, and … was still running it when last seen: stop it at …, or turn its plug-in on so that FilmOpen can.* (`serverLeaseOverrunOff`); what it ran for — while unconfirmed, no later than its deadline or than when it was last seen running past that — and its estimate, its last problem. **Stop** (`gpu.<name>.stop`) follows the sessions' own refusals in their order (`gpuStopOf`): an ended server owing no stop has none; a deployment its provider has not answered with a server is disabled with *Not started yet: there is no server to stop*; a server whose provider's plug-in is off with *Turn …'s plug-in on so that FilmOpen can ask for its stop, or stop it at …*; one already asked to stop with *Stop was asked…* until its deadline, past which, unconfirmed, Stop asks its provider again (*Press Stop to ask … to stop it again.*); a refusal is said under the row, and a press is never left held. Under a running server — the lending loop serves an offer from the first running server of its provider and GPU offer that holds its stack — *Stop makes its N shared offers unavailable*, N the shared offers on that provider and GPU offer the website acknowledged as published that this server can serve and no other running one can (`Lending.published`); nothing where none is. **Start server** (`gpu.start`) is absent where no sessions are kept (the web), disabled with *Turn on a server provider's plug-in in Settings → Plugins to start a server.* where none is on, and opens the provider's offers (`ServerOffersDialog`); a server started is said once the window closes: that it starts and stops by itself after its minutes without work, or that FilmOpen is checking whether it started. A refusal said under a row goes once the session moves on or its provider is turned on or off.
