Under *Start server*, while any counts, how many servers may still be costing — the badge's count, said on the page. With no server, *No GPU server has been started* and where one comes from — or, with no provider on, why none can be started, on the page and not on a hover alone. **The rail's badge** (`nav.gpu.badge`, its own semantics node, its value the count, a press on it the button's) counts, from the same projection (`GpuSessionsView.counts`), the servers this app started that may still cost — deploying, uncertain, starting, running or stopping, or ended with a stop owed — never a found one; the button's tooltip and label keep *GPU* above *N GPU servers may still be costing you*, said once. The sessions' rules are theirs: a server its provider cannot be asked about stays listed and counted until its provider says it stopped. **This computer's ComfyUI** stands above the servers (`gpu.comfyui`), where its platform plug-in is installed: the plug-in that implements `platform` and that some plug-in rendering on `comfyui` calls, else one that implements `platform` and renders on `comfyui` itself, as the stock `fo-cui` does — never found by its tag, and a called one always first, since nothing stronger names the engine; of two copies, the development folder's and the library's, the first enabled one; no row where there is none, and none on the web. Each finding is logged `gpu.comfyui.found {by: servers | share, platforms, called, reason: called | self | none, stem, enabled, ms}`. The row names it (*This computer's ComfyUI*) and says its plug-in's state from the readiness record (§6.11), with the Plugins page's words: *Off* where the plug-in is off; *Checking…* while a phase is under way; a problem — read before the fact, as the plug-in's page reads it, since a failed *Check* keeps the earlier fact — or any fact that is not ready, in the plug-in's own words, else *What it needs is not there*; *Ready*; and, with nothing known yet, *Enabled — open to check.*, since nothing is asked of a plug-in until a person turns it on or presses *Check* — the row follows the record's changes. Its link (`gpu.comfyui.open`, *Open the plug-in's page*) opens Settings → Plugins on the plug-in's page, where *Check* is. FilmOpen does not run ComfyUI: the row has no *Start* and no *Stop*.

**Share my plugins**, the second tab (`share`), opens on the audience block (`share_audience.dart`, `ShareAudience`): who may use the offers this person publishes, read from and written to the account (`Lending.audience` / `setAudience`, `GET` / `PUT /v1/lending/settings`, §6.6). Three controls in the owner's order: *Share my plugins with my other devices*, checked and disabled, its tooltip saying that the account always shares them with the person's own devices (the website fixes `ownDevices` to true); *Share for free with* — a box (`gpu.share.free.box`, *Username or group*), *Add* (`gpu.share.free.add`) and a row of chips, one per id of the account's `freeWith`, each with its × (`gpu.share.free.<id>.remove`, the chip's one control); *Share with the public* (`gpu.share.public`), unchecked as the account answers it. Under them one line says to whom and in what order: *Your devices and the names above use your published offers free; with the public on, anyone else can buy them at their price.* **What is shown is the account's answer, never the change being sent**: a change — the public box, an id added or removed — is one `PUT` with the revision last read, every control disabled meanwhile with *Wait for the step under way to finish.*, and the answer drawn after it. *Add* trims and lower-cases the text and holds it to the username rule (`ShortName.validateUsername`, §5.6; the refusal logged `share.audience.refused` with the name and the rule's answer) — a name that fails reads *A name is 3 to 32 lowercase letters, digits and dashes.* under the box, `official` its reserved sentence and a name led by a dash the username rule's, nothing sent —, then resolves it through the public name lookup (`AccountProjects.name`, sent with no token): a record adds its id; null reads *No user or group is called {name}.*; an id already listed changes nothing on the account. A chip reads the username this device resolved — kept in the names store (`PluginSettingsControl.freeWithNames`, §6.11) and in the block's own memory for the session, the store written back trimmed to the answer's `freeWith` whenever an answer leaves it different —; a group the person's account holds reads its name from the *Groups* card's last read (`ShareGroups.onNames`, logged `share.audience.groupNames` with the names and the chips they name); an id neither resolved here nor held, added on another device, reads *Added on another device* and is removed the same way. The block reads in its `initState`, each time the tab opens and never at the app's start, and follows the account's state (`AccountSnapshots.current.snapshot.authentication`, its `installations` and `changes`): `authenticated` reads; `loading` shows a wheel and sends nothing; signed out, or the inert source of a build with no account and of the web, shows *Sign in to {domain} in Settings to choose who may use your plugins.* and sends nothing. A read refused, or nothing answering, hides the controls — never shown unchecked — and shows *Your sharing settings could not be read or saved just now ({code}).* with *Retry* (`gpu.share.retry`); `device-not-admitted` is such a refusal, the device not registered yet. A write refused keeps the last answer and shows the same line; on `terms-conflict` — another device changed the setting — the block reads again first and shows the fresh answer beside the line, the change not replayed; a re-read that fails replaces the line's code with its own. Under the block, signed in, **the *Groups* card** (`share_groups.dart`, `ShareGroups`, over the `CommunityGroups` seam, §6.6), shown and read whatever the sharing setting's read answered — a refused read or a device not admitted yet hides only the audience's controls, never the groups: read after its first frame — the card is made again each time the tab is shown, so this is the tab's `shown` — and again after every gesture and when the app is resumed while it is on screen (`AppLifecycleListener`, cause `resumed`; a resume while a read is under way is logged `skipped`), never on a timer, a change elsewhere while the person looks at the card shown at the next of these, every list through all its pages, with the account's usernames (`AccountProjects.identities`); *New group* (`gpu.groups.new`) and, while any wait, *Invitations to me*, each with *Accept* (`gpu.groups.invitation.<id>.accept`) and *Decline* (`….decline`); then each of the person's memberships — one row per identity, a group two of their usernames belong to having two — as *{name} · {role}, as {username}* with *Share for free* (`gpu.groups.<group>.<identity>.share`, which adds the group's id to *Share for free with* with one write and its name to the chips; disabled, saying *Already shared for free with this group* once listed, the busy sentence while the audience writes, and *Your sharing settings have not been read, so a group cannot be added to them yet* without its answer), *Invite* (`gpu.groups.<group>.<identity>.invite`) for an owner or an admin, and *Leave* (`gpu.groups.<group>.<identity>.leave`) unless that identity is its only owner; under a membership that manages, its members with their roles and its pending invitations, *{name} — invited*. With no group, *You belong to no group yet: New group makes one, and an invitation accepted joins one.* *New group* and *Invite* ask a username in a dialog (`gpu.groups.name.box`, `gpu.groups.name.ok`): the username rule (§5.6) refuses under the box with nothing sent, and the website's refusal is said in the dialog by its code. A refusal of a read or of a gesture on the card shows the block's failure sentence in the card with *Retry* (`gpu.groups.retry`), which reads again. Every gesture logs `share.groups.<load|accept|decline|create|invite|leave|reload>` at its start and its end — `done` (with the counts read, after a gesture that reads), `refused` with the code, the status and its `ms`, or, in a dialog, `refused` with the username rule's problem and its `ms` —, the load's start and `done` carrying its `cause` (`shown` or `resumed`) and the `done` naming the memberships read; a group shared logs `share.audience.group {group, name}`. Below the block, **the offer tree** (`share_tree.dart`, `ShareTree`; `sharePlaces` builds its rows), shown signed in or not: this computer's ComfyUI where a platform plug-in reaches one (its state as the *Servers* row says it, ready only where its plug-in is on, no phase is under way, no problem stands and its fact is ready — `comfyuiReady`, which the *Servers* row reads too); one row per provider and GPU offer of a starting or running server, ready where one runs, greyed with its phase while it starts; and one greyed row, *Stopped*, per server a saved offer names with no such session — a saved offer is never hidden. Under each place, the enabled, runnable plug-ins whose `render` runs on its engine (`comfyui` here, the session's `engine` on a server) and that share a `render` of an `image` — a plug-in that renders on a cloud key is not offered — and any plug-in with offers saved there, else *No plug-in can be shared here.* Under each plug-in, its offers: *Enable* (`gpu.share.offer.<id>.enabled`), the name, *{price} per image*, and *Published* only where the website acknowledged it, else *Not published*, and on a ready server place *No running server holds its stack* where none of its running servers holds the offer's; on a ready place where renders run, *Edit* (`gpu.share.offer.<id>.edit`) and *Add an offer* (`gpu.share.<tag>@<place>.add`, the place `local` or `<provider>:<offer>`). The editor (`ShareOfferEditor`, *Shared offer*) asks the plug-in's options on the route the lending loop would use (`lendingRoute`): *Way of running it* (`gpu.share.editor.stack`), a way not ready listed and disabled; a pull-down for each choice among more than one value of the chosen way, its own over the route's (`gpu.share.editor.param.<name>`), every other option left to its default and not kept; *Name* (`gpu.share.editor.name`), made from the way's label and the chosen values until the person types one; *Cents per image* (`gpu.share.editor.price`), a whole number up to nine digits, refused inline otherwise; *Enable* (`gpu.share.editor.enabled`); *Save* (`gpu.share.editor.save`), which writes the offer under its id — a new one a fresh UUID — and says a refused write inside the dialog, and *Cancel* (`gpu.share.editor.cancel`). A busy answer is asked once more when the plug-in says. There is no *Remove*: a disabled offer stays listed, withdrawn by the loop. The tab is the list's one writer, its writes one after another. Under the places, **Published, no longer on this computer** (`_Stale`): read once when the tab is shown through `Lending.supplied` (§6.6, every version of the account's offers), the account's published offers whose plug-in id is not among the plug-ins installed here, each as *{name} · {plugin}* with *Withdraw* (`gpu.share.stale.<offer>.withdraw`), which sends the publication with `published: false` at the version read and drops the row on its answer; a refusal keeps the row and says *Your published offers could not be read or withdrawn just now ({code}).*, disabled meanwhile with the busy sentence. Nothing is shown where none is stale, nor signed out (no lending client installed, or `no-token`); the website's own `lending-unavailable` is said. A sign-out while the tab is shown clears the list (`accountSnapshotProvider`), logged `share.stale.cleared` with the offers it held. Logged `share.stale.read` (start with the installed tags; `done` with the stale offers' ids, versions and plug-ins, the published ones installed here, the number of versions read and `ms`; `refused` with the code) and `share.stale.withdraw` (start, `done` or `refused` with the offer, version, plug-in, code and `ms`). An offer published from another device for a plug-in this computer has is not told apart. **Using remote plugins**, the third tab (`remote`), lists the offers this person may use, read-only (`gpuUsableOffersProvider`): the last read of GPU's two lines, judged as they count it against the plug-ins of the moment, one row per offer — its name (`configuration.name`, else its model), its lender's username (*No username* where the lender has claimed none) and its place: *Your device* for the account's own, *Free*, or each rate as declared, an image's *… per image* and another unit's *… per {unit}* —, the list logged `gpu.remote.listed` with its count and ids each time it is made again, at each answer of the two lines. With none it is one empty state: *Plug-ins your other devices and other people share with you are offered among the choices when you render.* — the render dialog lists them as routes (§6.11). **Turning an offer to published asks for the device's name first** — its checkbox, or *Enable* in the editor of an offer not enabled before — through the root's `shareClaimDeviceName`, the same dialog as *Remote control*'s (§6.6b), logged `share.offer.named {named}`; a cancel or a refusal leaves the offer unpublished. Offers the supplier publishes at start from saved settings are not asked about: a device may lend without a name, and has no connector address then.

### 9.8 Prose editing

The shared form name, aliases and fitting prose path boxes, a project's logline, synopsis and rating, a character's summary, a script block's dialogue, direction and action, and every prompt's boxes, a block's frame prompts included, draw `ProseEditor`; so do the boxes a row or a dialog owns in its own state — a relationship's relation, every garment box, an epoch's label and the new epoch's and a kept voice's name — through `LocalProse` and `localProseBox`, with the microphone after them and their committed source sampled as a form field's is. The synopsis carries the microphone in the editor's suffix and no assistant control of its own: the dock is the assistant's one place (§9.3). A treatment's five primary leaves are prose inside their `EditableComboBox`, through its `fieldBuilder`: the microphone and then the arrow after the words, a suggestion filling the box as typing does, and Down going into the editor's chooser while one is open — a typed mention's or a chip's replacement — else opening the suggestions (`fieldTakesDown`, fed by the editor's `onChooserOpen`). Beside a consumer's identifier and constraints, `ProseEditor` takes `autofocus`, `onSubmitted` (reporting canonical source) and `onChooserOpen` (whether one of its choosers is open, as it opens and closes). Plug-in text (the entity's `plugins.` keys and a plug-in's own page) and the version label stay plain until the plug-ins can declare which strings are prose. Only drawing that editor upgrades its seeded ordinary controller; subsequent form lookups retain the upgraded controller. Programmatic seeds, agent assignments, external patches and copies use canonical source without reporting typing. Agent setters publish the resulting seeded canonical source through existing validation and draft setters, including removal of foreign shown placeholders. The form's input mode controls read-only state and cancels pending edits before a mode change is rendered. Literal paths (`default_outfit`, `voice.lang`, `areas`, `geography.coordinates`, `visual_style.palette` and `plugins.`), numbers, the door's suggestion boxes and token controls stay plain. A path of another shape retains its locked presentation and original value.

The project's mention source offers character, location and prop groups and only declared epochs, using their canonical tags. It offers no entity in the viewer's recycle bin — deleted at every epoch it has (`isGroupDeleted`, §5.4) —, and neither do a scene's **Location** pull-down and its *Add character* list (`sceneLocationChoices`, `sceneCharacterChoices`), while a location the scene already names that is deleted is shown as written and not called unknown; nor does a location's *Located within* (`withinChoices`), where a deleted location the file already names is said as one in the recycle bin (`withinDeleted`), never as a tag no location has. Names use the contextual version selected by the project's existing resolution rules; a picture comes only from that version's declared preview through the existing media and thumbnail helpers. An unresolved version keeps its chooser membership with the kind icon. Viewer changes and project re-reads refresh candidates without changing source, controller identity or a captured range: a form holds one candidate source, updated in place; when it takes another film or viewer the form compares each candidate's target, name and picture with the list its boxes show, and its revision changes only when the list does (`form.candidates.changed` or `form.candidates.unchanged {stem, revision, viewer, candidates, differences, reason, boxes, ms}`, each difference a target added, removed or changed with its old and new name and picture); a scene's script, sharing it, re-projects every box it has made at that revision and at no other build (`script.candidates.refreshed {stem, was, revision, boxes, ms}`). A script placeholder carries only committed field snapshots when its first words or Add block make a block; a provisional span never allocates a block or enters that change.

`filmopen_text_ui` separates the field's shown text from its saved source. `ProseController.value.text` is the shown text, where each collapsed mention is one U+FFFC code unit. `source` retains the original reference spelling and all other text. The controller uses `ReferenceCodec` to recognise references, and one injected `MentionCandidates` source supplies both the chooser candidates and the answer to whether a target is known. Candidate pictures are `MediaLocation` values, independent of project state. Each rendered prose consumer registers its committed canonical source for recorder sampling, replacing its registration when its identifier or controller changes and releasing only its own registration on removal. Native and agent display semantics retain shown text. The live-dictation value registry retains a field's last safe recorder sample, or its canonical committed baseline before the first sample, excluding any captured provisional span. Provisional semantics cannot replace that sample; live field identifiers and caption identifiers are refused as tape values. Caption-derived labels and tooltips from pointer or wheel hits are also omitted, including hits named only as within the caption. A hit within a live field or its dotted derived controls omits source-bearing labels and tooltips when captured, so delayed gesture publication stays private after cancellation; the tape also refuses live derived values and metadata while the guard remains. Their gesture coordinates and deltas remain, as do ordinary control labels. A guard is released only after the committed or restored value reaches semantics.

The controller owns the stable `EditableTextTarget` adapter exposed as `controller.target`. Its selection and captured selections are UTF-16 offsets in the shown text. The controller maps those boundaries, the caret and the composing region into source coordinates before comparing them with reference tokens or inserting text. Each chip has two editable boundaries and no editable interior. Captures retain their original source range when the caret moves. Partial updates replace the same opaque provisional span; a final commit or cancellation happens once. External edits, a switch of source and a read-only transition invalidate outstanding captures. Cancellation restores the original source and its collapsed or deferred mention projection. Rollback preserves selection direction and keeps a collapsed caret collapsed; a caret inside the replaced span maps to its restored end boundary. Disposal locks the target and invalidates captures without publishing another value. The consumer sets the controller's `readOnly` property as well as the field's read-only state. `ControllerDictationTarget` adapts a plain controller without writing provisional words into its text. It captures the range, advances its generation on external text changes rather than selection movement, and commits through the box's formatters, length limit and callback. Disposing the adapter invalidates its captures and detaches its listener.

The optional pure `acceptsEdit(source, shown)` check validates complete proposed source and shown values before an edit changes state or notifies listeners. It can combine a form's source validation with its shown-text constraints. A refused partial retains the last accepted span; a refused final restores its original selected source because the final handle is already sealed. A throwing final validator releases its owned provisional span and restores the original before rethrowing; a throwing partial leaves the accepted span usable. Loading existing source bypasses edit validation so an invalid saved value is not silently truncated. Rich source drops unbacked U+FFFC on construction and source switch, preserving every other byte. Literal fields retain reference and bracket syntax as plain text, including U+FFFC, and apply no reference projection. Their existing single-line, shown-text length and source-validation rules still govern edits; loaded source remains untouched.

Every incoming value passes through the controller's `value` setter. The controller records the source and offset map for each emitted shown text, keyed by text alone, retaining the most recent 256 records. Re-emitting a text makes its record newest; the oldest record is evicted when the bound is exceeded. A matching incoming text reuses its record only when every placeholder that survives outside the edited range has the same chip source spelling in that record as it does in the current value. The incoming selection and composing region are retained. Otherwise the unmatched path keeps current prefix and suffix chips and removes foreign placeholders in the inserted rich text. A restoration beyond the retained records uses that same path and cannot reconstruct a removed chip whose record was evicted. These records support incoming shown-text values and foreign-placeholder handling; rich-field Undo restores independent canonical source snapshots.

Rich-field Undo and Redo restore complete canonical source transactions, including a replacement whose shown chip placeholder stays the same. Consecutive insertions and deletions at a moving caret undo as a typing burst within a fixed 500 ms window from its first edit, bounded to the latest 256 transactions. Replacements, the chooser, paste, cut and dictation’s final are steps of their own. A caret jump, a field command or an adjustment of a reference separator ends the burst. Selection-only changes and captured partials add none; a final captured insertion adds one original-to-final transaction and cancellation adds none. A new accepted source change clears Redo, while a source reset clears both stacks. Replay is checked against current field validation before either stack moves; refusal leaves source, selection and history unchanged. Keyboard Undo and Redo, the optional `UndoHistoryController` and the focused iOS native Undo client dispatch through the same source history. An outgoing editor removes only its own listeners and releases native ownership only if it still owns that client. Literal fields keep the SDK's ordinary history and batching. A foreign placeholder input that exactly matches a retained shown text restores that record when surviving chips agree, including a paste into an empty field; other foreign placeholders are stripped. Shown-text records remain bounded to the latest 256 texts, and re-emission makes a record newest. This lookup does not decide rich source Undo.

Unknown shorthand references remain plain text. Canonical unknown references collapse into unresolved mentions. Known shorthand references retain their exact spelling through unrelated edits; their own replacement writes a canonical reference. A reference being typed remains text while its source range contains the mapped caret or overlaps the mapped composing region, and collapses when the caret leaves it or a following character ends the token. Already collapsed chips remain atomic as the caret moves across their boundaries while their complete source bounds and spelling remain unchanged. Every source edit preserves each surviving collapsed chip’s target and exact codec range. The source-proposal door inserts one space before new characters that would continue its id, including letters, digits, hyphens and a dot followed by those characters. A terminal dot already in the prose stays before the inserted space. A deletion whose only effect would join a chip to following id characters keeps one space. Collapsed Backspace on that space crosses left; Delete before it crosses right, without removing text. Selected separator replacement keeps the space and ends after it. Deletion that also removes the following characters or the chip proceeds. Selection and composing offsets follow the inserted separator. Each captured partial or final rebuilds from its original source snapshot and captured bounds, then records separator insertions for rollback mapping. Revisions never consume original text shifted by an earlier separator. Cancellation restores the exact loaded snapshot and maps selection through the added separators and replaced span, without committing normalization. The rule applies before projection and validation to typing, paste, cut, selection replacement, composition, captured insertion and chooser actions. A field formatter cannot remove a required separator. Undo and redo restore valid earlier source states. Plain composing text keeps its underline when another mention is drawn as a chip.

`ProseEditor` takes the consumer's field identifier, decoration, focus node, text style and line counts. It preserves the consumer's tap-region group and focus-request rule. A consumer that already wraps its field identifier disables the editor's own semantics wrapper. Its editable suffix follows any existing decoration suffix, supplies one microphone and disappears in read-only mode. It reports canonical source changes, including a chip's source-only replacement. While the controller has a provisional span, the editor neither reports a change nor advances its last committed source. A final commit reports the changed source once, including when it equals the last partial; cancellation reports no change. A source-epoch reset synchronizes the editor without reporting a user edit. It resets the actual field undo subtree when the controller or `sourceEpoch` changes. A same-target source reset retains the suffix control's state, including a binding refusal, while replacing that undo subtree. Read-only mode or editor removal disposes the suffix normally. A replacement editor retains ownership of its chip renderer and inherits only the original consumer validation rule; an outgoing editor neither clears the new renderer nor leaves its obsolete field constraints attached. The field's length limit counts shown-text graphemes, with a chip counting once; the consumer's source acceptance check counts saved source as its validation requires. The editor preserves the field's single-line filter and other input formatters. Typing and paste prepare the source and its shown projection, run the field formatters and length limit on that shown proposal, then publish only the final accepted source and map; a programmatic edit or chooser action that they would transform is refused before publication. Loading source is never silently truncated by these edit constraints. If a formatter changes text on both sides of a chip, the prepared map preserves the chip's exact source spelling. Unchanged prefix and suffix chips retain their positions; placeholders in the transformed middle reuse the remaining prepared chips in order, with surplus placeholders removed. The latest retained record takes precedence only when its surviving chips agree, and the editor does not infer which identical chip an ambiguous formatter deletion intended.

A query starts at `@` only at the start of text or after a character that is no letter, combining mark or digit of any script and no underscore — the whole character, a supplementary one read from its surrogate pair — so an at sign inside an e-mail address, a handle or a word such as `café@b` starts none. The menu opens only when candidates match the query or a chip replacement is open, anchored to the field and constrained to its width. Its controls share the consumer’s editing tap group, so using a field’s own chooser keeps an open script block and returns focus to that field; a genuine outside click retains the block’s normal departure rule. Its groups are Media, Characters, Locations, Epochs and Props, Media first in their source's order and the others with folded alphabetical display names and stable-id ties within each group. A repeated target uses its first candidate, matching membership lookup before filtering and keeping control identifiers unique. A case-insensitive substring of the display name filters the entries, with no kind-prefix filter. The query extends over letters, including uppercase letters, digits and hyphens, and dots between nonempty segments. Uppercase letters are folded for matching. Whitespace, `_` and any other character end the query; `@ch_` closes the chooser so a canonical reference can be typed unaided. Query detection uses its own grammar and maps the shown caret into source coordinates. Down or Alt+Down enters the first eligible menu control; Tab also enters an open chooser. Menu navigation leaves the field selection unchanged, Enter applies the choice and Escape closes the menu and returns to typing.

**A prompt's `@` offers media** (`PromptMentionCandidates`, `filmopen_prompt_ui/prompt_mentions.dart`): a prompt's boxes — on the forms, and the render dialog's prompt at its card, which reads the media again from the page's draft as it changes, so that its chooser, the writer's tokens, the kind and the request name the same pictures (`render.prompt.media {from, to, media, reason: draftChanged}` (`media` each token and the picture it names)) — take a source of their own, the media `promptTokenCandidates` gives for the prompt's writer kind (§5), where every other prose box offers the film's entities. On an entity's own prompts the entries are named *inspiration 1*, *reference 1*; in a block's frame and clip prompts *Kira Voss · reference 1* and *Kira Voss · sheet 1*, each with its thumbnail, or the picture itself where it is a local image, else the stock icon. The voice's and the treatment's boxes offer none, so their `@` opens nothing; nor does any negative box — an entity's, a block's frame's, the render dialog's —, a plain box taking its words literally, since media tokens are the positive prompt's only (Project Specification §9.0) and a render resolves the positive alone. **Where a query matches nothing**, a prompt that names pictures — a `*-reference`, a `*-sheet`, a frame's, a clip's — keeps its chooser open on one disabled line (`MentionCandidates.emptyHint`, `<field>.chooser.empty`; not a candidate, it inserts nothing): *No picture to name* when nothing matches, while retained unreviewed pictures are offered just like kept ones; the voice's and the treatment's say nothing, and every other prose box's chooser stays shut as before. The line is asked once per opening and logged `mention.empty {kind, query, reason: none, ms}`. A form's prompt sources read the draft as the saver holds it — its snapshot, at a box's creation and at each build of the form — not the file as last read, so a picture kept a moment ago is offered before it is written. The form holds one source per prompt kind (`FormScope.promptCandidates`, logged `form.prompt.candidates {stem, kind, sources}` when made): its own boxes and a scene's frame and clip prompts in the script (`ScriptEditor.promptCandidates`) take it there, and a change of a source's list is logged `form.prompt.candidates.changed {stem, kinds, revision, ms}` and counted in the revision the script's boxes re-project on; moving to another file drops the sources no box of the form holds (`form.prompt.candidates.dropped {from, stem, kinds, kept}`). A prompt's source still knows the film's characters, locations, props and epochs, so a reference already in its words keeps its chip, but offers only media. A chosen entry writes its token (`@reference-1`, `@ch_kira:character_sheet-1`), never the name, and shows as the chip of its name and picture; a media token that names nothing — a number past the group's entries, a stem not in the film — is the unresolved chip with *Repair* and *Remove*; `promptInputsOf` (§5) reports it among the tokens that name nothing. A frame's token typed by hand stays text while the caret is anywhere in what continues its entity after a `:` (lowercase letters, digits, `_`, `:`, `-`, `.`), so `@ch_kira:character_sheet-1` typed a character at a time is saved exactly so and becomes one chip as the caret leaves; an entity typed alone, or followed by a colon and a space, collapses as before. Building a prompt's source is logged as `prompt.mentions` (its kind, `bound` or `changed`, the targets offered, their count, each entry’s reference, `unreviewed` and `favorite` flags, and the milliseconds); where its film, viewer, language or file is new — the file being the draft read again, on every change of a comparison — a list a box has read is built again and compared as the form compares the film's (`shownMentions`: each target, name and picture), an equal one kept, unlogged, and no box re-projected, and a list nothing has read yet is not built for it; a new file whose media — its own and its blocks' — are those of the last builds nothing for an entity's own prompts, which read only them. A prompt's chips look a picture up in its list by target. The film's candidates (`ProjectMentionCandidates`) are listed once per project and viewer, pictures resolved, and logged as `mention.candidates` (the prompt kind whose source holds it, where one does, the viewer, the targets, their count and the milliseconds); every chip and projection looks a target up in that list by its target; the editor reports its chooser's actions through the source's `report`, which the forms write to the log, each naming its box (`box`, the field's identifier): `prose.mention.opened` (the field kind, `prompt` or `prose`, how it opened, what was typed after `@` or the chip being replaced, the targets offered with their count, and the milliseconds the build took to list them), `prose.mention.chosen` (the reference's kind, insert or replace, what was typed after `@` or the chip replaced, the target chosen and its name, `inserted` or `refused` with the reason — `read_only`, `no_query`, `stale`, `unrepresentable`, `field_refused` —, its place in the source, the source as it left it, and the milliseconds), `prose.mention.repaired` (the kind, the token replaced and the target) and `prose.mention.removed` (the kind, the token removed, `removed` or `refused` with the reason — `read_only`, `stale`, `field_refused` —, the source as it left it and the milliseconds). The chooser lists its entries once a build, and that list is what it draws, opens on and reports; a chip is built with its place and its bold from the span's one pass, and an edit's chip protection parses the proposed text once, again only after a separator it adds.

A chosen candidate becomes an atomic `@Name` chip. A choice at the end of source includes one trailing space in its accepted edit. The very next typed period, comma, semicolon, colon, exclamation mark, question mark, closing parenthesis, closing bracket or quotation mark replaces that chooser-created space as a separate transaction when the caret has not moved. A letter, digit, space, another edit or an intervening command ends this opportunity; Undo does not revive it. A terminal dot remains plain, and continuing id characters typed after it still receive the required separator. In exact-source fields a trailing space left by stopping after the chip remains as entered. A choice before a character that would continue the id includes one separating space; existing whitespace or punctuation that already separates the token is kept. Both the menu and chip draw its resolved picture through the shared media provider, or the kind's icon when there is no usable picture. A display-name change changes no saved reference id; refreshing candidates updates the projection and name while retaining captured source ranges. Clicking a known chip opens replacement. An unresolved canonical chip displays its source and an unresolved tooltip, and offers Repair and Remove. Repair opens explicit candidates without guessing a replacement. Its chosen entry replaces the whole unresolved token range; choosing another entry on a resolved chip does the same. Each accepted action resolves to the chosen target, preserves all other prose except its required right-edge separator and is one source Undo transaction. Selection-only reprojection rebinds a rendered token by its owner, source epoch, exact range and spelling; a departed token cannot act on another source. A candidate whose canonical encoding does not round-trip to its complete target is refused without changing source, selection or history. Remove deletes the whole token. Backspace and Delete also remove the atomic token.

The field preserves deliberate caret and range selections on focus, including a return from the mention menu. A refused chooser action leaves the menu and selection unchanged. The field keeps its consumer-owned semantics identifier. Its chooser entries derive `<field>.chooser.<kind>_<id>`, its shown-order chips derive `<field>.chip.<n>`, and an unresolved chip's actions derive `<field>.chip.<n>.repair` and `<field>.chip.<n>.remove`. Each chip supplies its name or unresolved canonical source as the spoken label. Identifiers remain unique when two fields occur on the same page.

Keyboard and context-menu Copy and Cut carry canonical reference source, never the shown placeholders. Fully selected known shorthand references are encoded canonically without changing the stored spelling; partial raw-token selections retain their selected text. Cut copies only the explicitly selected source and applies paired-delimiter deletion to its complete accepted removal. It removes the selected source only after the clipboard write completes and the same controller, generation and shown value remain current. Paste similarly checks the target after its asynchronous clipboard read, applies existing field constraints and enters through the controller's ordinary value door, placing the caret after the inserted text. A departed source, replaced chip, switched controller or disposed editor cannot receive a late clipboard edit. Clipboard platform failures leave source untouched. Each copy, cut and paste is reported through the source's `report` as `prose.clipboard {box, op: copy | cut | paste, start, end, text, outcome: copied | cut | pasted | stale | empty | refused | failed, reason, error, source, ms}` — `start` and `end` the selection in the shown text, a refusal's `reason` `read_only` or `no_selection`, `error` the platform's message, `source` the field's source as the action left it, which shows a field's limit refusing or trimming it; a box made without candidates — a negative prompt's — reports nothing and says nothing of a failure. Literal-mode clipboard text remains verbatim. The consumer configures source acceptance before attaching the editor; detachment restores it only if the editor still owns the installed wrapper.

The field keeps ordinary text navigation inside and across bracket pairs. Left and Right move one visible grapheme, crossing a chip atomically and stepping before an opening delimiter or after a closing delimiter. Shift extends selection with its anchor retained. Home and End keep the platform’s ordinary field behavior. Typing either `[` or `]` wraps a nonempty selection in a pair, or opens an empty pair with the caret inside. The caret after wrapping is immediately before the new closing delimiter. Typing `]` at a closing delimiter matched to a balanced unescaped pair moves the caret past it without changing text, running text constraints, invalidating captures or adding an undo entry. Nested pairs are balanced by a stack. Deleting either delimiter of a balanced pair removes its matching delimiter in the same edit, preserving all contents except those explicitly selected for deletion. Exact surviving shown positions retain each chip's original source spelling and identity, including when a selected range deletes another chip. A bracket action over a provisional span uses its restored source and mapped selection, invalidating the old capture without publishing an intermediate rollback.

Bracket actions prepare their whole source and shown proposal before the consumer's formatter and length checks. A constraint that changes or truncates that proposal refuses the complete action; refused edits publish no source, map or history record. Rich-field Undo and Redo restore the complete accepted source transaction without reinterpreting restored brackets. Owned typed brackets and paired deletion apply their rules even when the raw proposal matches an earlier shown-text record. A fresh typed delimiter does not inherit an old unmatched spelling, and deleting a paired delimiter still removes its mate. Generic incoming values and clipboard paste retain agreeing-record precedence; rich source replay restores its exact snapshot without bracket repair. An odd run of preceding backslashes makes `\[` and `\]` literal. Clipboard paste, loaded source and active or finishing input-method composition never repair unmatched text. Balanced unescaped pairs alone determine bold styling; nested interiors include inner delimiters, while outer delimiters stay plain. Bold text and chip labels remain editable, retain the base text style and combine with composing underline. Literal fields have neither pairing nor bracket styling.
